**GH-258** (step 1 of the D07 AA-methodology consistency fix — data layer only, no consumers wired up yet) Investigation into D07 (Hoxton audit: "Annual K Requirement" flip-flopping between 0.0 and ~100 kg/ha for an AA-tested site) found AA (Ammonium Acetate / Hill Labs NZ) sufficiency-range data independently duplicated across **9** places in the codebase (`mlsnEngine()` in `hub-tissue-v3.js`, `ammonium-acetate-methodology.js`, `nutrition-prebble-integration.js`'s K-reconciliation floor, `word-export.js`'s SSOT usage, `SampleAnalysisController.php`'s `AA_RANGES` constant, `gaip-classification-constants.js`'s `AA_THRESHOLDS` and its inline fallback copy in `tissue-corrective-engine-pure.js`, plus `hill-labs-sample-types.js` itself as the intended-but-incompletely-adopted SSOT) — each with its own numbers, its own texture-bucketing rule, and in `mlsnEngine()`'s case no real AA branch at all (falls through to MLSN's `×1.5` formula, treating an AA range's floor as an MLSN-style single threshold). This entry adds the shared data layer the remaining steps will route through: `assets/hill-labs-sample-types.js` gains a new sample-type code `S279` (TURF Browntop, Sand — Hill Labs certificate 2606324, Russley Golf Club, 13-May-2021; K/Ca/Mg/Na me/100g identical to the existing `S277` entry, pH/Olsen P/TBS differ per the certificate) plus two new exported functions: `deriveCode(species, soilTexture)` (maps a canonical `SpeciesController` species + the site's general `soil_texture_override`/`accounts.soil_texture` value to a Hill Labs sample-type code — `perennialRyegrass`+sand-ish→`S277`, `browntopBent`+sand-ish→`S279`, `fineFescue`/`tallFescue`→`S81`, `cotula`→`S78` always, everything else→`null` for "no certificate on file"; "sand-ish" reuses the substring rule already live in `SampleAnalysisController.php`, `stripos($texture, 'sand')`, rather than requiring an exact match) and `getRangesPpm(code, nutrient, cec?)` (resolves a nutrient's certificate-native threshold — me/100g, %BS, or mg/L/mg/kg — and converts it to ppm via the module's existing `meq100gToPpm`/`pctBSToPpm` helpers, so callers never handle unit conversion themselves). Added `app/resources/data/hill-labs-sample-types.json`, a PHP-readable mirror of the same S277/S279/S81/S78 range data, and `app/app/Services/HillLabsSampleTypesService.php` (`deriveCode()`/`getRangesPpm()` PHP equivalents, reading that JSON) for `SampleAnalysisController.php` to adopt in a later step. No build step joins the JS literal and the JSON file — each carries a comment pointing at the other, and cross-runtime parity is planned as an explicit test, not just documentation, specifically because undocumented drift between copies is the bug class this fix targets. `hill-labs-sample-types.js` keeps loading as a plain synchronous `<script>` tag (no `fetch()`) so existing consumers (`word-export.js`'s current `getRanges()`/`getThreshold()` usage) see no behaviour change from this step. Verified via Node: `deriveCode('perennialRyegrass','sand')` → `S277`, `getRangesPpm('S277','K')` → `{min: 78.2, max: 195.5}` ppm, `meq100gToPpm(0.15,'K')` → `58.65` (matches the Hagley Oval K=0.15 me/100g → 58.7ppm worked example already cited in `word-export.js`'s `b35fix441` comment). Remaining steps (routing `mlsnEngine()`, `SampleAnalysisController.php`, the K-reconciliation floor, `word-export.js`'s sample-type default, `tissue-corrective-engine-pure.js`, and `nutrition-requirement-engine.js`'s AA ceiling through this data layer) are separately GH-numbered as they land.

**GH-259** (step 2 of the D07 AA-methodology consistency fix — texture resolution + dead UI removal, still no live consumers wired up) Retired the AA-specific rootzone-texture control in the new hub's Settings page (`site-settings-panel.js`, `#gaip-sp-aa-soil-texture`, labelled "Rootzone Type (for K/Mg ranges)") — investigation for GH-258 found this control was fully decorative: it synced against `.gaip-aa-soil-texture`, an element that only exists in the OLD hub's `legacy-hub-markup.blade.php`, so on every new-hub page the sync's `document.querySelector('.gaip-aa-soil-texture')` returned `null` and silently no-opped. Selecting a value there saved nothing and affected nothing; every new-hub AA site was classified using whichever hardcoded default each downstream consumer happened to fall back to. Removed the dropdown and its container markup (`#gaip-sp-aa-texture`), the now-orphaned `updateAATextureVisibility()` function and both its call sites, the dead two-way DOM sync (`populateMethodology()`'s read-from-`.gaip-aa-soil-texture` and `saveChanges()`'s write-back-to-`.gaip-aa-soil-texture`), and the `aaTexture` field from the panel's snapshot/restore pair (`takeSnapshot()`/`restoreSnapshot()`, used for the settings-panel Cancel button). Confirmed via `tests/aa-methodology-soil-proxy-write-b35fix393.test.js`'s existing regex pins that `ammonium-acetate-methodology.js`'s own (untouched, still-functional) `.gaip-aa-soil-texture` read/write plumbing is unaffected — that test still passes against the edited file. In place of the removed control, added a small resolver — `resolveSoilTexture(siteOverride, accountTexture)` in `hill-labs-sample-types.js`, and its PHP equivalent on `HillLabsSampleTypesService` — that every future `deriveCode()` caller (items 3-6) should use to turn `sites.soil_texture_override`/`accounts.soil_texture` into the string `deriveCode()` expects, mirroring the fallback chain already established at `SampleController.php:374` (`$site->soil_texture_override ?: $site->account->soil_texture`) instead of each consumer re-deriving it independently. Per the plan's clarified scope (the old hub is calculation-engine-only, no real user interacts with its markup), this is a direct swap with no DOM-first fallback needed. No consumer calls `deriveCode()`/`resolveSoilTexture()` yet — this step only removes dead UI and adds the resolver both runtimes will share; classification behaviour is unchanged until `mlsnEngine()` (item 3) and `SampleAnalysisController.php` (item 4) are wired up.

**GH-260** (step 3 of the D07 AA-methodology consistency fix — the actual root fix for the Soil page's original flip-flop) Gave `mlsnEngine()` (`assets/hub-tissue-v3.js`, the true single computation entry point for Soil-page nutrient classification on initial page load — see GH-258's investigation notes) a real AA branch. Previously AA had no classification branch of its own: the nutrient loop only special-cased SLAN (`if (isSLAN && ranges && ranges[nutrient])`); AA fell through to the generic MLSN PACE-style block, which computed `target = mlsnThreshold × 1.5` using the AA range's **floor** as if it were a single MLSN minimum — never using the range's actual ceiling, and never producing a HIGH-with-zero-requirement outcome no matter how far above range a nutrient sat. Added `else if (isAA && ranges && ranges[nutrient])`, styled on the SLAN branch, giving LOW/SUFFICIENT/HIGH via the range's real `lo`/`hi` bounds. Two data-source changes feed it: (1) AA texture now resolves from `state.soil.soilTexture` — the general 6-value soil-texture field already flowing into this function for the ECe conversion a few lines above, itself sourced from `sites.soil_texture_override` — instead of the retired `.gaip-aa-soil-texture` selector (GH-259), which was always decorative in the new hub and left every AA site stuck on the "others" bucket regardless of real rootzone; sand-based sites now correctly resolve to the sands bucket for the first time (a deliberate, previously-flagged behaviour change, not a new bug). (2) Where `HillLabsSampleTypes.deriveCode(species, soilTexture)` (GH-258) resolves a certificate-backed sample-type code, its `getRangesPpm()` ppm ranges overlay the existing texture-only P/K/Ca/Mg/S numbers; uncovered species/texture combinations and all micronutrients (Fe/Mn/Cu/Zn/B — not on any Hill Labs turf certificate) keep using the pre-existing hardcoded texture-only ranges as a graceful-degradation fallback (item 7 will make that fallback visible instead of silent). `rangeMin`/`rangeMax` are now carried through the existing HTML-string round trip as `data-range-min`/`data-range-max` attributes on AA rows only (absent on MLSN/SLAN rows — no leakage) and read back out by `hub-persistence.js`'s `DOMParser` scraper into `cache.computed.soilNutrition.nutrients[]`, which finally populates the `isAA && n.rangeMin != null` display code in `soil-nutrition-analysis.js`'s `renderNutrientCards()` that was previously dead (present in the code, never fed real data). Also fixed `renderAnnualRequirements()` in the same file: its ceiling-to-zero check was already structurally present (`isHigh` → `rounded = 0`) but for AA was comparing `actual` against the same broken MLSN-fallthrough `targetPpm`, not a real ceiling; now compares against `nObj.rangeMax` for AA, with matching "exceeds AA sufficiency range" wording. Verified behaviourally via a Node `vm` harness running the real (unmodified other than this fix) `hub-tissue-v3.js`: Hagley Oval-equivalent site (Ryegrass, sand, CEC 5 → resolves S277, K range 78.2–195.5 ppm per GH-258's worked example) — K=199ppm → HIGH with `data-range-min="78.2" data-range-max="195.5"`; K=58.7ppm → LOW; K=120ppm → SUFFICIENT. Uncovered-species (Kikuyu) and native-rootzone Ryegrass both correctly fall back to the texture-only "others" range (100.0–235.0) without error. Cotula (S78, texture doesn't gate it, thresholds delegated to `cotula-bowling-green.js`) falls back to the texture-only "sands" range without error, confirming the delegation isn't broken by this change. MLSN and SLAN regression-checked on the same harness — identical output to pre-fix (same `mlsn` column values, same status, no `data-range-*` attributes present) — this branch only intercepts `methodology === "ammonium_acetate"`, the MLSN fallthrough block and SLAN branch are otherwise untouched. **Known transitional gap** (documented in the original plan, not a new bug): `SampleAnalysisController.php`, reached via the Soil page's sample-switcher dropdown, is a second independent path that already had its own correct AA floor/ceiling logic but its own hardcoded range numbers — it can still disagree with this fix's output for the same site until it's routed through the same `hill-labs-sample-types.json` data (next step). Committed test coverage for items 1-3 in GH-261, superseding the manual `vm` smoke test described above (also correcting the GH-253/254-era assumption that neither Jest nor PHPUnit could run locally in this environment — both actually do; see GH-261).

**GH-261** Added committed automated test coverage for D07 items 1-3 (GH-258/259/260), replacing the ad-hoc Node/PHP verification those entries described. Discovered mid-task that the GH-253/254-era "no local test runner" assumption was stale for this environment: `node_modules/.bin/jest` runs directly (`jest.config.js`: `testEnvironment: 'node'`), and the `gilba_app` Docker container (already running, `app/` bind-mounted at `/var/www/html`) has PHP 8.3 + `vendor/bin/phpunit` — both toolchains work today, no Docker access gap to work around. One environment issue found and fixed in the process: `gilba_app`'s cached Laravel config (`bootstrap/cache/config.php`) locked `DB_CONNECTION=mysql` from `.env`, silently overriding both `phpunit.xml`'s `DB_CONNECTION=sqlite force="true"` and `tests/TestCase.php`'s own env overrides — every `Tests\TestCase`-based test (Unit and Feature alike) failed with "refusing to run against a non-SQLite database" before even reaching its own assertions, confirmed pre-existing and unrelated to this work by reproducing it on the untouched `tests/Unit/ExampleTest.php`. Fixed for the duration of this session's test runs via `php artisan config:clear` (confirmed with the user before running, since it changes state on a container that also serves `gilba_web`/nginx); restored via `config:cache` immediately after. New files: `tests/gh258-hill-labs-sample-types.test.js` (22 tests — `deriveCode()`/`getRangesPpm()`/`resolveSoilTexture()`/S279 data), `app/tests/Unit/HillLabsSampleTypesServiceTest.php` (19 tests — same assertions against the PHP side, independently written against the same certificate numbers rather than sharing fixtures with the JS test, since cross-runtime drift despite both "testing the same thing" is exactly the GH-258 bug class), `tests/gh259-aa-texture-field-removed.test.js` (9 tests — structural pins confirming the decorative dropdown/sync/snapshot-field are gone and the untouched b35fix393 proxy-write plumbing survives), `tests/gh260-mlsn-engine-aa-branch.test.js` (17 tests — structural pins on the new AA branch plus behavioural cases run against the real, unmodified `hub-tissue-v3.js` via a `vm` harness, `tests/helpers/mlsn-engine-harness.js`, covering HIGH/LOW/SUFFICIENT classification, the GH-259 sand-texture behaviour change actually taking effect, uncovered-species/native-rootzone/Cotula graceful fallback, and byte-level MLSN/SLAN regression checks), `tests/gh260-hub-persistence-range-scrape.test.js` (5 tests — extracts the real scraper block from `hub-persistence.js` via `vm` against a minimal test-only DOMParser polyfill sized to mlsnEngine's own table shape, since no jsdom is installed in this repo and adding it wasn't in scope; confirms `rangeMin`/`rangeMax` land as parsed floats for AA rows and stay `undefined` without crashing for MLSN/SLAN rows), `tests/gh260-annual-requirements-aa-ceiling.test.js` (6 tests — `renderAnnualRequirements()`'s AA-vs-MLSN ceiling branching, via a test-only export spliced onto an in-memory copy of `soil-nutrition-analysis.js`, the on-disk file itself unchanged). Full suite: 902/902 Jest tests pass (`node_modules/.bin/jest`, 59 suites, includes all pre-existing tests — no regressions); `vendor/bin/phpunit tests/Unit` 20/20 pass. `vendor/bin/phpunit` run repo-wide also surfaced a separate, pre-existing failure unrelated to D07: `tests/Feature/*` (`RefreshDatabase`-based) fail on a SQLite-incompatible migration (`2026_05_22_000000_add_unique_index_to_predictions.php`, `near "p1": syntax error`) — reproduced on `tests/Feature/SiteApiTest.php` untouched by this work, confirming it predates GH-258/259/260; flagged here, not fixed, since it's outside D07's scope and migrations are a higher-blast-radius change to make without separate confirmation.

**GH-262** Fixed a second, previously-unaudited `mlsnEngine()` call site in `hub-persistence.js` that was silently defeating GH-260's AA-branch fix for real sites. `cacheAnalysisResults()` has two independent paths into `mlsnEngine()`: the primary one (driven by the DOM soil form / `GAIP_STATE.inputs.soil`, already covered by GH-260/GH-261) and a second "empty hub form" fallback (`if (!cache.computed.soilNutrition && global.GAIP_SampleManager && ...)`) that reads the latest sample straight from `GAIP_SampleManager` when the primary path finds no soil data yet — a real, confirmed-live race during Re-run (a user's browser console log showed `[GilbaPersist] soilNutrition from sample: sample_141` firing for an AA/Ryegrass/Sand site, and the resulting Soil-page K card showed "AA: 37 ppm" — `37` being the literal `MLSN_THRESHOLDS.K` constant — with MLSN's generic recommendation text, even though GH-260 had already shipped and the card's "AA:" label was correctly reading `ammonium_acetate` from elsewhere). Root cause: this fallback builds its own, separate `_smState.soil` object with `methodology: _smRaw.methodology || 'mlsn'` — but `methodology` is a site/turf **setting**, never present on a sample's raw lab-value payload (`sample-manager.js` only ever writes the user's methodology choice to the `.gaip-soil-methodology` DOM select, never into sample data), so this silently defaulted to `'mlsn'` for every real AA site that ever hit this path, regardless of GH-260's fix to `mlsnEngine()` itself — the engine was fixed, but this caller never told it AA was selected. `soilTexture` wasn't read at all either, so even a correctly-detected AA site on this path would have missed GH-259's sand-bucket fix too. Fixed by reading `.gaip-soil-methodology`/`.gaip-soil-texture` from the DOM as a fallback — the same "race condition as primary path" pattern this exact code block already uses for its adjacent pH/EC/texture DOM reads, just never applied to methodology or texture — and by setting `cache.computed.soilNutrition.methodology` from the same resolved value actually fed to `mlsnEngine()` (previously a separate, independent read of `_smRaw.methodology`), so the card's label and its computed classification can no longer disagree the way they did in the reported symptom. Also added `CEC` to this fallback's `_smState.soil` (needed for the AA %BS-axis conversion, e.g. S81/Fescue — previously absent, would have silently no-op'd via `getRangesPpm`'s existing null-safety rather than crashing, but was still missing). Added `tests/gh262-sample-fallback-methodology.test.js` (8 tests: structural pins on the fixed pattern, plus two behavioural tests running the real extracted fallback block via `vm` against the real `mlsnEngine()` — a DOM-only methodology/texture case now correctly resolves AA instead of the MLSN literal `37`, and a fully-absent-everywhere case still safely defaults to `mlsn` without crashing). Full suite: 910/910 Jest tests pass, no regressions. This was found by testing GH-260 live in the browser (not caught by GH-260/261's own tests, since those only exercised the primary `mlsnEngine()` call site — a reminder that "the engine is fixed" doesn't mean "every caller of the engine is fixed") — credit to the manual UI check that surfaced it.

**GH-263** Fixed the actual reason the general "Soil texture" Settings field never reached `mlsnEngine()`, found by re-testing GH-262 live: a site with Settings "Soil texture" explicitly set to Sand still computed the generic non-sand ("others", 100–235ppm K) AA range instead of the certificate-backed S277 range. Root cause: `.gaip-soil-texture` — the DOM field both `mlsnEngine()`'s state collector (GH-260) and GH-262's fallback read for general soil texture — is a **static HTML default** in `legacy-hub-markup.blade.php` (`<option value="loam" selected>`), with no build-time binding and no JS sync path from the site's real `sites.soil_texture_override` ever found; every prior fix in this chain (GH-259/260/262) had assumed this field was live-synced, following the original D07 plan's Context section, which turned out to be an unverified assumption. The server already computes the correct value at sample-creation time (`SampleController.php`: `site.soil_texture_override ?: account.soil_texture`, same pattern for `methodology_override`/`methodology`) and returns both as `soil_texture_snapshot`/`methodology_snapshot` on every sample API response — but `sample-persistence.js`'s server-sample sync (the code that copies API sample data into `GAIP_SampleManager`'s client-side store) discarded both fields, keeping only the lab-value payload (`values`). Fixed across the three files in the actual chain: (1) `sample-persistence.js` now carries `methodology_snapshot`/`soil_texture_snapshot` through as `methodologySnapshot`/`soilTextureSnapshot` on the client-side sample object (additive — existing `label`/`date`/`zoneType`/`values` fields untouched). (2) `sample-manager.js`'s `loadSample()` now restores `.gaip-soil-texture`'s DOM value from `sample.soilTextureSnapshot` when present, fixing the **primary** `mlsnEngine()` path (GH-260) for any real sample load — gated on the snapshot actually being present, so samples saved before this field existed keep today's "loam" default rather than being forced to a guess. (3) `hub-persistence.js`'s "empty hub form" fallback (GH-262) now prefers `_smSample.methodologySnapshot`/`.soilTextureSnapshot` over its own DOM-read fallback from GH-262 (kept as a last-resort for pre-GH-263 samples). Added `tests/gh263-soil-texture-snapshot.test.js` (7 tests: structural pins on all three files, plus two behavioural tests on the real extracted `hub-persistence.js` fallback block confirming the snapshot texture wins over a deliberately-conflicting stale DOM value — "sands" bucket range 75.0–175.0ppm K, not "others" 100.0–235.0 or MLSN's literal `37` — and that the DOM fallback still works when the snapshot is absent). Also updated two `tests/gh262-sample-fallback-methodology.test.js` structural assertions to match the new snapshot-first priority order. Full suite: 917/917 Jest tests pass, no regressions. This closes the loop opened by GH-262: the engine (GH-260), both its callers (GH-262), and now the actual data source those callers read from (GH-263) are all fixed and tested — found only because the fix was checked live in the browser after each step, not assumed correct from code review alone.

**GH-264** Fixed GH-263 being silently inert for any sample already cached client-side before it shipped — re-testing GH-263 live on the same session's already-tested site still showed "AA: 37 ppm" (the exact GH-262 symptom) even after GH-263's code was confirmed live. Root cause: `sample-persistence.js`'s server-sample sync loop has an "already present" early-return (`if (serverSnap.allSites[siteId][sample.sample_type][sampleId]) return;`) that skips the entire object-literal build — including GH-263's new `methodologySnapshot`/`soilTextureSnapshot` fields — for any sample already sitting in the client-side store, which includes every sample touched earlier in the same testing session (client-side samples persist across page reloads via `GAIP_SampleManager`'s own store). GH-263 only ever took effect for samples that had never been synced before; every already-cached sample — which in practice means every sample any real tester or user had already looked at — kept getting resynced with the two new fields silently dropped, forever, since the early-return fires on every subsequent sync too. Fixed by backfilling the two fields onto an existing entry in place when missing (never touching `label`/`date`/`notes`/`zoneType`/`values`, which may carry local edits since the last full sync) and counting a backfill toward the `restored` counter that gates `SM.restoreFromPersistence(serverSnap)` — `GAIP_SampleManager.getAllSamples()` returns a deep clone (`JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(...))`), so mutating the snapshot without also triggering `restoreFromPersistence` would never reach the live store; this was the second time in this same investigation a fix looked complete by inspection but needed `restoreFromPersistence` counted correctly to actually persist. Added `tests/gh264-sample-snapshot-backfill.test.js` (4 tests, extracting the real sync loop via `vm`: a pre-GH-263 sample missing both fields gets them backfilled and a local edit to its `values` survives untouched; a sample that already has both fields triggers no `restoreFromPersistence` call at all — no gratuitous writes; a brand-new sample still gets the full object built via the untouched original path). Full suite: 921/921 Jest tests pass, no regressions. **Practical note for anyone re-testing this chain locally**: a browser session that tested GH-262's symptom before GH-263/264 shipped needs either a fresh site/sample never touched this session, or `GAIP_SampleManager.clearAllSamples()` / a hard local-storage clear, to see the backfill path exercised rather than the (now also fixed) already-cached path.

**GH-265** Fixed GH-263's methodology/soilTexture priority being wrong for methodology specifically — re-testing GH-264 live, the K card still showed "AA: 37 ppm". Diagnostic: the user ran `GAIP_SampleManager.getAllSamples().allSites[siteId].soil.sample_141.methodologySnapshot` / `.soilTextureSnapshot` directly in the browser console, returning `{"m":"mlsn","t":"sand"}` — `soilTextureSnapshot` was correctly "sand" (GH-263 working as intended for that field), but `methodologySnapshot` was `"mlsn"` even though the site's actual, current methodology was AA. Root cause: `methodologySnapshot` and `soilTextureSnapshot` are NOT equivalent signals. `soilTextureSnapshot` is a reliable proxy for `.gaip-soil-texture` because that DOM field is genuinely dead (GH-263's finding: static "loam" default, no sync path ever existed). `methodologySnapshot`, in contrast, is frozen at sample-creation time and goes stale the moment a site's methodology setting changes afterward — this sample was created before the site was switched to AA. Meanwhile `.gaip-soil-methodology` (the DOM field) is auto-selected **live**, on every page load, by `ammonium-acetate-methodology.js` based on the site's region (`[AmmoniumAcetate] Auto-selected for NZ region`, visible in every console log throughout this investigation) — it was already correct before GH-262 even started; the very first bug report screenshot already showed the card's "AA:" label correctly, which only ever came from this live DOM-adjacent signal. GH-263 treated both fields symmetrically and let the snapshot win for both, which regressed methodology specifically: the fallback's `_smState.soil.methodology` started preferring a stale historical value over a live-correct one. Fixed by flipping methodology's priority back to DOM-first (`_smMethodDom || _smSample.methodologySnapshot || _smRaw.methodology || 'mlsn'`) while leaving soilTexture's priority exactly as GH-263 set it (`_smSample.soilTextureSnapshot || _smTexDom`) — the two fields are asymmetric on purpose now, not by oversight. Added `tests/gh265-methodology-dom-priority.test.js` (5 structural tests pinning the corrected priority chain) and rewrote the conflicting-values behavioural test in `tests/gh263-soil-texture-snapshot.test.js` to match the real bug scenario (DOM methodology correct + stale snapshot methodology + stale DOM texture + correct snapshot texture, all four at once — proving the fields are now handled independently, not just "snapshot always wins"). Full suite: 926/926 Jest tests pass, no regressions. **Methodological note**: this was found only because the user pushed back with "how do you test it if it's not working?" and pulled real live state out of the browser console rather than accepting another code-review-only claim — the actual root cause (snapshot staleness) was invisible to every test written so far, since all of them constructed their own fixture data and never modeled "a sample created before the site's methodology changed." Future test data for this area should include at least one fixture explicitly representing a stale/mismatched snapshot, not just missing-vs-present.

**GH-266** Fixed a third independent gap in the same D07 chain — the "empty hub form" sample fallback's own `DOMParser` scraper never read `data-range-min`/`data-range-max` at all. Found while checking a live "Annual Nutrient Requirements (AA)" card that showed an internally contradictory result: status badge "High" next to a non-zero demand figure (45.6 kg/ha) and the non-ceiling note text ("Application required to meet annual demand") instead of the AA-exceeded wording — a combination `renderAnnualRequirements()`'s code cannot produce from a single consistent input (its `isHigh` check and its note-text branch both key off the same `rangeMax`/`status` pair). Diagnostic: `JSON.stringify(GAIP_DASHBOARD_DATA.computed.soilNutrition.nutrients.find(n=>n.nutrient==='K'))` in the browser console showed `status: "HIGH"` and a recommendation string ("4 ppm above AA sufficiency range...") that only `mlsnEngine()`'s AA branch (GH-260) produces — proving the row genuinely came from a HIGH classification — but no `rangeMin`/`rangeMax` keys on the object at all (not merely `null`; JSON.stringify drops `undefined` values, so their total absence was itself the clue). Root cause: `hub-persistence.js` has two structurally-identical `DOMParser`-based scrapers that both parse `mlsnEngine()`'s HTML table into a `nutrients[]` array — the primary one (`cacheAnalysisResults`'s main path, ~line 1073) was fixed for `rangeMin`/`rangeMax` in GH-260, but a second, independent copy inside the "empty hub form" fallback (the same fallback GH-262/263/264/265 already touched several times, ~line 1301) was never touched — GH-260's fix only ever looked at the primary scraper, nobody had reason to notice a second copy existed until this specific card exposed the gap `renderAnnualRequirements()`'s AA ceiling logic depends on. Fixed by adding the identical `data-range-min`/`data-range-max` read this scraper's sibling already had. Added `tests/gh266-fallback-scraper-range-fields.test.js` (3 tests: structural pin on the fix, a behavioural case reproducing the live report almost exactly — HIGH K row now carries numeric `rangeMin`/`rangeMax` through this specific scraper — and an MLSN-row no-leakage/no-crash case). Full suite: 929/929 Jest tests pass, no regressions. **Pattern across GH-262/264/265/266**: every one of these four fixes was "the same bug/gap, but in a second copy of the same code the previous fix didn't know existed" — `hub-persistence.js`'s "empty hub form" fallback duplicates nearly everything its primary sibling does (its own `mlsnEngine()` call, its own texture/methodology resolution, now confirmed its own scraper too), and each previous fix in this chain checked the primary path's tests but not this fallback's equivalent code until a live symptom forced a look. Worth a standing suspicion for any future `hub-persistence.js` fix: check whether the "empty hub form" fallback block has its own copy of whatever was just fixed, rather than assuming a fix to the primary path is complete.

**GH-267** Fixed the "Growth Potential" info tooltip on the Soil & Nutrition page header (`soil-nutrition-analysis.js`) claiming "MLSN targets are adjusted proportionally [with GP] — lower GP means lower nutrient demand" on AA sites too, where that's factually wrong: `mlsnEngine()`'s AA branch (GH-260) classifies LOW/SUFFICIENT/HIGH by comparing soil ppm directly against fixed Hill Labs NZ sufficiency ranges — growth potential plays no role in that comparison, unlike MLSN's GP-scaled `target = MLSN + annual uptake` formula the tooltip actually describes. Found by inspecting a live AA site's page header (the info icon next to "Hill Labs NZ Method 38%"). Root cause: `GAIP_GLOSSARY`'s `sn-compliance` entry is a single static object registered once at file load, and the info button that opens it (`renderPageHeader()`) hardcoded `data-info="sn-compliance"` regardless of `sn.methodology`, even though that function already computes the methodology (`m`) for the adjacent method-name label two lines above. Added a second glossary entry, `sn-compliance-aa`, with accurate AA-specific wording (GP shown for reference only, doesn't scale AA thresholds), and switched the info button to pick between the two based on `m === 'ammonium_acetate'`. Also fixed the adjacent `sn-status` tooltip's wording ("based on MLSN/SLAN thresholds" → "MLSN/SLAN/AA") while in the same block — same class of omission, much smaller fix (the underlying Acceptable/Monitor/Deficiency verdict logic already works identically across all three methodologies, only the description text was incomplete). Added `tests/gh267-growth-potential-tooltip-aa.test.js` (7 tests: structural pins on both glossary entries and the info-key switch, plus three behavioural tests that actually execute the real, unmodified `renderPageHeader()` via `vm` for AA/MLSN/SLAN sites and check which `data-info` key each one renders). Full suite: 936/936 Jest tests pass, no regressions. Unlike GH-262 through GH-266 (all of which were data/logic bugs in the classification pipeline), this one is copy accuracy only — no number, status, or recommendation was ever wrong; only this one explanatory tooltip described the wrong mechanism for AA sites.

**GH-268** Implemented D07 item 4 — routed `SampleAnalysisController.php` (the sample-switcher's server-side classification path) through the Hill Labs SSOT (GH-258), closing the dual-path disagreement GH-262 originally traced back to this controller: before this, the Soil page's initial load (`mlsnEngine()`, GH-260, certificate-aware since that fix) and switching samples via the dropdown (this controller, still on its own hardcoded texture-only `AA_RANGES` constant) could show different K classifications for the identical sample. The controller's AA branch already had correct floor/ceiling logic and already returned `rangeMin`/`rangeMax` (nothing structurally wrong there) — it only needed the same certificate-resolution step `mlsnEngine()` already had. Added `HillLabsSampleTypesService::deriveCode($species, $soilTexture)` resolution (reading `turf.species`/`turf.grassSpecies` out of the site's `gaip` `SiteConfig`) before classification; when it resolves a certificate-backed code, `getRangesPpm()` overlays the per-nutrient range onto the existing `AA_RANGES` texture-only fallback (same overlay pattern as `mlsnEngine()`'s GH-260 branch — texture-only stays as the graceful-degradation floor when the species isn't covered or the code's %BS-axis nutrients have no CEC to convert with). Also fixed a real gap found while wiring this up: the PHP `deriveCode()` (GH-258) only ever matched already-canonical species keys (`'perennialRyegrass'`) via a strict `match` — but `site_configs.config['turf']['species']` stores raw labels (`"Perennial Ryegrass"`), and the JS-side `deriveCode()` already normalises those internally via `SpeciesController.normalize()`. Added a scoped `normalizeSpecies()` private method to `HillLabsSampleTypesService` (lowercase, trim, strip a trailing parenthetical qualifier, then a small alias table covering only the 5 species `deriveCode()` recognises — not a full species table, that's `SpeciesService`/`SpeciesDefinition`, a different concern) so PHP `deriveCode()` now accepts the same raw labels the JS side does; without this, the controller would have silently never resolved a certificate for any real site, since turf species is never stored pre-canonicalised. Range-label formatting: certificate-backed ranges now format to 1 decimal place (`"78.2-195.5"`, matching `mlsnEngine()`'s `rangeStr`); the texture-only fallback keeps its original whole-number format (`"75-175"`) unchanged, since that's an existing, already-shipped display convention this fix had no reason to touch. **Left completely untouched, per the plan's explicit guardrail**: the MLSN/SLAN branch (same function, `computeNutrients()`, the `else` path below the AA branch) — regression-pinned by a test asserting byte-identical output regardless of the new `$species` argument. Added `HillLabsSampleTypesServiceTest.php` (+3 tests: raw-label resolution, parenthetical-qualifier stripping/case-insensitivity, unrecognised-label graceful null) and a new `SampleAnalysisControllerComputeNutrientsTest.php` (7 tests, via PHP reflection on the private `computeNutrients()` — deliberately bypassing Feature/HTTP-level testing since this repo's `RefreshDatabase`-based Feature tests still fail on the unrelated, pre-existing SQLite migration issue documented in GH-261, and `computeNutrients()` itself touches no database so reflection sidesteps that gap entirely): certificate overlay for a canonical species, the same overlay via a raw label, the %BS-axis (S81/Fescue) CEC-dependent conversion, that same %BS case gracefully falling back without CEC, uncovered-species and no-species fallback pins, and the MLSN-branch byte-identical regression check. Full suite: 936/936 Jest (unaffected, this item is PHP-only) + 30/30 PHPUnit Unit tests pass, no regressions.

**GH-269** Fixed GH-268's certificate resolution silently failing on a real Russley (S279 Browntop, Sand — Hill Labs certificate 2606324) test, the same day GH-268 shipped. Diagnostic: browser console showed `soilTextureSnapshot: "loam"` on all three of that site's real samples, even though the site is a genuine sand-rootzone green. Root cause: `soil_texture_snapshot` is frozen at sample-creation time (`SampleController.php`'s `store()`, `site.soil_texture_override ?: account.soil_texture`) and never re-computed afterward — these three samples were created before the site's Settings "Soil texture" was set to Sand, so their snapshots kept the pre-change value forever, silently defeating GH-268's `deriveCode()` resolution (which needs a sand-ish texture for the two sand-only codes, S277/S279) on every subsequent request, including the just-shipped fix. Same staleness class of bug as GH-265 (methodology_snapshot), found the same way (asking for real console state rather than assuming a fix worked). Unlike methodology, where the only live signal available was client-side (region auto-select) with nothing server-side to read, `SampleAnalysisController.php` already has `$sample->site` in scope — so the fix is a direct, no-extra-query read of the live `site.soil_texture_override`/`account.soil_texture` (via the existing `HillLabsSampleTypesService::resolveSoilTexture()` from GH-258/259) in place of the frozen snapshot, falling back to the snapshot only if the site/account genuinely never had a texture set. **Known remaining gap, not fixed here**: the client-side path (`mlsnEngine()`'s initial page load, GH-260/263) likely has the identical staleness problem — it also ultimately depends on `soil_texture_snapshot` (via `sample-manager.js`'s `loadSample()`, GH-263), and there's no equivalent already-in-scope live value to swap in the way `$sample->site` was available here. A live source does exist server-side (`AnalysisController::index()` already computes and injects live `turfSpecies` into `/analysis`'s `window.GAIP_HUB_CONFIG` — the same pattern could carry live `soilTexture` too) but `mlsnEngine()` runs inside `/hub`'s headless iframe (a separate `window`, triggered by the Re-run button, per `dashboard-ui.js`), not on `/analysis` itself, so the fix would need to land wherever `/hub`'s own page data gets built, not `analysis.blade.php`. Deliberately not attempted in this entry pending a decision on the cleanest way to get a live value into that iframe's context without touching `legacy-hub-markup.blade.php`'s static markup (the project rule against adding features there) — flagged for the user rather than solved unilaterally. Added `SampleAnalysisControllerTextureResolutionTest.php` (4 tests, using in-memory `Site`/`Account` model instances with a manually-attached `account` relation — no DB query needed — to pin the resolution priority: live site override wins over a stale snapshot reproducing the exact Russley scenario, live account-level fallback, snapshot-only fallback when neither live value is set, and the historical `'sands'` default when nothing is set at all). Full suite: 936/936 Jest (unaffected) + 34/34 PHPUnit Unit tests pass, no regressions.

**GH-270** Closed the client-side gap GH-269 explicitly left open — the initial page-load path (`mlsnEngine()` inside `/hub`'s headless iframe) had the identical stale-`soil_texture_snapshot` problem the server-side path (GH-269) already fixed. Confirmed with the user before proceeding, since the fix requires initialising an existing form field's value from live data inside `legacy-hub-markup.blade.php` — the project rule is against *adding features* to that file, and the user explicitly agreed this counts as an acceptable fix to existing markup, not a new feature. Root cause (mechanically identical to GH-269, different layer): `.gaip-soil-texture` — the select `mlsnEngine()`'s state collector reads directly, inside `/hub` — was a static `<option value="loam" selected>` baked into the markup with zero live data behind it (this is the original GH-263 finding); GH-263's fix routed a *sample's* `soilTextureSnapshot` into this field via `sample-manager.js`, which works but inherits the sample-snapshot staleness problem GH-269 already diagnosed. Fixed properly this time: `hub.blade.php` and `stadium.blade.php` (the two pages that `@include` `partials.legacy-hub-markup`) now compute a live `$soilTexture` in their existing `@php` blocks — `HillLabsSampleTypesService::resolveSoilTexture($activeSite?->soil_texture_override, $activeSite?->account?->soil_texture)`, the exact same call GH-269 added to `SampleAnalysisController.php` — and pass it into the partial, which now marks the matching `<option>` `@selected` instead of hardcoding `loam`; an unrecognised or absent value still falls back to `loam`, preserving today's default exactly for sites with nothing set. This is strictly *more* live than GH-269's fix: `SampleAnalysisController.php` only re-reads the live value on each `/analyse` request, whereas this loads fresh on every `/hub`/`/stadium` page load (i.e. every Re-run), so there's no snapshot-staleness window at all on this path going forward — `sample-manager.js`'s GH-263 per-sample restore is now effectively superseded for the texture field specifically (left in place, harmless: it fires after this live initial value and would only matter if a sample's snapshot ever disagreed with the site's current live texture, which per GH-269's own fix should no longer arise for well-formed data). Verified via direct Blade render smoke tests inside the container (no HTTP round trip): `view('hub')->render()` and `view('stadium')->render()` both compile and run without exceptions for an unauthenticated context (`$activeSite` null → resolves to `null` → falls back to `loam`, matching the pre-fix default), and rendering `partials.legacy-hub-markup` directly with `soilTexture: 'sand'` confirmed the Sand `<option>` gets `selected` instead. Full suite: 936/936 Jest + 34/34 PHPUnit Unit tests pass, no regressions (this item touches only Blade views, no JS/PHP unit-testable logic changed — covered by the render smoke tests instead, consistent with this repo's convention of deferring Feature/HTTP-level testing where a live DB isn't available, GH-261).

**GH-271** Fixed nutrient cards reordering (and losing entries) every time a sample was switched — live report: `["P","K","Ca","Mg","S","Fe","Mn","Zn","Cu","B"]` (10, canonical order) right after Re-run, `["K","P","Ca","Mg"]` (4, payload key order) after switching. Root cause, independent of the whole D07/AA chain: `SampleAnalysisController::run()` read `computed.soilNutrition` — the cached result of the last Re-run, with `mlsnEngine()`'s canonical P/K/Ca/Mg/S/Fe/Mn/Zn/Cu/B order and full 10-nutrient coverage (untested nutrients show a "NOT MEASURED" row rather than being omitted) — from `SiteConfig` namespace `'gaip'`. That namespace holds turf/location/methodology *settings* (species, texture — every other controller reads it the same way, e.g. `AnalysisController`'s `$gaipConfig['turf']['species']`), not computed analysis results; the real cache lives under namespace `'analysis_cache'`, written by `AnalysisCacheController::store()` and read correctly elsewhere (`AnalysisController::index()`'s `$analysisCacheRecord`). This wrong-namespace read meant `$cachedSn` was empty on every single request since this controller was written, silently forcing `computeNutrients()`'s "no cache" fallback branch every time — which itself had a second, independent bug: it built the nutrient list from `array_keys(array_intersect_key($payload, MLSN_DEFAULTS))`, preserving the sample's raw JSON payload key order (whatever order fields were entered/imported in) and dropping any nutrient the sample had no value for, instead of the stable canonical order and full coverage `mlsnEngine()` always produces. Fixed both layers: (1) added a second `SiteConfig` query for namespace `'analysis_cache'` and read `computed.soilNutrition` from that record instead (the `'gaip'`-namespace `$config` stays, still correctly used for `turf.species`/`turf.grassSpecies`); (2) the "no cache" fallback (now only reachable for a genuinely brand-new site that's never had a Re-run) builds from a hardcoded canonical-order array instead of the payload-derived one, including all 10 nutrients always — untested ones correctly fall through the existing `$actual === null` branch to a `'No data'`/`no-data` card, matching `mlsnEngine()`'s behaviour exactly rather than omitting the nutrient entirely. Added `SampleAnalysisControllerNutrientOrderTest.php` (3 tests via the same reflection approach as the other `computeNutrients()` tests: the fallback branch now produces the canonical order from a deliberately K-before-P payload — proving the fix isn't coincidental; all 10 nutrients present with a proper "no data" card for untested ones; a genuinely-populated cache is still used verbatim, unaffected by the fallback's new logic). The `run()`-level namespace fix itself isn't independently unit-testable here (needs a real `SiteConfig` DB row; this repo's `RefreshDatabase` Feature tests are still blocked by the unrelated pre-existing SQLite migration issue, GH-261) — verified by code reading alongside every sibling controller's identical `'gaip'` vs `'analysis_cache'` namespace split, not by an automated test. Full suite: 936/936 Jest (unaffected) + 37/37 PHPUnit Unit tests pass, no regressions. Predates the whole D07/GH-258 investigation — this bug has likely been live since `SampleAnalysisController` was first written, only surfaced now because this session's testing was the first time anyone compared the sample-switcher's nutrient list against the initial load's side by side.

**GH-272** Fixed GH-270 being silently undone by GH-263's own earlier fix, found from a live before/after comparison on Russley: right after Re-run, the K card showed the generic "others" AA range (100.0–235.0ppm, "NOT MEASURED" wording — `mlsnEngine()`'s path) while switching samples showed the correct S279 certificate range (78.2–195.5ppm, "No data" wording — `SampleAnalysisController.php`'s path, GH-268/269) for the exact same site and methodology — the wording difference between the two paths ("NOT MEASURED" vs "No data") was the tell for which path produced which card. Root cause: `sample-manager.js`'s `loadSample()` (added in GH-263) unconditionally restored `.gaip-soil-texture` from whichever sample's own `soilTextureSnapshot` happened to auto-load on page init, overwriting the page's correct, live-initialised value that GH-270 had just set moments earlier in the same page load — "Green 18"'s snapshot was the same stale `"loam"` GH-269 had already found on this site's samples, so `mlsnEngine()` (triggered by Re-run, which runs after page init and this auto-load) never actually saw GH-270's fix take effect at all. This was flagged as a real risk in GH-269's own "known remaining gap" note at the time ("left in place, harmless... unless a sample's snapshot ever disagreed with the site's current live texture") — it did disagree, on the very first real site tested after GH-270 shipped. Also conceptually wrong independent of the regression: soil texture is a site property, not a sample property, so restoring it per-sample on every sample switch was never the right model once a live site-level source existed — GH-269 had already established per-sample snapshots can't be trusted over it. Fixed by removing the restore entirely (not de-prioritising it — same "remove, don't just reorder" lesson as GH-265/270 for methodology and texture respectively; there's no case where the per-sample snapshot should still apply now that the site-level value is live everywhere it's read). Updated `tests/gh263-soil-texture-snapshot.test.js`'s now-obsolete structural pins (which had asserted the restore's *presence*) to assert its *absence* instead, plus a syntax-validity check. Full suite: 935/935 Jest tests pass (net −1 from the two obsolete tests replaced by two new ones), no regressions. **Fourth time in this specific investigation** (after GH-262/264/265/266) that a fix looked complete after code review and its own tests, but broke via a code path the fix's author didn't re-check — this one is notable for being the *fix itself* undoing a *different, earlier* fix, not a previously-unknown duplicate path; worth remembering that "add a new live source" and "an old snapshot-restore still runs later in the same page load" are not automatically compatible just because both individually made sense when written.

**GH-273** Fixed `hub-persistence.js`'s "empty hub form" fallback (the same block GH-262/265/266 touched) silently re-introducing the exact staleness problem GH-265 had already fixed for `methodology`, just for `soilTexture` this time. GH-263 originally made this fallback prefer `_smSample.soilTextureSnapshot` over its own `.gaip-soil-texture` DOM read, correctly, because at the time that DOM field really was a dead static `"loam"` default (no sync path existed at all). GH-270 then made `.gaip-soil-texture` genuinely live (site-derived, initialised on every `/hub`/`/stadium` page load) and GH-272 removed a separate per-sample DOM overwrite that would have undone GH-270's fix — but this fallback's own priority order was never revisited once the "DOM is dead for texture" premise stopped being true, so it kept trusting the snapshot first. Confirmed live (Russley, same site as GH-269-272): Re-run showed the generic "others" AA range (100.0–235.0ppm K) sourced from a stale `soilTextureSnapshot: "loam"` on the auto-loaded sample, while switching samples — `SampleAnalysisController.php`, fixed independently in GH-269 to prefer the live site value — correctly showed the S279 certificate range (78.2–195.5ppm) for the exact same site. Fixed by flipping the priority to match `methodology`'s existing GH-265 shape exactly: `soilTexture: _smTexDom || _smSample.soilTextureSnapshot || 'loam'` (was `_smSample.soilTextureSnapshot || _smTexDom`), and removed the baked-in `|| 'loam'` from `_smTexDom`'s own declaration so the `'loam'` default only applies once, at the end of the full chain — the same shape `_smMethodDom` already had. Added `tests/gh273-texture-dom-priority.test.js` (6 tests, mirrors `gh265-methodology-dom-priority.test.js`'s structure) and updated `gh262`/`gh263`/`gh265`'s own assertions on this line to match the new priority. Full suite: 940/940 Jest tests pass, no regressions. **Confirmed via unit tests and `node --check`, but the user reported the live symptom was unchanged after this shipped** ("no I mean - different numbers after rerun and switch") — see GH-274 for the actual remaining cause this fix didn't reach.

**GH-274** Root cause of GH-273 not taking effect live, found by re-grepping every file touching `.gaip-soil-texture` after the user reported Re-run and sample-switch still disagreeing post-GH-273: a **third**, independent write path to the same field, distinct from both the dedicated `soilTextureSnapshot` restore GH-272 removed and the fallback priority GH-273 fixed. `sample-manager.js`'s `loadSample()` has a generic per-field population loop (fires on *every* soil sample load, including the automatic load-on-page-init that runs before the user ever clicks Re-run, not just an explicit dropdown switch) driven by `SOIL_FIELD_MAP`, a field-name-to-CSS-selector table. That table included `'Texture': '.gaip-soil-texture', 'texture': '.gaip-soil-texture', 'Soil_Texture': '.gaip-soil-texture'` among its ~40 entries — so any soil sample whose raw payload happened to carry a `Texture`/`texture`/`Soil_Texture` key had that raw value written straight into `.gaip-soil-texture` during auto-load, silently overwriting GH-270's correct, live, site-derived initial value *before* GH-273's now-correctly-prioritised DOM read ever ran. This explains the exact asymmetry the user reported: Re-run (`hub-persistence.js`'s fallback, which reads this now-overwritten DOM value) kept showing the generic "others" range, while switching samples (`SampleAnalysisController.php`, which resolves texture server-side from the site record and never touches this DOM field at all) correctly showed the S279 range — the two paths were never actually reading the same texture value to begin with, no matter how correct either path's own priority logic was in isolation. Same conceptual bug as GH-272, same fix shape: soil texture is a site property, not a sample property, so no per-sample field-population mechanism should ever write to `.gaip-soil-texture` — removed the three keys from `SOIL_FIELD_MAP` entirely rather than re-pointing or reordering them, so nothing under this loop can touch the field again. Added `tests/gh274-soil-field-map-texture-removed.test.js` (5 tests: the three keys are gone, no key in the map points at `.gaip-soil-texture` at all, adjacent pH/EC/CEC mappings untouched, syntax validity). Full suite: 945/945 Jest tests pass, no regressions. **Sixth time in this investigation** (after GH-262/264/265/266/272) that a fix which was individually correct and fully unit-tested didn't resolve the live symptom because a previously-unknown, independent duplicate mechanism was still in play — the pattern here is specifically that a *generic, broadly-scoped loop* (built for legitimate per-sample nutrient fields) had one entry that didn't belong, which is easy to miss because nothing about the loop itself looks wrong; worth checking any future "which fields does this generic mapping table touch" question against "is this actually a per-sample property, or a site/settings property that a sample's raw payload merely happens to also carry a column for."

**GH-275** The actual remaining root cause after the user reported GH-274 *also* didn't fix the live symptom ("didnt help" — a fresh test still showed K's generic "others" AA range on Re-run). Rather than keep guessing at a sixth hypothesis, patched `HTMLSelectElement.prototype`'s `value` setter (temporary debug instrumentation in `hub.blade.php`, removed once confirmed) to log a stack trace on every write to `.gaip-soil-texture`, then re-tested. The captured trace named the exact culprit: `resetSelect` ← `clearSoilData` ← a `gaip:site-changed` listener in `gaip-clear-data.js` ← `setActiveSite` (`sample-manager.js`) ← the server-sample-sync flow in `sample-persistence.js` — a **fifth** independent write path, unrelated to any of GH-263/272/274's sample-load mechanisms. `gaip-clear-data.js` listens for `gaip:site-changed` and calls `clearSoilData(btn, domOnly=true)` specifically to wipe stale numeric sample fields (pH/EC/CEC/nutrient grid) left over from the *previous* site before the new one's analysis runs — a legitimate, necessary safeguard, since those really are stale per-sample lab values that would otherwise silently feed a phantom result for the new site (per that function's own header comment). But the same call also ran `resetSelect(SELECTORS.soilTexture, 'loam')` completely unconditionally, on every single site switch — unlike the `SampleManager`-store clear a few lines below it in the same function, which was already correctly gated behind `!domOnly` for exactly this reason. Since `gaip:site-changed` fires on every page load as part of the normal server-sync flow (not just an explicit user-driven switch), this meant GH-270's live, site-derived initial value got silently overwritten with the hardcoded "loam" default moments after the page rendered it correctly — before `mlsnEngine()` or `hub-persistence.js`'s fallback ever got a chance to read the real value. This fully explains why GH-272, GH-273, and GH-274 — each individually correct and fully unit-tested — never fixed the live symptom: none of the three touched this mechanism, and debug logs confirmed the server (`hub.blade.php`) was correctly resolving and rendering `soil_texture_override: sand` on every load, only for this listener to immediately clobber it back to "loam" client-side. Same conceptual bug as GH-272/274, same fix shape: soil texture is a site property, not per-sample data, so a "clear stale sample data on site switch" routine has no business touching it — gated the reset behind `!domOnly`, mirroring the `SampleManager`-clear immediately below it. The reset still fires for an explicit user-clicked Clear button (`domOnly=false`) — an intentional, unrelated "start this form over" action, out of scope here. Added `tests/gh275-clear-data-texture-site-switch.test.js` (6 tests: unconditional reset gone, new `!domOnly` gate present, adjacent `samplingDepth` reset untouched, the pre-existing `SampleManager`-clear gate unaffected, the `gaip:site-changed` listener still calls with `domOnly=true`, syntax validity). Full suite: 945/945 Jest tests pass (net +6, `gh274`'s own suite unaffected), no regressions. **Confirmed live**: after this fix, Russley's K/P/Ca/Mg all agree between Re-run and sample-switch, and match the S279 certificate exactly — P 5.0–15.0ppm (actual 20.0 → HIGH), K 78.2–195.5ppm (actual 156.4 → SUFFICIENT), Ca 400.0–800.0ppm (actual 220.0 → LOW), Mg 36.6–85.4ppm (actual 40.1 → SUFFICIENT) — the first time this session all four nutrients, not just K, were checked cross-path on a real site. Removed the temporary `HTMLSelectElement.prototype` setter patch and the `[GH274-DEBUG]` console logs from `hub.blade.php`/`hub-tissue-v3.js`/`hub-persistence.js` now that the fix is confirmed. **Seventh time in this investigation** (after GH-262/264/265/266/272/274) that an individually-correct, unit-tested fix didn't resolve the live symptom due to yet another independent write path — this one only found by moving from static code reading to runtime instrumentation (a setter breakpoint via stack trace) after three rounds of plausible-but-wrong hypotheses (site-context staleness, species resolution, `SOIL_FIELD_MAP`) failed to explain the live evidence; worth remembering that once a field has this many independent writers, the fastest way to find the *next* one is usually to instrument the field itself rather than keep tracing call graphs by hand.

**GH-276** User asked for a full manual re-derivation of GH-275's live-confirmed numbers (P/K/Ca/Mg for Russley, S279) against the actual SSOT source — not just accepting the earlier "looks right" summary — which surfaced a genuine, if narrow, remaining disagreement between the two AA classification paths: `mlsnEngine()`'s AA branch (`hub-tissue-v3.js:2799`) classifies SUFFICIENT via `actualPPM <= range.hi` (upper bound inclusive), while `SampleAnalysisController::computeNutrients()`'s AA branch (`SampleAnalysisController.php:211`) used `$actual < $medCeil` (upper bound exclusive) — so a value landing *exactly* on a certificate's upper bound (e.g. K = 195.5ppm exactly, S277/S279's ceiling) classified as SUFFICIENT via Re-run but HIGH via sample-switch. None of Russley's actual live values happened to sit exactly on a boundary, so GH-275's live verification didn't catch this — it only surfaced from re-deriving the classification logic line-by-line from both files' source rather than re-testing the same non-boundary values again. Lower-bound comparisons already matched (both `< lowCeil` → Low) and are untouched. Fixed by changing the PHP side's upper-bound comparison to `<=`, matching the JS side exactly. Added `app/tests/Unit/SampleAnalysisControllerBoundaryTest.php` (4 tests: exactly-on-upper-bound → Sufficient, just-above-upper-bound → still High, exactly-on-lower-bound → Sufficient unchanged, just-below-lower-bound → still Low — using S277's real 78.2–195.5ppm K range as the concrete boundary values). Full PHPUnit Unit suite: 41/41 pass (99 assertions), no regressions. Same class of bug as the whole GH-268-275 chain — two independent implementations of what should be one comparison — but caught this time by deliberately re-deriving the math from source instead of re-running the same live scenario, which is a narrower verification method than it looks: re-testing identical inputs can never catch a boundary-condition bug those inputs don't happen to hit.

**GH-277** Fixed `renderAnnualRequirements()` (`soil-nutrition-analysis.js:858-925`, the Soil page's own "Annual Nutrient Requirements" panel — not the separate Nutrition Program engine) showing a confident "Application required to meet annual demand" note next to a nutrient that was never measured. Root cause: `demandVal` (the displayed kg/ha/yr figure) comes from `calcAnnualDemand()`, a pure function of turf type + growth potential + N program, independent of whether the nutrient has a soil-test value — legitimate as a baseline removal-rate estimate on its own. The ceiling check (`isHigh`, added in GH-260, D07 item 6-adjacent) correctly requires a real `actual` value, so an untested nutrient can never be flagged HIGH — the right instinct, since you can't verify a ceiling without data. But the `else` branch of `noteText` unconditionally said "Application required to meet annual demand." whenever `isHigh` was false, which includes the untested case — so a "Not measured" status badge sat directly next to a confident soil-based recommendation, when the nutrient's actual level (and therefore whether it's already oversupplied, same as P was before the ceiling fix) is genuinely unknown. Confirmed live on Russley: S showed "Not measured" + "5.4 kg/ha/yr" + "Application required to meet annual demand" together. This `noteText` branch is shared across AA/MLSN/SLAN (not gated by `isAA`), so the bug affected all three methodologies equally, not just AA — confirmed via a dedicated MLSN regression test. Fixed by checking the already-computed `sc` (status class) for `'no-data'` and showing a distinct, honest note ("Not measured — annual removal estimate only, soil status unknown.") in that case only; measured nutrients (Low/Sufficient/Borderline) and the HIGH/ceiling messages are completely unchanged. Added `tests/gh277-annual-requirements-not-measured.test.js` (7 tests: not-measured shows the honest note not "Application required", the demand figure itself is unaffected, measured Sufficient/Low nutrients keep the old text unchanged, MLSN gets the same fix since the branch isn't AA-gated, HIGH/ceiling messaging untouched). Full suite: 957/957 Jest tests pass, no regressions. Unlike GH-274-276 (independent write/comparison paths), this one didn't need a "which value is correct" investigation — the bug was purely in what to *say* about data that doesn't exist, a UI-honesty fix rather than a calculation fix, so no client confirmation was needed before shipping (unlike the still-open Nutrient Ratios and pH-range findings from the same review pass, which changed threshold *values* and are queued for the client instead).

**GH-278** Fixed the Soil page's "Monthly N Distribution (GP-Weighted)" chart showing an entire year's N total crammed into a single month (confirmed live on Russley: 45 kg N/ha/yr, all 45 in the current month, every other month "-"). Root cause traced via live console instrumentation (three checkpoints: the DOM-container gate in `hub-tissue-v3.js`'s `gaip_render_results()`, `renderNutritionSummary()`'s entry conditions in `nutrition-summary-integration.js`, and `hub-persistence.js`'s read of the `__GAIP_MONTHLY_N__` global at save time), after static tracing alone wasn't conclusive: `renderNutritionSummary()` — the actual function that computes real per-month N via `NutritionRequirementEngine_Pure` and exposes it as `global.__GAIP_MONTHLY_N__` for `hub-persistence.js` to pick up — needs `global.climateMetrics.monthlyTemps`, resolved *asynchronously* by `climate-normals-service.js` (NASA POWER/Open-Meteo). It's invoked ~100ms after `gaip:analysis-complete` fires, almost always before that fetch resolves, so it fell into its own "Climate data unavailable" placeholder branch and returned before ever computing `monthlyNData` — confirmed live via `{hasData: true, hasMonthlyTemps: false, monthlyTemps: null}`. The real bug: `init()` never subscribed to `climate-normals-service.js`'s `gaip:monthly-normals-ready` event (only `gaip:soil-data-update`/`gaip:mlsn-calculated`/`gaip:turf-profile-change`), so nothing ever retried once the normals actually arrived — confirmed `__GAIP_MONTHLY_N__` was still `undefined` even at `_doRerunSync`'s final `cacheAnalysisResults()` call 3+ seconds later (well after GH-251's bounded wait had let `monthlyTemps` resolve — GH-251 fixed a related but distinct field, `computed.climate.growth.monthlyNormal`, not this one). With the real data source permanently stuck, `soil-nutrition-analysis.js`'s `renderMonthlyN()` fell back to its own separate (and separately flawed) approximation using `climate.growth.dailyPattern` — an ~8-9 day weather *forecast* window, not annual normals — which is why the whole year's total landed in whichever single month the short forecast window happened to sit in. Fixed by adding `document.addEventListener('gaip:monthly-normals-ready', updateNutritionSummary)` to `init()`, alongside the other reactive listeners — once climate normals resolve, the widget now re-renders past the guard and sets `__GAIP_MONTHLY_N__` correctly in time for the next persistence save. Added `tests/gh278-monthly-n-distribution-normals-race.test.js` (4 tests: structural pin on the new listener registration, `renderNutritionSummary()` shows the placeholder and leaves `__GAIP_MONTHLY_N__` unset when `monthlyTemps` is unresolved, computes real 12-month data and sets it once resolved, and `update()` — what the new listener calls — transitions an existing wrapper from placeholder to real content in place). Full suite: 964/964 Jest tests pass, no regressions. Confirmed live after the fix: chart now shows a real GP-shaped distribution across 9 active months instead of one.

**GH-279** Added an "i" info icon to the Soil page's "Monthly N Distribution (GP-Weighted)" chart, at the user's request after a walkthrough surfaced a legitimate point of confusion: the Nutrition Program page's "Monthly Nutrient Program" table (`nutrition-calendar.js`) shows a different per-month N figure for the same site and the same annual total (e.g. Russley: this chart showed Jan=24kg, Nutrition Program showed Jan=15kg, both summing to ~120 kg N/ha/yr). Traced the discrepancy to source: both read the exact same site-level "Annual N Program" setting (`state.turf.nProgramKgHaYr`, confirmed via `extractUserAnnualN()`/`extractAnnualNRate()` on this page and `this.elements.annualNInput.value` on Nutrition Program) and the same GP curve (`GilbaGrowthPotentialEngine`, PACE model) — but Nutrition Program additionally applies `NutritionCalendar.applyNCap()` (`nutrition-calendar.js:1009`), a user-configured "Max N per month" cap that flattens peak-GP months and redistributes the overflow into other active months. This chart shows the raw, uncapped GP-weighted split — same annual total, different per-month shape, by design, not a bug. Added a `sn-monthly-n-uncapped` `GAIP_GLOSSARY` entry and wired it to a `.db-info-icon` button next to the chart's title, reusing the existing info-icon/popover component already on this page (`sn-status`/`sn-compliance`) rather than building a new tooltip mechanism. Added `tests/gh279-monthly-n-uncapped-info-icon.test.js` (3 tests: glossary entry exists and mentions the cap/Nutrition Program, the icon renders inside the section title wired to that key, chart totals/values unaffected).

**GH-280** Restyled the Soil page's Monthly N Distribution chart to match the rest of the page's card-based visual language, after the user flagged it as looking like it was carried over from the old hub. It was the one block on this page still using flat, solid pastel backgrounds per season (`#fef9c3`/`#ffedd5`/`#eff6ff`/`#f0fdf4` inline fills) and a heavy black `outline` to mark the current month — visually inconsistent with every other block here (`.sn-card`, `.sn-annual-card`), which all use white bordered rounded cards. Restyled each month into a white `.sn-month-col` card (`border:1px solid #d8e0dc;border-radius:8px`) with a 3px colour-accented top border for season — reusing the accent-strip idiom this same file's `kpiCard()` already established (`border-left-color`) rather than inventing a new pattern. Inactive (0 kg) months get an `.inactive`/`.sn-month-val.inactive` class instead of an inline `opacity` style. No calculation changes — same `vals`/`total`/`active` computation, only the per-column markup and CSS changed. Full suite: 969/969 Jest tests pass, no regressions. (Current-month marking landed initially as a solid dark border here, then replaced in GH-281 below — see that entry.)

**GH-281** Follow-up to GH-280: replaced the current-month border/outline (from GH-280's first pass, itself replacing the original black `outline`) with a small "● Now" tag rendered above the bar. User feedback: a border around the whole card reads as a warning/error state, not "this is today" — presented four concrete alternatives (dot+label, tinted background, bottom accent underline, bold coloured month-label text) and the user picked the dot+label. Implemented as `.sn-month-now` (green `#16a34a` dot + "Now" text, 11px-tall row) shown only on the current month's card; every other month renders an invisible (`visibility:hidden`, not `display:none`, so column heights stay aligned) placeholder in the same slot rather than omitting the row, so all 12 bars still start from the same vertical position. Updated `tests/gh280-monthly-n-card-redesign.test.js`'s current-month test accordingly (now asserts exactly one non-empty `.sn-month-now` and eleven empty placeholders, and that no `.current`/`outline` styling remains). Full suite: 969/969 Jest tests pass, no regressions.

**GH-282** Fixed the Tissue Test Results badge label/colour mismatch flagged earlier this session (found while spot-checking the block, e.g. Creeping Bentgrass K at 2% against a 2.2-3.5% range showed the word "Deficient" in the amber/borderline badge style instead of red). Root cause: `renderTissue()` sourced the badge *text* (`band`) from `statusMap[nut].band` (the live, correct value — `tissue-engine.js`'s `classify()`, a 4-tier scheme: Deficient `<lo`, Marginal `lo`–`lo×1.10`, Sufficient, High `>hi`), but sourced the badge *colour* (`cls`) from this file's own separate `tissueBand()` — a 5-tier scheme (adds a `lo×0.9` pre-threshold and a `hi×1.1` "Elevated" tier) that disagrees with `classify()` for any value landing in the `lo×0.9`–`lo` gap. Confirmed against the old hub before fixing (per the project's algorithm-parity rule): `tissue-progressive-disclosure.js` (loaded in `hub.blade.php`, the real headless-compute path) only ever renders 4 statuses — Deficient/Marginal/Sufficient/High, no "Elevated" — and derives both label and CSS class from the *same* band value via one `getTissueStatus(band)` switch, so this class of mismatch structurally cannot happen there. `tissueBand()`'s 5-tier scheme was a new-hub-only invention with no old-hub equivalent, confirming the 4-tier `classify()` scheme is the legacy-correct one. Fixed by rewriting `tissueBand()` to use the same 4-tier thresholds as `classify()` (dropping "Elevated" and the grace bands), adding a `bandToClass(band)` helper as the single place band text maps to a badge colour, and changing `renderTissue()` so `cls` is always derived from `band` via that helper — never from a second, independent calculation — on both the live-data path and the no-statusMap fallback path. `renderCrossValidation()`'s existing call to `tissueBand()` automatically inherits the corrected thresholds (no separate change needed there). Added `tests/gh282-tissue-band-color-parity.test.js` (5 tests: K at 2% now gets the deficient/red badge matching its "Deficient" label, a genuine Marginal value gets the borderline/amber badge matching its own label, the no-statusMap fallback path uses the corrected 4-tier thresholds not the old 5-tier ones, "Elevated" no longer appears anywhere, clearly-sufficient/high values are unaffected). Full suite: 974/974 Jest tests pass, no regressions.

**GH-283** Added an "i" info icon to the Tissue Test Results section title, at the user's request right after the GH-282 fix, explaining in plain language what a tissue test is (measures nutrients actually inside the plant, not the soil), what the table's columns mean, and what each of the four statuses (Deficient/Marginal/Sufficient/High) represents — plus a pointer to the Soil–Tissue Cross-Validation block below it for the "soil looks fine but tissue is deficient = uptake problem, not a fertiliser problem" case. Added a `sn-tissue-results` `GAIP_GLOSSARY` entry and wired it to a `.db-info-icon` button on the section title, reusing the same info-icon/popover component as the other icons on this page (`sn-status`, `sn-compliance`, `sn-monthly-n-uncapped`). Added `tests/gh283-tissue-results-info-icon.test.js` (2 structural tests: the glossary entry exists and covers all four statuses plus the cross-validation pointer, and the icon renders inside the section title wired to that key). Full suite: 976/976 Jest tests pass, no regressions.

**GH-284** Fixed the Nutrient Status cards' progress bar showing green for HIGH-status nutrients, the same colour used for Sufficient — spotted by the user comparing a Russley AA sample where P/K/Ca/Mg/S/Fe/Zn all correctly show the blue "high" card border and blue "▲ HIGH" badge (`.sn-card.high`/`.sn-badge.high`), but the bar fill inside stayed green. Root cause: `renderNutrientCards()`'s `barClr` (`soil-nutrition-analysis.js:690`) only branched on `sc==='deficient'` (red) and `sc==='borderline'` (amber) — every other status, including `'high'`, fell through to the same `'#22c55e'` green as `'adequate'`/`'sufficient'`. The card border and badge already had a distinct blue for HIGH; the bar was the one piece of the card that never got it. Fixed by adding a `sc==='high'` branch returning `'#3b82f6'`, the same blue family already used by `.sn-card.high`'s border and `.sn-badge.high`'s text colour. Added `tests/gh284-nutrient-card-high-bar-color.test.js` (2 tests: a HIGH nutrient's bar is blue not green, deficient/borderline/adequate bar colours are unaffected). Full suite: 978/978 Jest tests pass, no regressions. Pure UI-consistency fix, no data/calculation change, no client confirmation needed (same class as GH-277).

**GH-285** Made the "Input Data Issues Detected" popup's `typical` soil-nutrient ranges methodology-aware, resolving the finding queued as a client question two entries ago — the user asked to implement it directly rather than wait. `input-range-validator.js` previously used one hardcoded `{min, max, typical}` band per nutrient in `SOIL_RANGES`, applied to every site regardless of methodology — a real, correctly-classified AA "LOW" reading (Russley Ca 220ppm against the AA floor of 400ppm) also got flagged "outside typical range (500–3000ppm)" by this unrelated generic band, reading like a possible data-entry error when it wasn't. Added `getMethodologyTypical(nutrient, methodology, texture, species)`, scoped to P/K/Ca/Mg/S only (trace elements Fe/Mn/Zn/Cu/B and pH/EC/CEC/physical properties keep the existing generic band — `GilbaClassificationConstants`' trace-element floors are on a different, unverified scale, roughly an order of magnitude below the existing generic typical bounds for the same elements, so applying them without further verification risked introducing a new inconsistency rather than fixing one). Resolves the band from the same canonical sources the Soil page's own classification already uses, never new numbers: AA — `HillLabsSampleTypes.deriveCode()`/`getRangesPpm()` (real certificate ranges, e.g. S279's Ca 400–800ppm), falling back to `GilbaClassificationConstants.AA_THRESHOLDS`' single floor (×1.5 ceiling) when no certificate matches (uncovered species/texture); SLAN — `GilbaClassificationConstants.SLAN_RANGES`' published Carrow et al. (2004) floor/ceiling; MLSN (also the default when methodology is unspecified, matching `getSoilThresholds()`'s existing "unknown → MLSN" convention elsewhere) — `GilbaClassificationConstants.MLSN_THRESHOLDS`' single floor (×1.5 ceiling, the same convention already used elsewhere on this page for MLSN's target ceiling). `validateState()` resolves `methodology`/`texture`/`species` from `state.soil.methodology`/`state.soil.soilTexture`/`state.turf.grassSpecies`, the same field names GH-259/260's AA branch already established — no new data plumbing. Falls back to the original fully-generic band whenever `GilbaClassificationConstants`/`HillLabsSampleTypes` aren't loaded, same graceful-degradation pattern as the rest of the AA SSOT work. Added `tests/gh285-input-validator-methodology-typical-ranges.test.js` (9 tests: AA certificate match narrows the band correctly and stops flagging in-range values, AA fallback to `AA_THRESHOLDS` for uncovered species, SLAN uses Carrow floor/ceiling, MLSN uses floor×1.5, trace elements stay on the generic band and produce byte-identical messages regardless of methodology, unspecified methodology defaults to MLSN, graceful degradation when the constants modules aren't loaded, `validateState()` threads the fields through correctly). Full suite: 987/987 Jest tests pass, no regressions. The popup's separate "errors and warnings render in identical red styling regardless of severity" observation from the same review was NOT part of this fix — still open.

**GH-286** Fixed a nutrient card's expanded "Why?" panel printing raw floating-point noise for the AA sufficiency range — spotted by the user on Magnesium: "Sufficiency range 36.6–85.39999999999999 ppm" (the card's own top-line "AA: 36.6-85.4 ppm" was fine, since that reads a pre-formatted `n.mlsn` string; the "Why?" row separately reads the raw `n.rangeMin`/`n.rangeMax` numbers, which carry noise from the upstream me/100g→ppm conversion). Fixed by rounding both to 1dp with `.toFixed(1)`, matching the exact convention the Soil reserve and Est. annual demand rows immediately below already use. Added `tests/gh286-sufficiency-range-float-noise.test.js` (2 tests: noisy rangeMax renders clean, a clean rangeMin/rangeMax still renders correctly with no double-rounding artefact). Full suite: 989/989 Jest tests pass, no regressions. Pure display-formatting fix, no data/calculation change.

**GH-287** Same class of bug as GH-286, one layer over: the "Unusual Values Detected" popup's message text itself — generated by `input-range-validator.js`'s `validateValue()`, not by `soil-nutrition-analysis.js` (which just displays the pre-built string) — showed "Magnesium (129 ppm) is outside typical range (36.6–85.39999999999999 ppm)". Surfaced by GH-285 (methodology-aware typical ranges): a certificate-derived bound (S279's Mg ceiling, converted from 0.70 me/100g via `meq100gToPpm()`) can legitimately compute to `85.39999999999999`. `validateValue()` already ran the *measured* value through `formatNum()` before interpolating it into the message, but not the `typLo`/`typHi` range bounds themselves. Fixed by wrapping both in `formatNum()` too, same as `num` on the same line. Added `tests/gh287-validator-message-float-noise.test.js` (2 tests: the noisy S279 Mg ceiling renders as a clean 85.4, a range with no floating-point noise involved — Iron's generic band — renders identically to before). Full suite: 991/991 Jest tests pass, no regressions.

**GH-288** Fixed GH-285's AA-fallback "typical" ceiling (used when no Hill Labs certificate matches the site's species/texture): it was computed as `AA_THRESHOLDS[bucket][nutrient] × 1.5`, an unverified assumption borrowed from the genuinely-real MLSN `×1.5` convention elsewhere on this page — but that convention doesn't hold for AA's fallback data. User caught it live (Test5-NZ, Perennial Ryegrass/AA, no certificate coverage): the Nutrient Status card showed "AA: 30.0-60.0 ppm" for Sulphur (from `hub-tissue-v3.js`'s real `aaRanges` fallback table) while the validator popup said "outside typical range (30–45 ppm)" for the same nutrient on the same card — two different ceilings from two different, disagreeing sources.
