Checked all five nutrients against the real `aaRanges` table: the `×1.5` guess only coincidentally matched Ca (500×1.5=750, also the real ceiling); P (18 vs real 28), K (112.5 vs real 175 sands / 150 vs real 235 others), Mg (150 vs real 200 sands / 210 vs real 250 others), and S (45 vs real 60) were all wrong. Fixed by replacing the multiplier with `AA_FALLBACK_CEILINGS`, a small local table mirroring `hub-tissue-v3.js`'s `aaRanges` ceilings exactly (that table is a local `const`, not exported, so it's copied with a comment rather than imported — keep in sync if `aaRanges` changes) — `AA_THRESHOLDS`' existing floors were already independently verified to match `aaRanges`' floors exactly, so only the ceiling half needed fixing. Per explicit user instruction after this was found ("do not come up with your ideas without asking me"), also audited every GH-274–287 change made this session for other unverified/invented numbers or conventions — the only other instance found was this same one; everything else (GH-282's `classify()`/`tissueBand()` parity, GH-284's `#3b82f6` reuse, GH-286/287's `.toFixed(1)`/`formatNum()` calls, GH-285's MLSN and SLAN branches) was checked against real, already-existing source data before shipping. Added `tests/gh288-aa-fallback-ceiling-real-numbers.test.js` (6 tests: P/K/Mg/S each get the real ceiling, not the old ×1.5 guess, both "just inside the real range" and "just over the real range" cases; Ca is unaffected since ×1.5 coincidentally matched; sands-bucket ceilings are also real, tested via a species with no certificate on any texture so the fallback path is actually exercised). Full suite: 997/997 Jest tests pass, no regressions.

**GH-289** Fixed the Soil–Tissue Cross-Validation block's antagonism findings showing JSON-stringify artefact quotes — the exact text spotted at the very start of this session's Tissue Test Results review: `Antagonism: "High P with low Zn (possible P→Zn antagonism)" interaction detected in tissue.`. Investigated after the user asked to verify this block's calculations generally. Root cause: `tissue-engine.js`'s `detectAntagonisms()` returns an array of plain, already-complete sentence strings (e.g. `"High P with low Zn (possible P→Zn antagonism)"`), never objects — but `renderCrossValidation()` (`soil-nutrition-analysis.js`) tried `ant.element || ant.pair` (neither property exists on a string) and fell through to `JSON.stringify(ant)`, which just wraps the string in quotes. Fixed by using the antagonism string directly; also dropped the `'Antagonism: '+...+' interaction detected in tissue.'` wrapper entirely, since the string already reads as a complete sentence and the wrapper was redundant on top of being broken. The rest of the block's logic (soil-status-vs-tissue-status cross-check, e.g. "Soil OK but tissue deficient" → uptake constraint) was checked and found conceptually correct — noted but did not fix, as a separate observation, that a HIGH soil status is never cross-checked against tissue at all (only adequate/sufficient and deficient soil trigger a comparison), which could be a legitimate additional case (excess soil nutrient + deficient tissue = poor uptake efficiency) or an intentional scope limit — not acted on without confirmation. Added `tests/gh289-cross-validation-antagonism-json-stringify.test.js` (3 tests: a real antagonism string renders clean with no quotes and no redundant wrapper, multiple antagonisms each render correctly, the unrelated soil-vs-tissue findings are unaffected). Full suite: 1000/1000 Jest tests pass, no regressions.

**GH-290** (D07 item 5, first of 2 consumers routed this round) Fixed `word-export.js`'s AA sample-type resolution always using S277 in practice. The existing priority chain was (1) `soilInput.aaSampleType`, (2) DOM `.gaip-aa-sample-type`, (3) hardcoded `'S277'` — confirmed both (1) and (2) are dead: no file anywhere ever sets `soilInput.aaSampleType`, and `.gaip-aa-sample-type` doesn't exist in any blade view, so every AA site's exported "Annual Soil Amendments" table silently used S277's certificate thresholds regardless of the site's real species/texture (e.g. a Browntop Bent/Sand site, which has its own S279 certificate, still got S277's numbers). Fixed by adding `HillLabsSampleTypes.deriveCode(species, texture)` as a new step between the dead DOM fallback and the hardcoded default — reading `data.turf.grassSpecies`/`.species` and `soilInput.soilTexture`, the same resolver `mlsnEngine()` (GH-260) and `SampleAnalysisController.php` (GH-268) already use. The `%BS`/`me/100g` axis-routing logic downstream (`b35fix437/441`) was already correct and untouched — this only fixes which certificate code gets selected. The hardcoded `'S277'` default is now only reached for genuinely uncovered species/texture combinations. Added `tests/gh290-word-export-aa-sample-type-derive-code.test.js` (4 structural tests, following this repo's existing convention for word-export.js — see `monthly-n-distribution-renderer-b35fix387.test.js` — since the file is too large/DOM-dependent to execute in a vm sandbox for one branch: `deriveCode()` call present with the right species/texture sources, tried strictly between the dead DOM fallback and the S277 default, only applied when non-null, the S277 fallback line itself untouched). Full suite: 1004/1004 Jest tests pass, no regressions.

**GH-291** (D07 item 5, second of 2 consumers routed this round) Fixed `nutrition-prebble-integration.js`'s K Reconciliation preview (`_evaluateKReconPreview()`, the "Spot-K reconciliation" row in the Plan page's Nutrient Delivery Summary table) sourcing its AA floor from `ammonium-acetate-methodology.js`'s `getSufficiencyRange('K', soilType)` — generic "agricultural/horticultural" ranges (documented fallback: sands 75ppm / others 100ppm) that don't match Hill Labs' actual turf certificates (e.g. S277/S279's real K floor is 78.2ppm), the same class of gap GH-260 already fixed for the Soil page's own cards. Not just a display discrepancy — this floor feeds directly into `synthSoil.thresholds.K.min`, which drives the real `_synthesiseKReconDecision()` call deciding whether Spot-K reconciliation fires at all. Fixed by replacing the `ammonium-acetate-methodology.js` call with `HillLabsSampleTypes.deriveCode(species, texture)` + `getRangesPpm(code, 'K').min`, species read from `GAIP_STATE.turf.grassSpecies`/`.warmBase` (same fields GH-260 established), texture reusing the branch's own already-extracted `tx` string. The existing documented sands/others fallback (75/100) is preserved as the final default, now only reached when `deriveCode()` finds no certificate match. SLAN and MLSN branches of the same function untouched. Added `tests/gh291-k-recon-preview-derive-code.test.js` (5 structural tests, same convention as GH-290: old `ammonium-acetate-methodology.js` call gone from the branch, `deriveCode()`+`getRangesPpm()` resolve the floor correctly, species sourced from the right fields, the sands/others fallback still guards on `floor === null`, SLAN/MLSN branches unchanged). Full suite: 1009/1009 Jest tests pass, no regressions. Item 5's third consumer (`tissue-corrective-engine-pure.js`) confirmed dead code (zero consumers found anywhere) during this round's investigation — deprioritized, not fixed this round; a newly-found fourth issue in the same area (`soil-tissue-integration.js`'s `recommendSoilAmendments()`, live in Word export but silently never fires for AA sites via a `ratioVsMLSN` field the AA branch never sets) was added to the plan but not yet fixed.

**GH-292** Fixed the Plan page's K Reconciliation preview (`_evaluateKReconPreview()`, the "Spot-K reconciliation" row in Nutrient Delivery Summary — the same feature GH-291 fixed the AA floor for) always showing "Load a soil sample to see whether spot-K reconciliation will fire at export," even for sites with real, complete soil data (confirmed live: Test5-NZ has a real sample with K=199ppm). Root cause: the function's very first line gated its entire soil-data lookup on `window.GAIP_WordExport` existing (`if (!wx || typeof wx._synthesiseKReconDecision !== 'function') return result;`), and `word-export.js` — the only file that ever defines `_synthesiseKReconDecision` — was never loaded on `plan.blade.php` at all. So the function returned `'no-soil'` immediately, before ever attempting to read soil data, regardless of what soil data actually existed. This meant the Spot-K reconciliation preview had structurally never been able to work on the Plan page, for any site, ever. Considered just adding a `<script>` tag for the full ~14k-line `word-export.js` to `plan.blade.php`, but per user direction extracted the actual decision logic into a new lightweight shared file instead. Traced `_synthesiseKReconDecision`'s real dependencies (2 small pure helpers, `_potassiumDisplayLabel`/`_detectKDisplayRegion` — region-aware K-product label formatting, also independently used elsewhere in `word-export.js`) and moved all three, verbatim, into new `assets/k-reconciliation-decision.js` (exposed as `window.GAIP_KReconDecision`). `word-export.js` keeps thin same-named wrapper functions delegating to the shared file — every existing internal call site (4 of them) and `word-export-combined.js`'s `_wx._synthesiseKReconDecision(...)` calls needed zero changes, since the wrappers preserve the exact same local function names and the export object. `nutrition-prebble-integration.js`'s preview now gates on `window.GAIP_KReconDecision` (always available on the Plan page) instead of `window.GAIP_WordExport`; the optional monthly-split-preview step (which genuinely does need the full `word-export.js`, for `_amendmentDecisionsToProducts`) stays defensive (`if (wx && typeof wx._amendmentDecisionsToProducts === 'function')`) and simply produces no monthly split rows when `word-export.js` isn't loaded, same graceful-degradation shape as before. Added `k-reconciliation-decision.js` (before `word-export.js`) to all 4 blade views that already loaded `word-export.js` (`hub.blade.php`, `reports/export.blade.php`, `reports/forensic.blade.php`, `reports/scenarios.blade.php`) so nothing there changes behaviour, plus `plan.blade.php` (before `nutrition-prebble-integration.js`) — the actual fix. Verified Blade/PHP syntax via `php artisan view:cache` (compiles every view; caught nothing) inside the `gilba_app` container, then `view:clear` to restore normal dev-mode recompilation; verified JS syntax via `node --check` on all three touched JS files. Added `tests/gh292-k-reconciliation-decision-shared-file.test.js` (17 tests: the shared file's two-gate decision logic — balance gate, soil-K sanity gate, near-floor buffer, capped spot rate — and region-aware label formatting, all executed directly via vm; word-export.js's wrapper functions delegate correctly and the old heavy implementation is gone; `nutrition-prebble-integration.js` gates on the new file and defensively guards the optional `wx` step; all 5 blade views load the new file before its consumer). Full suite: 1026/1026 Jest tests pass, no regressions.

**GH-294** Fixed the Plan page's K Reconciliation preview (GH-291/292) still showing the generic sands/others fallback floor (100ppm) instead of the site's real Hill Labs certificate floor (e.g. S277's 78.2ppm), even for a site with a real soil sample and a real "Soil texture" setting (confirmed live: Test5-NZ, Perennial Ryegrass/AA/Sand). Root cause, confirmed via temporary debug instrumentation in `_evaluateKReconPreview()`: species resolved correctly (`'Perennial Ryegrass'`) but soil texture resolved to `''` (empty) every time. Traced upstream: `Site::soil_texture_override` (falling back to `Account::soil_texture`) lives on the Site/Account models, not inside the `gaip`-namespace `SiteConfig` JSON blob that `PageController::topbarData()` and `plan.blade.php`'s inline `GAIP_STATE` bridge read soil data from — so the bridge's `si.soil` object never carried a `soilTexture` field at all, and `deriveCode(species, '')` reads an empty texture as "not sand," silently falling back to the generic range regardless of the site's actual setting. Fixed by adding `$soilTexture` to `PageController::topbarData()` (same site-override-then-account-fallback chain already established at `SampleController.php:374`/`HillLabsSampleTypes.resolveSoilTexture()`), passed to both `plan()` and `reports()` (only `plan.blade.php` consumes it); `plan.blade.php`'s bridge now threads it into both `window.GAIP_HUB_CONFIG.soilTexture` and `state.inputs.soil.soilTexture`. Verified Blade/PHP syntax via `php artisan view:cache`/`view:clear`. Added `app/tests/Feature/PlanPageSoilTextureTest.php` (4 PHPUnit tests: site-override wins, falls back to account when override is null, null when neither is set, bridge threads the value into `state.inputs.soil`) — not yet confirmed passing in this environment: `RefreshDatabase`-based Feature tests currently fail here on an unrelated, pre-existing SQLite incompatibility (`database/migrations/2026_05_22_000000_add_unique_index_to_predictions.php`'s MySQL-only multi-table `DELETE p1 FROM predictions p1 INNER JOIN predictions p2 ON ...` syntax, rejected by the sqlite `:memory:` test DB), which appears to affect every `RefreshDatabase` Feature test in this setup, not something introduced by this change — needs re-confirming once that migration issue is resolved or worked around. JS side unaffected (PHP/Blade-only change); full Jest suite unchanged at 1026/1026.

**GH-295** Fixed a regression report — "Monthly N Distribution" chart back to showing the entire year's N total crammed into a single month (the exact pre-GH-278 symptom), reproduced live via a Re-run + `/analysis` reload on the Russley/Browntop Bent site. Investigated and ruled out the GH-292/294 changes as the cause (neither touches `nutrition-summary-integration.js`, `hub-persistence.js`, or their script-loading order in any relevant way; the `[GilbaPersist] Augmented dailyPattern, length: 8` log line the user's console output showed is an unrelated, always-present reconstruction of the "Growth & Temperature" panel's 8-day forecast window, not the Monthly N Distribution pipeline — a wrong inference in this session's own notes, corrected here rather than acted on). Real root cause: GH-278 fixed *when* `nutrition-summary-integration.js` computes the correct monthly N breakdown (on `gaip:monthly-normals-ready`, once `climateMetrics.monthlyTemps` resolves) but never fixed *whether that correction ever gets persisted*. `hub-persistence.js`'s `cacheAnalysisResults()` (which writes `cache.computed.soilNutrition.monthlyN` from `global.__GAIP_MONTHLY_N__`) only runs on a fixed list of `gaipEvents`, debounced 1s (`CONFIG.saveDebounce`) — and `'gaip:monthly-normals-ready'` (dispatched by `climate-normals-service.js` after its own async NASA-POWER/Open-Meteo fetch) was never in that list. Whenever that fetch resolved more than 1s after the last of the other trigger events (analysis-complete/orchestrator-complete/weather-ready — entirely plausible for a real network call, not an edge case), GH-278's corrected value existed only in memory and was never written to the persisted cache; any page that reads from that cache (`/analysis` reload, a fresh Re-run) fell back to `soil-nutrition-analysis.js`'s `renderMonthlyN()`'s `climate.growth.dailyPattern` approximation (an 8-day forecast window, not annual normals) — reproducing the exact "all N in one month" bug non-deterministically, depending on fetch timing. Fixed by adding `'gaip:monthly-normals-ready'` to `hub-persistence.js`'s `gaipEvents` list, so a save is (re-)scheduled once the monthly normals resolve, same as every other reactive analysis event; confirmed `cache.computed.soilNutrition` is rebuilt fresh from the live MLSN table on every `cacheAnalysisResults()` call (not a one-time signal), so a later resave correctly picks up the by-then-corrected `__GAIP_MONTHLY_N__`. Added `tests/gh295-monthly-n-persistence-save-trigger.test.js` (3 structural tests: `gaipEvents` includes the new event, it's wired through the same `forEach`/`scheduleSave()` as the others, the monthlyN capture line is unconditional on `__GAIP_MONTHLY_N__`'s current value). Full suite: 1029/1029 Jest tests pass, no regressions.

**GH-296** User reported GH-295 alone didn't fully fix it — 3 consecutive Re-runs of the same site showed correct/wrong/correct Monthly N Distribution, proving a second, independent race upstream of persistence. Root cause: `extractMonthlyTemps()`'s two reads (`global.climateMetrics.monthlyTemps`, then `GilbaHubOrchestrator`'s `state.climate.monthlyTemps`) both get silently wiped on **every** Re-run, not just once — `hub-tissue-v3.js`'s `validateClimateMetrics()` rebuilds `window.climateMetrics` from a strict whitelist (`temperature`/`stress`/`growth`/`moisture` only, no `monthlyTemps` field) on every weather fetch, and `hub-orchestrator.js`'s canonical-state rebuild (`GAIP_CANONICAL_STATE.climate = {source, temperature, humidity, dewpoint, precipitation, et, wind, solar, growthPotential, ...}`) does the identical whitelist-rebuild to the orchestrator's own `state.climate`. Meanwhile `climate-normals-service.js`'s NASA POWER/Open-Meteo fetch (the thing that originally populates both of those fields via `applyResult()`) only ever runs **once** per page load (`DOMContentLoaded`) — nothing re-triggers it on Re-run (`gaip:site-config-applied`, the only event `autoTrigger()` listens for besides page load, fires only on initial restore/site-switch, not Re-run). So whether either read still has `monthlyTemps` at the moment a given Re-run's save fires depends purely on whether that one-time fetch happened to resolve before or after that particular Re-run's climate rebuild — a genuine, non-deterministic race across repeated Re-runs of the same site, exactly matching the reported correct/wrong/correct pattern. Fixed by adding a third fallback tier, `_resolvedNormals()`, reading `climate-normals-service.js`'s own per-coordinate result cache directly via its `getResolvedSync(lat, lon)` API — a private in-memory cache neither wipe above ever touches, so once the one-time fetch has resolved at all (true by the time a user has clicked Re-run even once), this tier stays correct across every subsequent Re-run, closing the race rather than trying to win it. Coordinate read (`_readCoordsForNormals()`) duplicates `climate-normals-service.js`'s own `readCoords()` pattern (`.gaip-lat`/`.gaip-lon` DOM, falling back to `GAIP_STATE.location`) since that file exposes no "current site's coords" getter to import. Also fixed `extractMonthlyTempsSource()` and the info-tooltip's period text for the same reason — both previously read only the same wipeable `global.climateMetrics.monthlyTempsSource`/`.monthlyTempsPeriod`, which would have shown "unavailable"/blank period even in a run where the third tier successfully recovered real data, a smaller version of the same inconsistency. Added `tests/gh296-monthly-temps-race-proof-fallback.test.js` (5 tests: structural pins for the new fallback tier and helper functions in all 3 read sites; behavioural — `renderNutritionSummary()` still computes real monthly N via `vm` when `climateMetrics`/orchestrator state are both wiped but `GilbaClimateNormalsService.getResolvedSync()` still has the data; still shows "Climate data unavailable" when every tier is genuinely empty). Full suite: 1033/1033 Jest tests pass, no regressions.

**GH-297** User reported the Monthly N Distribution bug a third time after GH-295/296, on a fresh site switch (not a repeated Re-run). Added temporary `[GH296-DEBUG]` console.log instrumentation (to `hub-persistence.js`'s `_ensureMonthlyNormalsBounded()`/`cacheAnalysisResults()`'s monthlyN capture, and `nutrition-summary-integration.js`'s `updateNutritionSummary()`) and asked the user to reproduce — live logs showed `climateMetrics.monthlyTemps: true` and `.gaip-nutrition-summary-wrapper` found, i.e. GH-295/296's fixes were working correctly, yet `__GAIP_MONTHLY_N__` stayed `undefined` after `renderNutritionSummary()` ran — proving this was **not** a climate-normals race at all. Root cause: `extractSoilValues()` (the function `renderNutritionSummary()` calls first, gating everything else on its `hasData` check) only reads `GAIP_STATE.soil`, `GilbaHubOrchestrator`'s `state.soil`/`state.inputs.soil`, `GAIP_NUTRITION_SOIL_CACHE`, and DOM fields — all of which derive from the MLSN engine's rendered HTML table. On a fresh site switch that table can still be empty at the exact moment `gaip:monthly-normals-ready`/`gaip:analysis-complete` fire for the new site — confirmed directly in the same log: `hub-persistence.js`'s own primary MLSN-scrape path logged `verdict: NO DATA` for this switch, while its separate `GAIP_SampleManager`-based fallback (site-aware `getAllSamples()` keyed by `GAIP_HUB_CONFIG.activeSiteId`, falling back to `getSamples()`) found a real, complete sample (`verdict: HIGH_RISK | nutrients: 10`) seconds later. `extractSoilValues()` had no equivalent fallback, so it always saw empty soil data for this site and `renderNutritionSummary()` bailed on its `hasData` check before ever reaching the monthly-N computation — regardless of whether climate data was ready. Fixed by giving `extractSoilValues()` the same `GAIP_SampleManager` fallback `hub-persistence.js` already uses (same site-aware lookup, same `_ppm`/`_me` field-name cleanup), as a fifth tier before the DOM-field fallback. Debug instrumentation removed after the root cause was confirmed. Added `tests/gh297-soil-values-samplemanager-fallback.test.js` (4 tests: structural pin for the new fallback's position in the function; behavioural — `renderNutritionSummary()` computes real monthly N via the site-aware `getAllSamples()` path and via the simpler `getSamples()` fallback when all four prior sources are empty; still shows the "enter soil test values" placeholder when `GAIP_SampleManager` genuinely has no soil samples either). Full suite: 1037/1037 Jest tests pass, no regressions.

**GH-298** User reported the Monthly N Distribution bug a **fourth** time after GH-295/296/297, switching to yet another site (Buffalograss/"westview", brand-new coordinates never fetched before this session). Live log confirmed GH-297's `GAIP_SampleManager` fallback was working (`soilNutrition from sample: sample_118 | verdict: ACCEPTABLE | nutrients: 10`), but by that point the debug instrumentation used to diagnose GH-297 had already been removed, so the exact new gap (most likely: a brand-new coordinate's NASA POWER/Open-Meteo fetch not resolving inside `_ensureMonthlyNormalsBounded()`'s 4s bound) wasn't directly re-confirmed this round. Rather than re-add instrumentation and chase a 5th individual event-ordering gap in `nutrition-summary-integration.js`'s purely reactive render chain (a fixed list of trigger events — `gaip:soil-data-update`/`gaip:mlsn-calculated`/`gaip:turf-profile-change`/`gaip:monthly-normals-ready`/~100ms after `gaip:analysis-complete` — none of which is guaranteed to fire at the exact moment both soil data and climate normals are simultaneously ready), took a structural approach: `_doRerunSync()` — the one function that actually `POST`s to `/api/analysis-cache` (the debounced `gaipEvents`-driven `scheduleSave()` path elsewhere only writes to `localStorage`, so it isn't what `/analysis` reads after a reload) — now calls `window.GilbaNutritionSummary.renderNutritionSummary()` directly and synchronously, immediately after `_ensureMonthlyNormalsBounded()` resolves and immediately before `cacheAnalysisResults()` reads `window.__GAIP_MONTHLY_N__`. This guarantees one last, direct computation attempt using whatever soil/climate state is actually available at the latest possible moment before persisting, independent of whether any specific reactive listener fired in time — closing the whole class of "some event didn't fire before the save" bugs GH-295/296/297 were each individually patching, rather than adding a 5th special-cased fix. Guarded (`GilbaNutritionSummary`/method existence check) and wrapped in try/catch, matching `_ensureMonthlyNormalsBounded()`'s own defensive style; the two sibling `cacheAnalysisResults()` call sites (`GilbaPersistence.save()`'s autosave, `gaip:sensor-upgrade-complete`'s re-sync) are untouched, matching GH-251's established scoping. Added `tests/gh298-doRerunSync-forces-monthly-n-render.test.js` (3 structural tests: the render call sits between the bounded wait and `cacheAnalysisResults()`; it's guarded and try/catch-wrapped; GH-251's existing behaviour and sibling call sites remain unchanged). Full suite: 1040/1040 Jest tests pass, no regressions.

**GH-299** (D07 item 6) Added the AA ceiling to `nutrition-requirement-engine.js` — the Nutrition Program's separate annual-fertiliser-planning engine (distinct from items 3-5's Soil-page classification, already fixed). This is the actual fix for the originally-reported D07 symptom: the AA branch of `calculateNutrientRequirement()` never had a ceiling at all — it always returned pure removal regardless of how far above the certificate's sufficiency range the soil already was, so the Annual K Requirement figure could never settle at 0 for a HIGH AA site the way MLSN's "above target → 0" tier already does. Fixed by adding an optional `config.aaRange` ({min,max} ppm) to the `AMMONIUM_ACETATE` branch: when present and `currentLevel >= aaRange.max`, returns `annualRequirement: 0, status: 'High'` (mirrors the MLSN tier's shape exactly); absent (uncovered species/texture, or a nutrient the SSOT has no range for) keeps the unconditional pure-removal behaviour unchanged — the same graceful-degradation contract every other item in this plan uses. Since `calculateNutrientRequirement()` is a shared function looped over P/K/Ca/Mg/S by `calculateAllRequirements()`, the ceiling applies to all five nutrients automatically — not a K-only carve-out, matching the plan's explicit requirement (`calculateAllRequirements()` now resolves a per-nutrient `config.aaRange` from a new `config.aaRanges` map, since the SSOT's ranges differ per nutrient — e.g. S277's K and Ca bounds aren't the same me/100g range). The engine itself stays a pure function — it never reads `HillLabsSampleTypes`/`window`/DOM; `compute()` just threads a caller-supplied `inputs.aaRanges` through. All 3 external callers updated to resolve `aaRanges` via `deriveCode()`/`getRangesPpm()`, gated so MLSN/SLAN sites never attempt the resolution at all: `nutrition-summary-integration.js` (new `_resolveAARanges()` helper, called before `Engine.compute()` — also fixed a previously-unnoticed gap where `extractSoilValues()`'s `extractFromSoilData()` dropped `soil.methodology`/`.soilTexture` entirely, meaning this call site silently treated every AA site as MLSN for the Nutrition Program figure regardless of its real methodology, since `compute()` never saw a real `soil.methodology` to dispatch on); `word-export.js` (resolved once inside `_buildEngineInputs()`, using the same `window.GAIP_STATE.soil.soilTexture` source GH-290's `aaSampleType` resolution already established, attached to `data.engineInputs.aaRanges` so it's available wherever that object is read); `word-export-combined.js` (inherits `_ei.aaRanges` from the same `data.engineInputs` object rather than a separate resolution — same "inherits the fix" pattern GH-290 established for `aaSampleType`). Added `tests/gh299-aa-ceiling-nutrition-requirement-engine.test.js` (11 tests: ceiling fires at/above `aaRange.max` for K and a non-K nutrient (Ca), stays pure-removal below the ceiling and when `aaRange`/`aaRanges` is absent entirely, `calculateAllRequirements()` resolves per-nutrient ranges without leaking one nutrient's ceiling onto another, `compute()` threads `inputs.aaRanges` end-to-end, MLSN/SLAN branches byte-identical regression pin) and `tests/gh299-aa-ceiling-consumers.test.js` (9 tests: structural pins for all 3 callers' resolution gating and compute() call sites, plus a `vm`-sandboxed end-to-end behavioural test proving an AA site with soil K above the S277 ceiling renders `0`/`High` in the Nutrition Program card, and that an MLSN site never even calls `deriveCode()`). Full suite: 1060/1060 Jest tests pass, no regressions.

**GH-300** (D07 item 6 follow-up) User regenerated the Plan page's Nutrition Program on a HIGH AA site (K=199ppm, S277 certificate ceiling 195.7ppm) right after GH-299 shipped and annualK still showed 110. Root cause: `nutrition-calendar.js`'s `computeProgram()` — the Plan page's "Generate Nutrition Program" feature — is a **structurally separate** implementation from `nutrition-requirement-engine.js` (GH-299), with its own `getThresholds()`/`calculateDeficit()`, never routed through the shared engine, so GH-299's fix never reached it. Its `calculateDeficit()` only ever adds a correction when soil is *below* the floor (`CONFIG.aaThresholds`, a texture-only sands/others table with a single per-nutrient floor, no ceiling concept at all) — a HIGH-classified nutrient always fell through to pure removal, the exact pre-GH-299 bug, just in a parallel code path. Fixed by adding the same certificate-backed ceiling check after `computeProgram()`'s STEP 5 builds `annualRequirements`: resolves a per-nutrient `{min,max}` ppm range via `HillLabsSampleTypes.deriveCode()`/`getRangesPpm()` (same SSOT items 3-5/GH-299 already use) when methodology is AA, overriding `annualRequirements[nutrient]` to `0` when `soilPpm[nutrient] >= range.max`; graceful-degradation to today's uncapped behaviour when `deriveCode()` finds no certificate match (uncovered species/texture) or `HillLabsSampleTypes` isn't loaded. Deliberately left `calculateDeficit()`'s existing below-floor correction logic untouched — `nutrition-calendar.js`'s AA branch adds deficit correction on top of removal, but `nutrition-requirement-engine.js`'s AA branch (GH-299) never does; this is a real, pre-existing algorithmic difference between the two implementations that this fix does not attempt to reconcile, per the project's rule against inventing algorithm changes without confirming which behaviour is correct. `collectFromState()` gained two new fields feeding the resolution: `soilTexture` (from `state.inputs.soil.soilTexture`, the real GH-294 field — distinct from the existing `aaTextureKey`, which reads the decorative/dead `.gaip-aa-soil-texture` DOM element per the D07 item 2 finding) and `CEC` (for %BS-axis certificates, not currently exercised by S277/S279). Species passed to `deriveCode()` is `speciesDisplay` (the raw human-facing string), not `species` (already collapsed through `normalizeSpecies()`'s `toNutrientKey()` step into a different key space `deriveCode()` doesn't expect) — caught before shipping by checking `deriveCode()`'s own canonical-key assumptions against what `collectFromState()` actually returns. Since `word-export-combined.js` also calls `NutritionCalendar.computeProgram()` directly (a 4th consumer, found during this investigation, separate from the 3 `NutritionRequirementEngine_Pure.compute()` callers GH-299 covered), it inherits this fix automatically — no separate change needed there. Added `tests/gh300-aa-ceiling-nutrition-calendar.test.js` (5 tests: HIGH nutrient at/above the ceiling shows 0, a nutrient below its ceiling is unaffected, MLSN methodology never calls `deriveCode()` at all, graceful degradation for uncovered species/texture and for `HillLabsSampleTypes` not being loaded). Full suite: 1065/1065 Jest tests pass, no regressions.

**GH-301** User regenerated the program again after GH-300 and annualK still showed 110. Added temporary `[GH300-DEBUG]` console.log instrumentation to GH-300's ceiling-check block and asked the user to reproduce — live log showed `hasHLST: false`: `window.HillLabsSampleTypes` was never loaded on the page at all, so the whole ceiling block short-circuited on its very first guard, before ever reaching `deriveCode()`. Root cause: `plan.blade.php` (the Plan page — where "Generate Nutrition Program" actually runs) never had a `<script>` tag for `hill-labs-sample-types.js` in the first place; it was only ever loaded on `hub.blade.php` and the 3 `reports/*.blade.php` views. Confirmed this is a Plan-page-only gap — all 4 other views that load `nutrition-calendar.js` already load `hill-labs-sample-types.js` too. Fixed by adding `<script src="{{ $legacyAssetUrl('hill-labs-sample-types.js') }}"></script>` to `plan.blade.php`, immediately before `nutrition-calendar.js`'s own script tag (same "must load before" pattern GH-292 already established for this page). Verified Blade syntax via `php artisan view:cache`/`view:clear`. No JS test coverage needed (a Blade `<script>` tag addition, not application logic) — Jest suite unchanged at 1065/1065. *(Correction, GH-303 entry below: debug instrumentation was prematurely removed here before the user had confirmed the fix worked live — this fix alone did not resolve the reported symptom; see GH-303 for the actual final root cause and the user's standing "don't remove logs before I confirm" instruction.)*

**GH-302** User regenerated the program a third time after GH-301 and annualK **still** showed 110. Added temporary `[GH301-DEBUG]` console.log instrumentation to `plan.blade.php`'s bridge script and to `syncSoilFromDOM()`'s carry-over/writeback points, and asked the user to reproduce — two live log captures (one at page load, one at the "Generate" click) told the full story: the page-load bridge correctly set `hub.soilTexture: 'sand'` / `si.soil.soilTexture: 'sand'` / `GAIP_STATE.inputs.soil.soilTexture: 'sand'` (GH-294/301 both working), but by the time `syncSoilFromDOM()` ran at the "Generate" click, its carry-over read showed `existing.soilTexture: undefined` — the field had been silently dropped somewhere between page load and the click. Root cause: `site-selector-ui.js`'s "Loaded soil sample" cascade (visible in the same log, firing multiple times during site-config restore) rebuilds `GAIP_STATE.inputs.soil` from the active sample's own P/K/Ca/Mg/S values — a soil *sample* has no concept of site-level soil texture, so that rebuild has no field to preserve and the bridge's earlier value is gone by the next read. Fixed by giving `syncSoilFromDOM()`'s carry-over a second, more stable fallback: `window.GAIP_HUB_CONFIG.soilTexture` — a plain top-level config object set once by the page bridge that sample-load code never touches — applied only when the carried-over `soilState.soilTexture` is still empty, and before the writeback so the recovered value actually persists back into `GAIP_STATE.inputs.soil` for the rest of the request (not just this one read). Added `tests/gh302-soiltexture-hubconfig-fallback.test.js` (4 structural tests: the fallback assignment exists and reads the right source; it runs after the existing carry-over so it only fires when needed; it runs before the writeback so the recovered value is actually persisted; it's guarded so it never clobbers an already-present value). Full suite: 1069/1069 Jest tests pass, no regressions. Debug instrumentation was removed here, then the user reported annualK **still** showed 110 a fourth time — the removal was premature (this fix was real and necessary, but not sufficient on its own); the user explicitly instructed "do not remove logs until I confirm that issue is fixed" going forward, and the instrumentation was restored. See GH-303 for the actual final root cause.

**GH-303** Per the user's new standing instruction, re-added (rather than re-created) the `[GH302-DEBUG]` instrumentation from GH-302 to `plan.blade.php`'s bridge, `syncSoilFromDOM()`, and GH-300's AA-ceiling-check block in `computeProgram()`, and asked the user to reproduce once more. The live log this time showed the full chain working correctly end to end for the first time — `soilTexture: 'sand'` at every checkpoint (bridge, carry-over fallback, `computeProgram()` inputs) — but `deriveCode result: null` despite `speciesDisplay: 'Perennial Ryegrass'` and `soilTexture: 'sand'` being exactly the inputs `deriveCode('perennialRyegrass', <sand-ish>)` should resolve to `S277`. Root cause: `deriveCode()` (`hill-labs-sample-types.js`) normalises its `species` argument via `SpeciesController.normalize()` when that global is present (`"Perennial Ryegrass"` → canonical key `'perennialRyegrass'`), falling back to the raw, un-normalised string otherwise — and `species-controller.js` was never loaded on `plan.blade.php` at all (confirmed: `hub.blade.php` and all 3 `reports/*.blade.php` views that also load `nutrition-calendar.js` already load it; only the Plan page didn't), same missing-script-tag bug class as GH-301, different asset. So `canonical` stayed `"Perennial Ryegrass"` (capitalised, with a space), which never matches `deriveCode()`'s exact `canonical === 'perennialRyegrass'` check, and `deriveCode()` silently returned `null` on every call from this page — regardless of how correct `speciesDisplay`/`soilTexture` were, which is why GH-301 and GH-302's fixes were both real and necessary but never sufficient on their own. Fixed by adding `<script src="{{ $legacyAssetUrl('species-controller.js') }}"></script>` to `plan.blade.php`, before `nutrition-calendar.js`'s script tag (`species-controller.js` has no page-specific DOM dependency in its `init()` — confirmed safe to add standalone). Verified Blade syntax via `php artisan view:cache`/`view:clear`; full Jest suite for the GH-299/300/302 test files re-run with the debug logs present (29/29 pass, confirming the debug instrumentation doesn't disturb the structural regex pins). **Debug instrumentation intentionally left in place** — per the user's GH-302 correction, it will only be removed once the user confirms live in the UI that the K ceiling now actually applies (annualK shows 0, not 110, for this AA site).

**Update**: user confirmed live — regenerating the Nutrition Program now shows P/K/Ca/Mg all correctly zeroed (Hagley Oval's actual soil values all sit above their S277 certificate ceilings; only S and N remain non-zero, S because S277 prints no Sulphur range at all, N because it's outside the AA-ceiling logic entirely). This confirms the GH-299/300/301/302/303 chain is genuinely fixed end to end. `[GH302-DEBUG]` logs remain in place at time of writing (not yet explicitly asked to be removed — will stay until the user says to remove them, per the standing GH-302 instruction).

**GH-304** (D07 item 7, "label, don't remove" — user decision, 2026-08-24) User asked why the Analysis page's Soil card showed a range for Sulphur ("AA: 30.0-60.0 ppm", flagged HIGH) on the same Hagley Oval/S277 site where the Nutrition Program correctly zeroed everything except S — traced to the fact that S277's certificate prints no Sulphur range at all, so `mlsnEngine()`'s AA branch (GH-260) silently falls back to the old texture-only "sands" generic range (30-60ppm) for S specifically, indistinguishable in the UI from the certificate-backed P/K/Ca/Mg ranges next to it. Revisited the plan's item 7 (previously scoped as "replace the figure with a message") — user chose a lighter design: **label the generic number, keep it visible**, rather than removing it. Implemented across all four places item 7 calls for:
- `hub-tissue-v3.js`: `mlsnEngine()`'s AA branch now builds an `aaRangeSource` map (defaults every nutrient to `'texture-fallback'`, flips to `'certificate'` only when `getRangesPpm()` actually resolves a range for that specific nutrient on the matched code), attaches `rangeSource` to each AA nutrient result, and carries it through the existing HTML round-trip as a new `data-range-source` attribute (alongside the existing `data-range-min`/`max`).
- `hub-persistence.js`: both DOMParser scrapers (the primary one and the GH-266 sample-switch-fallback copy) now read `row.dataset.rangeSource` into `nutrients[]`, matching the existing `rangeMin`/`rangeMax` precedent in both places.
- `app/app/Http/Controllers/SampleAnalysisController.php`: `computeNutrients()`'s AA branch (GH-268) now sets `rangeSource` (`'certificate'`/`'texture-fallback'`) directly in its JSON response per nutrient, using the identical rule as the JS side, so the sample-switcher path can't disagree with the initial-load path on which nutrients are certificate-backed.
- `soil-nutrition-analysis.js`: `renderNutrientCards()` and `renderAnnualRequirements()` render a small `.sn-generic-badge` ("Generic") next to the figure whenever `n.rangeSource === 'texture-fallback'` — the number is unchanged, only the framing changes. Also appended a note to the AA "Why?" panel's source line for generic-range nutrients.
- `site-settings-panel.js`: `updateMethodNote()` gained an AA branch that resolves `HillLabsSampleTypes.deriveCode()` from the live `.gaip-species`/`.gaip-soil-texture` DOM fields (same fields this panel already reads elsewhere) and shows an inline note when the combination has no certificate at all, before the user ever looks at a result page. Wired the species-change handler to re-check this note too (coverage depends on species, not just methodology).
Deliberately deferred (not part of this round, per the plan's effort/impact note): a matching visual label on the K Reconciliation floor preview — K is covered by 3 of the 4 SSOT codes today so the generic-fallback case is comparatively rare there, and the feature has no existing "range display" to attach a badge to without a larger refactor; flagged in the plan as intentionally deferred, not silently dropped.

**Follow-up, same session**: user reported the badge showed a question-mark cursor on hover (from an initial `cursor:help` CSS rule) but clicking it did nothing — confirmed as a real, self-inflicted UX bug (implied interactivity with no actual action). Fixed by making the nutrient-card badge instance genuinely clickable: gave the existing "Why?" toggle button a stable id, and wired the badge's `onclick` to trigger that same button's click handler (so badge and button can never disagree about panel-open state) — the Annual-Requirements badge instance has no "Why?" panel to open, so it was left as a plain, non-interactive hover label with the misleading `cursor:help` removed from the shared base CSS rule entirely (only the clickable nutrient-card instance now sets `cursor:pointer`, inline, only when a panel genuinely exists to open).

**Follow-up 2, same session**: user asked to check whether the generic-range label reached the Plan page's own "Annual Requirements (kg/ha)" card too (the one showing N/P/K/Ca/Mg/S totals from "Generate Nutrition Program") — still showed S at 10 kg/ha/yr with no indication it's a generic estimate. Traced to a **5th independent implementation**, not covered by the original GH-304 pass: that card is rendered by `nutrition-calendar.js`'s `renderSummary()` from `computeProgram()`'s own `annual_totals`, a structurally separate code path from `soil-nutrition-analysis.js` (same "N independent implementations of one AA concept" pattern this whole D07 chain keeps finding). Fixed the same way: `computeProgram()`'s existing AA-ceiling block (GH-300/303) now also builds `annual_totals_range_source` (per P/K/Ca/Mg/S, `'certificate'` when `getRangesPpm()` resolves a range for that nutrient — independent of whether the ceiling actually fires — `'texture-fallback'` otherwise; N excluded, no AA range concept applies to it), returned alongside `annual_totals`. `renderSummary()` reads it to show the same neutral-grey "Generic" badge (new `.gilba-nut-generic-badge` CSS class, matching `soil-nutrition-analysis.js`'s `.sn-generic-badge` styling/intent but using this page's own `--gaip-*` design tokens) next to the nutrient name whenever AA + `rangeSource[el] === 'texture-fallback'` — number unchanged, same label-don't-remove decision. Deliberately deferred, matching the K-recon-floor deferral: a footnote/marker on the CSV export (`exportCSV()`, plain text, no natural place for a badge) — flagged, not silently dropped. Added `tests/gh304-nutrition-calendar-range-source.test.js` (8 tests: `annual_totals_range_source` tagging for certificate/texture-fallback/uncovered-species/MLSN/no-SSOT-loaded cases, plus structural pins for the CSS class and the `renderSummary()` gating logic). Full suite: 1103/1103 Jest tests pass.

Added `tests/gh304-aa-range-source-labeling.test.js` (26 tests: structural + behavioural coverage of the `aaRangeSource` tagging and `data-range-source` round-trip in `hub-tissue-v3.js`, both `hub-persistence.js` scrapers reading it, `renderNutrientCards()`/`renderAnnualRequirements()`'s badge rendering including the S277+Sulphur live case, the `site-settings-panel.js` coverage-note structural pins, and the badge-click-opens-Why-panel follow-up fix) and `app/tests/Unit/SampleAnalysisControllerRangeSourceTest.php` (5 PHPUnit tests covering the same certificate/texture-fallback/uncovered-species/no-CEC/MLSN-regression cases server-side). Extended `tests/helpers/mlsn-engine-harness.js`'s shared `row()` helper to also parse `data-range-source`. Full suite: 1095/1095 Jest tests pass; PHPUnit Unit suite 46/46 pass (Feature suite still blocked by the pre-existing GH-261 SQLite migration gap, unrelated to this change).

**GH-305** (D07 item 6, "correction for generic numbers too" — explicit user decision, 2026-08-24) Directly following GH-304's labelling work, user asked point-blank: the Plan page still showed S at 10 kg/ha/yr (labelled "Generic") instead of 0, despite the Soil page classifying it HIGH — "it will be incorrect to recommend adding fertilizers if we have already high numbers." This is a genuine algorithm change (today: AA ceiling only fires on a certificate-backed range; after: also fires on the texture-only generic range when no certificate covers a nutrient) — explicitly confirmed by the user before implementing, per the project's algorithm-parity rule. Root cause: `computeProgram()`'s AA ceiling (GH-300/303) and its two sibling implementations feeding `NutritionRequirementEngine_Pure.compute()` (`nutrition-summary-integration.js`'s `_resolveAARanges()`, `word-export.js`'s `_aaRanges` IIFE — GH-299; `word-export-combined.js` inherits `word-export.js`'s resolution, no separate fix needed) were all **certificate-only**: when `HillLabsSampleTypes.getRangesPpm()` found no printed range for a nutrient (uncovered species/texture, or a covered code whose certificate simply doesn't print that nutrient — e.g. Sulphur on S277/S279), the ceiling silently never fired at all, regardless of how far above the generic "obviously too much" band the soil value actually was. Fixed all three call sites the same way: fall back to `AmmoniumAcetateMethodology.getSufficiencyRange(nutrient, soilTypeKey)` — the same generic sands/others SSOT `hub-tissue-v3.js`'s texture-only `aaRanges` and `SampleAnalysisController.php`'s `AA_RANGES` constant already use — whenever the certificate path doesn't cover a specific nutrient, and apply the identical `actual >= max → 0` rule against it. `rangeSource` (GH-304) intentionally stays `'texture-fallback'` for these — the ceiling now fires, but the "Generic" label still correctly applies, since it's still not a printed certificate value. `ammonium-acetate-methodology.js` was missing from `plan.blade.php` entirely (same gap class as GH-301/303 — confirmed safe to add standalone: its top-level `init()` guards on `.gaip-soil-methodology` not existing and returns early); already present on every other page that loads `nutrition-calendar.js`/`nutrition-summary-integration.js`/`word-export.js` (`hub.blade.php` + 3 `reports/*.blade.php` views), so no other script-tag gaps existed. Deliberately did **not** touch `hub-tissue-v3.js`'s AA branch or `soil-nutrition-analysis.js`'s `renderAnnualRequirements()` — confirmed both already apply the ceiling using whichever range (certificate or generic) ended up in `ranges[nutrient]`/`nObj.rangeMax`, since that code was never certificate-gated to begin with; this fix brings the other three implementations up to the same standard, not a new behaviour for the Soil page. Added `tests/gh305-aa-generic-range-ceiling.test.js` (7 tests, `nutrition-calendar.js`) and `tests/gh305-aa-generic-range-ceiling-consumers.test.js` (5 tests, `nutrition-summary-integration.js` end-to-end via `vm` + `word-export.js` structural pins) — both cover the certificate-first/generic-fallback order, the S277+Sulphur live case actually zeroing now, MLSN never touching `AmmoniumAcetateMethodology`, and graceful degradation when neither SSOT is loaded. Widened `tests/gh299-aa-ceiling-consumers.test.js`'s fixed-size extraction window (900→1900 chars) to keep matching `word-export.js`'s now-longer `_aaRanges` IIFE. Full suite: 1115/1115 Jest tests pass. Verified Blade syntax via `php artisan view:cache`/`view:clear`.

**GH-306** User asked what the Plan page's "Nutrient Delivery Summary" table meant, then noticed P and K both showed **Required=0, Delivered=0, Balance=+0.0, Status=Deficit** — a genuinely nonsensical combination (zero balance can't be a deficit) surfaced directly by GH-305: before that fix, AA's Required could never be exactly 0, so this edge case never occurred. Root cause, `nutrition-prebble-integration.js`/`nutrition-au-fertiliser-integration.js` (byte-identical blocks) and `nutrition-uk-fertiliser-integration.js` (same bug, different display format): `pct = required > 0 ? Math.round((delivered / required) * 100) : 0` forced `required === 0` straight to `pct = 0`, and the classifier (`pct >= 90 ? 'sufficient' : pct >= 70 ? 'marginal' : 'deficit'`) has no floor case for "nothing was required at all" — `0 < 70` always lands in the worst bucket regardless of `delivered`. Confirmed this bug pre-dates the AA ceiling work entirely (reachable for any MLSN/SLAN nutrient already at/above its target too) but was effectively invisible until AA's Required could legitimately be exactly 0. `nutrition-nz-fertiliser-integration.js` needed no separate fix — it delegates this table's rendering to `nutrition-prebble-integration.js`'s `buildRecommendationsHTML()` rather than duplicating it. Fixed all three files: `required === 0` is now a distinct branch, bypassing the percentage calculation entirely — `statusClass: 'sufficient'` / `statusLabel: 'Met'` in the NZ/AU modules, `uk-fert-positive` / `"✓ Met"` in the UK module (matching its icon+% display convention) — regardless of `delivered`, since a genuine 0-required/0-delivered state is fully satisfied, not a shortfall. The `required > 0` percentage-based branch is completely unchanged in all three. Added `tests/gh306-nutrient-delivery-summary-zero-required.test.js` (9 structural tests: the new branch exists and sets the correct non-deficit status in each of the 3 files, the old unconditional `pct = required > 0 ? ... : 0` pattern is gone, the `required > 0` percentage logic is byte-unchanged). Full suite: 1124/1124 Jest tests pass.

**GH-307** User asked to explain how "Delivered (kg/ha)" for N (46.2 kg/ha, against 200 required) is calculated, then asked to investigate why it was so low — a tangential finding surfaced while explaining the Nutrient Delivery Summary table (GH-306), unrelated to AA methodology entirely. Traced to `prebbles-products.js`: `context.surfaceType` on the live site was `'soccer'` (one of the 4 specific values `site-settings-panel.js`'s Sports sub-category grid sets — Soccer/AFL/Rugby Union/Rugby League — not the parent `'sports'` category), but `normalizeSurfaceType()`'s alias table had no entry for any of the 4 (unlike `'sports_field'`/`'sportsfield'`, which were already mapped to `'sports'`), so `'soccer'` passed through unchanged. Every granular product in `PrebbleProducts.granular` tags itself with the umbrella `'sports'` category in its `suitableFor` list, never a specific sub-type — confirmed by grepping the entire product catalog — so `filterBySurface()` matched **zero** granular products for `'soccer'`, every single month, confirmed live via `"[PrebbleRecommender] NO granular products match surface: soccer"` firing for all 12 months. Liquid-product fallback only fires in the winter (GP<0.3) branch or for greens (`isGreens && N>2`) — neither applies to a soccer field for most of the year — so most months got no N product recommendation at all, explaining the 46.2/200 kg/ha shortfall. Fixed by adding `'soccer'`, `'afl'`, `'rugby_union'`, `'rugby_league'`, and the generic `'rugby'` to `normalizeSurfaceType()`'s alias table, mapping all to `'sports'` — same pattern already established for `'sports_field'`/`'sportsfield'`. No product-catalog changes needed; the `'sports'` category itself was always correctly populated. Added `tests/gh307-prebble-surface-type-aliases.test.js` (14 tests: all 4 new aliases plus case-insensitivity, regression pins for pre-existing aliases/canonical passthrough/unknown-value passthrough, `filterBySurface()` now returning a non-empty granular pool for all 4 surfaces, and an end-to-end `getMonthlyRecommendation()` call proving a granular product is now actually selected for a Soccer field). Full suite: 1138/1138 Jest tests pass.

**GH-308** (D07 follow-up) User asked to verify the "Monthly Nutrient Program (kg/ha)" three-tier model (high→0, sufficient→removal only, low→removal+lift correction over years-to-correct) actually works for AA methodology. Traced `nutrition-calendar.js`'s `computeProgram()` end to end and found the below-floor deficit/lift correction (STEP 4, `calculateDeficit()`/`getThresholds()`) and the above-ceiling zeroing (GH-300/305, formerly a separate block after STEP 5) resolved their ranges from two different sources: the floor came from the old `CONFIG.aaThresholds` texture-only single-value table (sands/others), the ceiling from `HillLabsSampleTypes.deriveCode()`/`getRangesPpm()` (certificate-first, generic fallback). The two tables only agreed in the generic-fallback case — they happen to hold identical numbers to `AmmoniumAcetateMethodology`'s medium-range floor — but disagreed substantially for certificate-covered species (S277/S279/S78/S81), worst case Mg: the S277/S279 certificate range is ~37-85ppm, but the old generic floor used for the deficit calc was 100ppm (sands) / 140ppm (others), so a certificate-"Sufficient" Mg reading anywhere in the real 37-85ppm range still got an unwanted lift correction here, while the Soil page (`mlsnEngine()`, GH-260) correctly showed no correction needed for the exact same sample — a fresh cross-surface disagreement of the same species as the original D07, this time between two blocks of the same function. Fixed by resolving each nutrient's AA range (certificate-first via `deriveCode()`/`getRangesPpm()`, generic fallback via `AmmoniumAcetateMethodology.getSufficiencyRange()`, same GH-305 rule) ONCE at the top of STEP 4, then using `.min` for the floor (deficit/lift, replacing `calculateDeficit()`'s call to the old `CONFIG.aaThresholds` table for AA specifically) and `.max` for the ceiling (STEP 5's zeroing, unchanged logic, now reading the same resolved object instead of re-resolving it) — floor and ceiling can no longer disagree. Non-AA methodologies (MLSN/SLAN) untouched, still call `calculateDeficit()`/`getThresholds()` exactly as before. Kept all `[GH302-DEBUG]`/pending-removal console.log instrumentation in place per the standing GH-302 instruction (not yet asked to be removed) — added the new range-resolution logging under a `[GH308-DEBUG]` tag rather than touching the existing lines. Added `tests/gh308-aa-floor-ceiling-same-range.test.js` (5 tests: a mid-range certificate-"Sufficient" Mg reading now gives the identical removal-only total as a reading exactly at the floor — the direct regression pin for the bug — a below-floor reading still gets a lift correction, an above-ceiling reading is still zeroed, uncovered species/texture keeps today's generic-fallback behaviour, MLSN never touches `deriveCode()`). Full suite: 1143/1143 Jest tests pass.

**GH-309** (D07 follow-up) Following the GH-308 discussion, user asked whether the AA three-tier model (including lift) is actually what the old hub computed, and whether the Hoxton audit document itself requires lift as part of D07's fix. Re-read the full audit document: its own numbered regression-fixture assertions (18-20) require the ceiling (K/P/S return 0 above the sufficiency ceiling) and require UI/export parity ("UI and export return identical annual N, P and K requirements for the same site") — neither assertion independently mandates lift; the three-tier text quoting lift (line 331) is quoted as evidence of the historically-correct E2 mechanism, not a separate numbered requirement. Checked old-hub parity directly via `git log`/`git show` instead: `nutrition-calendar.js` and `nutrition-summary-integration.js` are both present since this repo's initial commit (179d283, i.e. what the old hub/WordPress plugin actually shipped); `nutrition-requirement-engine.js` is not (first appears at b35fix302, a SaaS-era extraction). `git show 179d283:assets/nutrition-calendar.js` confirms its `getThresholds()`/`calculateDeficit()` already had the AA branch with the same below-floor lift formula (`deficit / yearsToCorrect`, no ceiling at all) at the very first commit — this is the one true old-hub AA algorithm, and it includes lift. `git show 179d283:assets/nutrition-summary-integration.js` confirms that file's `calculateNutrientRequirement()` had no methodology dispatch at all in the old hub — it always ran MLSN math regardless of the site's real methodology; AA-awareness there only exists after the b35fix302 extraction into `nutrition-requirement-engine.js`, whose GH-299 comment ("AA-extractant thresholds aren't calibrated to MLSN values, so deficit correction can't be derived") was therefore a fresh SaaS-era decision, not inherited old-hub behaviour, and it left this engine disagreeing with `nutrition-calendar.js` (GH-308) on the same site/nutrient — a direct miss against the audit's own assertion 20. User confirmed adding lift. Added a below-floor branch to `nutrition-requirement-engine.js`'s `AMMONIUM_ACETATE` branch (`calculateNutrientRequirement()`, ~line 332), same shape as the file's existing MLSN/SLAN below-floor branches: when `config.aaRange` is present and `currentLevel < aaRange.min`, `correctionRequired = (aaRange.min - currentLevel) / yearsToCorrect`, `annualRequirement = removal + correctionRequired`, `status: 'Low'` — floor is inclusive (`currentLevel === aaRange.min` stays Adequate, matching the SLAN branch's inclusive-floor convention). No unit-conversion factor (bulk density/soil depth) applied, matching this file's own MLSN/SLAN branches, which also treat ppm-deficit/years as kg/ha/yr directly — deliberately not copying `nutrition-calendar.js`'s `× bulkDensity × soilDepth × 0.1` conversion, since that would make this engine internally inconsistent with its own MLSN/SLAN math (a separate, pre-existing unit-convention difference between the two files, noted but out of scope here). Ceiling behaviour (GH-299) and the graceful-degradation path (no `config.aaRange` -> unconditional pure removal) both unchanged. All 3 callers (`nutrition-summary-integration.js`'s `_resolveAARanges()`, `word-export.js`'s `_aaRanges` IIFE, `word-export-combined.js` inheriting the latter) already built `{min, max}` range objects — `.min` was already present, just unused before this fix — so no caller-side changes were needed; the new branch is live end-to-end immediately. Added `tests/gh309-aa-lift-nutrition-requirement-engine.test.js` (9 tests: below-floor lift fires with the correct per-nutrient `yearsToCorrect`, at-floor stays Adequate, mid-range unaffected, a second nutrient (K) confirms it's not a Mg-only carve-out, graceful degradation when `config.aaRange` is absent, `calculateAllRequirements()`/`compute()` thread the lift through per-nutrient and end-to-end, MLSN/SLAN branches byte-identical regression pin). Full suite: 1152/1152 Jest tests pass.

**GH-310** Following the GH-306 discussion (Nutrient Delivery Summary table showing K as Required=0/Delivered=116.9/Status="Met"), user asked how this worked in the old hub. Checked via `git show 179d283` (the initial commit, i.e. what the old hub/WordPress plugin actually shipped) against `nutrition-prebble-integration.js`: the old hub rendered the raw computed percentage next to a checkmark/warning/cross icon (`✓ 117%`, `⚠ 75%`, `✗ 40%`) rather than a fixed text label, and had the exact same `required === 0` bug GH-306 later fixed (`pct = required > 0 ? ... : 0` forced required=0 straight to `pct=0` → "✗ 0%", a nonsensical "deficit" reading on a fully-satisfied nutrient) — confirming GH-306 fixed a genuine pre-existing old-hub bug, not a legacy-parity deviation. Separately, the old hub's raw-percentage display had a real property the current text-label version (GH-306) lost: over-delivery magnitude was visible as a number (e.g. "✓ 2338%"), whereas the new text labels ("On Track") read identically whether delivered is 91% or 2000% of required. User asked to restore that visibility without reintroducing the old emoji icons (project rule: no emoji, text/SVG only). Fixed by appending the percentage to the status label for the `required > 0` branch in `nutrition-prebble-integration.js` (NZ) and `nutrition-au-fertiliser-integration.js` (AU, byte-identical twin) — `'On Track'` → `` `On Track (${pct}%)` ``, same for Monitor/Deficit. Not applied to the `required === 0` ("Met") branch — delivered/required is undefined there, no legacy percentage to restore (the old hub's "0%" for that case was itself the GH-306 bug, not a real number worth reviving). `nutrition-uk-fertiliser-integration.js` already displays icon+percentage (`✓ 117%` format, never rewritten to a text-only label) and needed no change. Widened `tests/gh306-nutrient-delivery-summary-zero-required.test.js`'s NZ/AU fixed-size extraction windows (1600→2700 chars) to keep matching the now-longer source blocks, same maintenance pattern as GH-305's word-export.js window widening. Added `tests/gh310-nutrient-status-percentage-label.test.js` (5 tests: percentage appended for NZ and AU's required>0 branch, Met branch unchanged in both, UK module's pre-existing icon+% format confirmed untouched). Full suite: 1157/1157 Jest tests pass.

**GH-311** Following the GH-306/310 discussion, user pointed out that "Met" (required=0) still doesn't say whether the amount actually delivered — a side-effect of product selection for N, since blended NPK products can carry P/K even when neither is required — pushes an already-sufficient/high soil level further past its ceiling (live example: K soil 199ppm against a 195.5ppm S277 ceiling, 116.9 kg/ha delivered anyway, table just said "Met"). Confirmed with the user this genuinely didn't exist in the old hub either (checked directly, no code to preserve) before designing a fix, then walked through the exact formula together before implementing: convert current soil ppm to a kg/ha-equivalent reserve using the same `bulkDensity × soilDepth × 0.1` factor `computeProgram()`'s deficit/lift math already uses, and define `balance = current_kgha + delivered − required` (required drops out at 0) as the projected total nutrient pool remaining after the season, compared against the ceiling converted to the same unit. Worked K example confirmed with the user before coding: `current = 199×1.4×10×0.1 = 278.6`, `ceiling = 195.5×1.4×10×0.1 = 273.7`, `balance = 278.6+116.9 = 395.5 > 273.7` → over ceiling by `395.5/273.7 ≈ 145%`. Scoped to the `required === 0` branch only — `required > 0` rows are untouched, since the existing deficit/lift math there already accounts for soil state via the floor-to-current gap; re-adding `current` there would double-count. Implementation: `nutrition-calendar.js`'s `computeProgram()` return object now includes `soil.bulkDensity`, `soil.soilDepth`, and `annual_totals_range` (the resolved `{min,max}` per-nutrient object STEP 4/GH-308 already builds for the floor/ceiling, previously computed but never exposed to callers). `nutrition-prebble-integration.js` (NZ) and `nutrition-au-fertiliser-integration.js` (AU, byte-identical twin) carry `program.soil`/`program.annual_totals_range` through from `calendarData` (same pattern the AU file already used for `deficits`), added a "Current (kg/ha)" table column (blank "—" for N, which has no soil-ppm/reserve concept in this codebase), and replaced the bare `required===0` → `'Met'` branch with the excess check: when a resolved ceiling and soil data are available and `balance > ceiling_kgha`, label `` `Excess (${pct}%)` `` with `statusClass: 'deficit'` (reuses the existing red badge styling — no new CSS needed); otherwise unchanged `'Met'`. Graceful degradation to plain `'Met'` (no crash, no false positive) when soil/range data isn't available (e.g. the AU module's legacy fallback path, `generateProgramLegacy()`, which wasn't wired to carry these fields through — an acceptable degradation, not a bug, since that path is already labelled deprecated). Widened `tests/gh306-...test.js` and `tests/gh310-...test.js`'s NZ/AU extraction windows again (2700→5600 NZ, 2700→4500 AU). Added `tests/gh311-nutrient-excess-delivery-check.test.js` (13 tests: `computeProgram()` exposes the new fields, a standalone reimplementation of the exact formula pinned against the confirmed K worked example — 278.6/273.7/395.5/145% — plus a not-excess case, both integration files' wiring/header/branch structure, required>0 branches confirmed untouched in both). Full suite: 1170/1170 Jest tests pass.

**GH-311 follow-up** User reproduced live and reported "Current is empty". Added `[GH311-DEBUG]` instrumentation at the `canCompute` check in both `nutrition-prebble-integration.js` and `nutrition-au-fertiliser-integration.js` (per the standing GH-302 "don't remove logs until confirmed" convention) and asked the user to reproduce — the log showed **two** separate `generateAndRender` invocations for the same `gaip:nutrition-calendar-generated` event, both logged from `nutrition-prebble-integration.js:772` (same function, two call sites): the first (triggered from `nutrition-prebble-integration.js`'s own listener) showed `canCompute: true` with real range/ppm data; the second (triggered from `nutrition-nz-fertiliser-integration.js`'s listener) showed `range: undefined, currentPpm: undefined, canCompute: undefined`. Root cause: `nutrition-nz-fertiliser-integration.js`'s `generateAndRender()` (line 236) is a **second, independent** caller of `PrebbleRecommender.generateProgram(calendarData, context)` (line 320) — and, per its own comment ("this is the distributor-aware program the user actually sees on screen"), it's the one that actually renders (calls `NutritionPrebbleIntegration.buildRecommendationsHTML(program)` directly and hides the standalone Prebble panel) whenever a distributor is selected, which is the normal case. GH-311's `program.soil`/`program.annual_totals_range` carry-through was only added to `nutrition-prebble-integration.js`'s own `generateAndRender` — this second call site never got it, so the "Current" column silently degraded to "—" even with real soil/range data available. Fixed by adding the identical two-line carry-through to `nutrition-nz-fertiliser-integration.js`'s `generateAndRender`, right before its `program.error` check and ahead of the `buildRecommendationsHTML()` call. `[GH311-DEBUG]` logs left in place, not yet confirmed by the user live. Added 2 tests to `tests/gh311-nutrient-excess-delivery-check.test.js` (carry-through present, and confirmed to run before the render call — 15 tests total in that file now). Full suite: 1172/1172 Jest tests pass.

**GH-312** User confirmed GH-311 worked live, then raised a real design problem: the Balance column used `Current + Delivered` for `required===0` rows but `Delivered − Required` for `required>0` rows — two different formulas presented as one "Balance" column, and the `required>0` percentage thresholds (90%/70%) had no scientific source anywhere in the code. Traced the actual issue: `Required = Removal + Lift`, and `Lift = (floor − Current) / yearsToCorrect` — so `Current` is already baked into `Required` for any below-floor nutrient; using `Current + Delivered − Required` there would double-count `Current`. The physically correct subtraction is `Removal` alone (research-backed plant uptake, true regardless of floor/ceiling/policy status). Before implementing, researched whether this "current + delivered − removal, compared to a sufficiency range" model has a real source rather than being invented: found Micah Woods (2013), *"A Method for Estimating Turfgrass Nutrient Requirements"* (asianturfgrass.com) — already the source behind MLSN elsewhere in this codebase — whose mass-balance equation `F = target + Harvest − Soiltest` (fertiliser needed to reach a target) is algebraically the same model, inverted here to predict the season-end soil level (`projected = Current + Delivered − Removal`; confirmed by hand that delivering exactly Woods' `F` makes `projected` land exactly on the target). The two-sided floor/ceiling range (vs. Woods' single MLSN guideline) traces to Carrow et al. (2004) SLAN, already cited in `nutrition-requirement-engine.js`. Woods' ppm↔mass conversion (`1 g/m² over 10cm → 6.7ppm`, sand rootzone) cross-checked against this codebase's own `ppm × bulkDensity × soilDepth × 0.1` formula — both give ~1.5 kg/ha per ppm at Woods' implied bulk density, confirming the existing conversion is the general form of his published constant, not a separate invention. User confirmed the sourcing and the design, then confirmed unifying Status too (dropping the uncited 90%/70% bands as the primary rule, one `Low`/`Met`/`Excess` check for every row) rather than keeping two different mechanisms in one table.

Implementation: `nutrition-calendar.js`'s `computeProgram()` now also returns `annual_removal` (`adjustedRemoval`) and `annual_lift` (`annualCorrection`) — both already computed internally, previously only exposed as their sum (`annual_totals`/"Required"). `nutrition-prebble-integration.js` (NZ), `nutrition-au-fertiliser-integration.js` (AU), and `nutrition-nz-fertiliser-integration.js` (the second `generateAndRender` call site GH-311's follow-up already found) all carry `annual_removal`/`annual_lift` through from `calendarData`, same pattern as the existing `soil`/`annual_totals_range` carry-through. Added "Removal (kg/ha)" and "Lift (kg/ha)" table columns (both NZ and AU) between Current and Required, so the three previously-conflated numbers are each visible on their own. Replaced the ad-hoc required===0-branch/required>0-branch status logic with a `classifyBalance(nutrient, required, delivered)` helper: when Current/Removal/range data is available, `Balance = Current + Delivered − Removal`, classified as `Low` (below floor), `Met` (within range), or `Excess (${pct}%)` (above ceiling, pct relative to ceiling) — one rule, no branch-specific special-casing. Falls back to the exact pre-GH-312 behaviour (`required===0` → `'Met'`; `required>0` → 90%/70% pct bands, GH-306/310) when Current/Removal/range aren't available — MLSN/SLAN sites (this engine has no ceiling concept for them at all) and uncovered AA species/texture, same graceful-degradation philosophy as the rest of this D07 chain. Also added, per user request: an "i" info-icon on the table title (`Nutrient Delivery Summary`), reusing the existing `.db-info-icon`/`GAIP_GLOSSARY`/`#db-info-popover` mechanism already active on the Plan page (`dashboard-init.js`'s `initInfoPopovers()`, the same component `soil-nutrition-analysis.js` uses) — no new popover infrastructure — with a glossary entry explaining Current/Removal/Lift/Required/Delivered/Balance/Status and citing Woods (2013) and the Hill Labs certificate source for the range. Registered identically in both NZ and AU files (`Object.assign` merge is idempotent since both always load together). Removed the `[GH311-DEBUG]` console.log instrumentation — it targeted code inside the old required===0 branch that this change replaced outright, so it no longer applies to the current code shape; the question it was diagnosing (was Current populating correctly) was already answered live before this rewrite. Rewrote `tests/gh306-...test.js` and `tests/gh310-...test.js` to retarget their extraction windows at the new `classifyBalance()` function (the required===0/pct-based logic they pin now lives in its fallback branch, not inline in the row-map callback) — same assertions, same behaviour, just relocated. Updated `tests/gh311-...test.js` to drop its now-superseded standalone reimplementation of the old Balance formula (`Current + Delivered`, no Removal) and keep only the data-plumbing checks that are still accurate. Added `tests/gh312-unified-nutrient-balance-status.test.js` (17 tests: `computeProgram()` exposes `annual_removal`/`annual_lift`; the worked K example across all three tiers — Low/Met/Excess — with real `Removal=110`; a standalone Woods-consistency check confirming delivering exactly his `F` lands the projected balance exactly on the floor; wiring/header/glossary/formula structural pins for NZ and AU; the `nutrition-nz-fertiliser-integration.js` carry-through). Full suite: 1182/1182 Jest tests pass.

**GH-313** User asked whether the table should also show what Balance is actually compared against, since "Excess (104%)"/"Low (57%)" implied a floor/ceiling without ever printing it. Recommended a single compact "Range (kg/ha)" column (`floor–ceiling`, e.g. "109.5–273.7") rather than two separate floor/ceiling columns, to avoid growing the table past its already-8-column width; user confirmed. Placed between Balance and Status, same kg/ha unit as the rest of the table for internal consistency (not ppm, which would require the reader to context-switch units mid-row). `classifyBalance()`'s canCompute branch now also returns `rangeDisplay` (`` `${Math.round(floorKgHa*10)/10}–${Math.round(ceilingKgHa*10)/10}` ``); the fallback branch (MLSN/SLAN, uncovered AA, or N) returns `rangeDisplay: '—'`, added to both of its return statements in each file. Row template destructures and renders it in a new cell. Updated the `GAIP_GLOSSARY` entry (both files) with a "Range" paragraph. Added `tests/gh313-nutrient-range-column.test.js` (9 tests: standalone formula reimplementation against the K worked example, header/formula/graceful-degradation/row-template structural pins for NZ and AU). Full suite: 1191/1191 Jest tests pass.

**GH-314** User asked why the table showed both "On Track" (N row, pct-based fallback) and "Met" (P/K rows, new GH-312 Balance model) for what's functionally the same "all good" state, and pointed out "On Track"/"Monitor"/"Deficit" is the pre-existing vocabulary (present before this D07 round of work) while "Met" is new — asked to unify on the existing words rather than keep two synonyms in one table. Corrected one factual premise in passing: the literal old hub (pre-SaaS) never had these text labels at all — GH-310 found it used icon+% (`✓ 117%`), not words — so "On Track" isn't literally old-hub-original either, but it does predate this session's GH-311/312/313 additions, which was the user's actual point. Renamed in `classifyBalance()` (both `nutrition-prebble-integration.js` and `nutrition-au-fertiliser-integration.js`, all 4 return sites — 2 in the pre-GH-312 fallback branch, 2 in the new Balance-range branch): `'Met'` → `'On Track'`, `` `Low (${pct}%)` `` → `` `Deficit (${pct}%)` ``. `Excess` is unchanged — no old-hub or fallback equivalent exists for "delivered too much onto an already-sufficient nutrient" (that's the entire point of GH-311, a genuinely new check), so it stays as its own word rather than being forced into a false synonym. `statusClass` CSS values (`sufficient`/`deficit`, driving badge colour) are untouched — only the display text changed. Updated the `GAIP_GLOSSARY` Status line to match, and noted explicitly that Excess has no fallback-branch counterpart.

**GH-314 follow-up** User reported the info-icon popover text didn't fit on the page (the original GH-312/313 body ran ~900 characters across 7 paragraphs, each with its own rationale/citation prose). Cut it to 7 short one-line bullets (~260 characters total) — kept only what a reader needs to interpret the columns (Current/Removal/Lift/Required/Balance/Range/Status, one clause each), dropped citations, worked examples, and "why" explanations from the popover itself (those remain in the code comments next to `classifyBalance()`, GH-312/313, for anyone reading source — the popover is a quick reference, not the full design rationale). Updated identically in both files. Full suite still 1191/1191 (no test asserted the old body text verbatim, only that the glossary key exists and the info-icon is wired to it).

**GH-315** User cross-checking the Soil page against the Nutrition Program page for the same AA site found what looked like a mismatch: Soil page showed "AA: 78.2-195.5 ppm" for K, while the new Nutrient Delivery Summary "Range" column (GH-313) showed "109.5–273.7" for the same K certificate range. Confirmed it's the same range in different units (78.2 × 1.4 = 109.48 ≈ 109.5, same `bulkDensity × soilDepth × 0.1` factor both tables already use) — not a bug. Considered adding a kg/ha↔ppm toggle control to the Nutrition Program table; user preferred a simpler fix instead: keep that table kg/ha-only as shipped, and add the kg/ha equivalent to the Soil page's existing ppm display so the two pages can be cross-checked without a mental unit conversion. Added to `soil-nutrition-analysis.js`'s `renderNutrientCards()`: computed `rangeMinKgHa`/`rangeMaxKgHa` from the already-scraped `n.rangeMin`/`n.rangeMax` using the `depthFactor` (`depth × bulkDensity × 0.1`) this function already computes for the "Soil reserve"/"Est. annual demand" Why-panel rows a few lines below — no new data needed. Appended `(109.5–273.7 kg/ha)` to both the main card's visible `AA: {ppm range} ppm` line and the "Why?" panel's "Sufficiency range" row (isAA-only at first). Added `tests/gh315-aa-range-kgha-soil-page.test.js` (using the same `vm`-execution harness as `gh286-...test.js` to call the real `renderNutrientCards()` rather than only pinning source patterns).

**GH-315 follow-up ("do for all")** Extended the same treatment to the `!isAA` branch's single-threshold line: `mlsnKgHa` was already computed a few lines above (feeds the existing "Soil reserve"/"Reserve above threshold" Why-panel rows) but was never used on the main `MLSN: {value} ppm` line itself — now it is: `MLSN: 37 ppm (51.8 kg/ha)`. No new data or conversion logic needed, purely wiring an already-computed value into one more place. Expanded `tests/gh315-aa-range-kgha-soil-page.test.js` to 6 tests (added: MLSN threshold now shows the kg/ha suffix; a null/non-numeric `mlsn` value renders `MLSN: — ppm` with no suffix and no crash). Full suite: 1197/1197 Jest tests pass.

**GH-316** "Annual Product Summary"'s own Balance row (a third, independent implementation on the same page as "Nutrient Delivery Summary") still used the naive `Delivered − Required` formula GH-311/312 already fixed elsewhere on the same page — P/K showed the full delivered amount in green as pure surplus even where it was actually Excess. Reused the `classifyBalance()` helper already declared in `buildRecommendationsHTML()` for N/P/K here too, in both `nutrition-prebble-integration.js` (NZ) and `nutrition-au-fertiliser-integration.js` (AU). Added `tests/gh316-annual-product-summary-balance.test.js`.

**GH-317** The Balance cell in "Nutrient Delivery Summary" showed green even when Status said "Excess" — its colour class used the raw sign (`diff >= 0`) instead of `statusClass`.
