Fixed in both files: colour now follows `statusClass === 'sufficient'`, same signal the Status badge already uses. Added `tests/gh317-balance-cell-color-matches-status.test.js`.

**GH-318** Rounded P to whole numbers in "Annual Product Summary" (product rows + tfoot: Total Delivered/Required/Balance), matching N/K's existing whole-number formatting in that table — was `Math.round(x*10)/10` (1dp), now `Math.round(x)`. The more detailed "Nutrient Delivery Summary" table keeps 1dp precision throughout, unaffected. Fixed in both `nutrition-prebble-integration.js` and `nutrition-au-fertiliser-integration.js`. Added `tests/gh318-annual-product-summary-p-rounding.test.js`. Full suite: 1207/1207 Jest tests pass.

**GH-319** ("do for all") The D07/AA ceiling work (GH-300 onward) never extended to MLSN/SLAN in `nutrition-calendar.js`'s `computeProgram()` — the below-floor deficit/lift and above-ceiling zeroing were gated to `isAAMethodology`, so MLSN/SLAN sites always got pure removal, never a ceiling, and the Nutrient Delivery Summary table's Current/Range columns always showed "—" for them (no resolved range to compute from). Removed the `isAAMethodology` gates from both the deficit-calc loop and the ceiling-zeroing block. Resolved a real floor/ceiling range for both: SLAN uses `GilbaClassificationConstants.SLAN_RANGES` (Carrow et al. 2004, GCM 72(1):194-198 — already used by `nutrition-requirement-engine.js` for the same purpose; this calendar previously only read the single-value floor derived from it). MLSN uses `MLSN_THRESHOLDS[nutrient] × 1.5` — Woods/Stowell/Gelernter (2016) publish a floor only, no published ceiling exists, so this reuses `nutrition-requirement-engine.js`'s own established hub-wide convention (`TARGET_MULTIPLIER = 1.5`) rather than inventing a new number. Consumer side (`classifyBalance()` in `nutrition-prebble-integration.js`/`nutrition-au-fertiliser-integration.js`) needed no changes — already written generically against `program.annual_totals_range`/`annual_removal`/`annual_lift` with no methodology check of its own. Verified end-to-end via a direct `buildRecommendationsHTML()` call for an MLSN site (K correctly shows Current=278.6, Range=51.8–77.7, Status="Excess (217%)"), not just at the `computeProgram()` level. Updated 3 outdated regression pins (`gh300`/`gh304`/`gh305`) that asserted the old "MLSN never gets a ceiling" behaviour. Added `tests/gh319-mlsn-slan-ceiling-nutrition-calendar.test.js` (9 tests). Full suite: 1217/1217 Jest tests pass.

**GH-320** The Soil page card's threshold/range line already showed a kg/ha equivalent alongside ppm (GH-315), but the card's main measured value (the large number at the top) did not, even though its kg/ha equivalent (`reserveKgHa`) was already computed for the hidden "Why?" panel's "Soil reserve" row. Appended the same kg/ha equivalent to the main visible value line in `soil-nutrition-analysis.js`, for both AA and MLSN/SLAN cards (not gated to `isAA`, since `reserveKgHa` is computed generically). Added `tests/gh320-soil-card-value-kgha.test.js` (3 tests). Full suite: 1220/1220 Jest tests pass.

**GH-321** On page reload/navigation, `NutritionCalendar.restoreFromPersisted()` restores the saved calendar program and calls `this.renderResults()`, but never dispatches `gaip:nutrition-calendar-generated` — so the regional recommendations panels, which only render on that live event, stayed empty after a reload unless the user clicked "Generate" again. `nutrition-nz-fertiliser-integration.js`'s `init()` already had a "late-render" catch-up block for this (checks `window.GilbaNutritionCalendar.program` directly and self-triggers `generateAndRender()` if the live event was missed); `nutrition-au-fertiliser-integration.js` and `nutrition-uk-fertiliser-integration.js` never had it. Reachable for any methodology — reproduced live on an AU/MLSN site and confirmed absent on an NZ/AA site, but the root cause was the region (missing block), not the methodology. Added the same block to both files, mirroring NZ's existing pattern exactly. Added `tests/gh321-au-uk-late-render-catchup.test.js` (3 tests, including a regression pin confirming NZ's original block is unchanged). Full suite: 1223/1223 Jest tests pass.

**GH-322** The Plan page's "Monthly N Need" KPI card always showed 0 — it read `soilN.annualDemand.n` (lowercase), but `calculateAnnualDemand()` (`mlsn-progressive-disclosure.js`) only ever sets `annualDemand.N` (uppercase); `.n` was always `undefined`. Even with the casing fixed, the card computed `annualDemand.N / 12` — a flat annual average — which would still disagree with the Monthly Nutrient Program table's real GP-weighted per-month value (e.g. a cool-season site's August is a low-GP winter month, far below annual/12). Sourced the card directly from the already-generated Nutrition Program's own monthly array instead (`window.GilbaNutritionCalendar.program.program.monthly[currentMonthIndex].N`) — the same data the table itself renders from, so the two can never disagree. Empty state now reads "Generate a Nutrition Program for N demand" when no program has been generated yet, instead of silently showing 0. Added `tests/gh322-monthly-n-need-kpi.test.js` (3 tests, via a `vm`-executed run of `plan-ui.js`'s `init()`). Full suite: 1226/1226 Jest tests pass.

**GH-322 follow-up** Even with the source fixed, the card still showed the "no programme" empty state when a program genuinely existed — `renderPlanHeader()` runs synchronously at `DOMContentLoaded`, but `window.GilbaNutritionCalendar.program` is populated asynchronously (either by `restoreFromPersisted()`, itself triggered later by `site-config-persistence.js`'s `gaip:site-config-applied` event, or by a live "Generate" click). The very first render usually ran before either had happened. Added listeners for both `gaip:nutrition-calendar-generated` (fires with `this.program` already set — no delay needed) and `gaip:site-config-applied` (200ms delay before re-rendering, matching the same cross-script ordering workaround already used in `nutrition-nz/au/uk-fertiliser-integration.js`'s "late-render catch-up", GH-321) that re-run `renderPlanHeader()` with freshly-read `GAIP_DASHBOARD_DATA`/`GAIP_SITE_CONFIG`. Expanded `tests/gh322-monthly-n-need-kpi.test.js` to 5 tests (added: re-render on each event, captured via a listener-recording sandbox). Full suite: 1228/1228 Jest tests pass.

**GH-322 follow-up 2** The `gaip:site-config-applied` listener from the previous follow-up never fired in practice — live `[GH322-DEBUG]` logs showed `restoreFromPersisted()` running successfully with no preceding `gaip:site-config-applied received` log at all. Root cause: `plan.blade.php` never loads `site-config-persistence.js`, so that event never exists on this page — `NutritionCalendar.init()` calls `restoreFromPersisted()` directly instead (see the comment at that call site), a page-specific path the previous fix didn't account for. Fixed at the source instead of patching every consumer's guesswork: `restoreFromPersisted()` now also dispatches `gaip:nutrition-calendar-generated` (the same event `generate()` always dispatched) once `this.program` is set, after the early-return guards (already has a program; nothing persisted) so it only fires on a genuine restore. Removed the now-dead `gaip:site-config-applied` listener and its 200ms-delay workaround from `plan-ui.js` — the single `gaip:nutrition-calendar-generated` listener now covers live-generate and restore, on every page, regardless of which internal path populated the program. `[GH322-DEBUG]` logs left in place, not yet confirmed live. Updated `tests/gh322-monthly-n-need-kpi.test.js` (replaced the now-invalid `gaip:site-config-applied` re-render test with one confirming that listener no longer exists) and added `tests/gh322b-restore-dispatches-generated-event.test.js` (4 tests: dispatch happens after `this.program` is set, same `detail` shape as `generate()`'s dispatch, early-return branches don't dispatch, `generate()`'s own dispatch unchanged). Full suite: 1232/1232 Jest tests pass.

**GH-323** The Soil page's "Annual Nutrient Requirements (MLSN/AA/SLAN)" and "Monthly N Distribution (GP-Weighted)" panels (`soil-nutrition-analysis.js`) duplicated the Nutrition Program page's Required/Monthly Nutrient Program figures, but with a cruder calculation disconnected from real soil status: `calcAnnualDemand()` computes a flat `effectiveN × per-nutrient ratio` estimate with no methodology parameter at all (identical output for AA/MLSN/SLAN given the same N/GP/turf inputs), sourced from a `localStorage` N-program snapshot and a single whole-year-average GP factor (clamped to a 0.3 floor) rather than the real per-month GP curve; real soil ppm only ever gates a badge colour and an all-or-nothing zero-out, never the displayed number itself. `renderMonthlyN()`'s fallback distributes that same flat annual figure by daily-GP pattern, independently of the real generated program's monthly array. Both produced numbers that could visibly disagree with the validated Nutrition Program page (GH-308–320) for the same site. Hidden from the Soil page's render pipeline (`renderAnnualRequirements()`/`renderMonthlyN()` no longer called from `bodyContent`) rather than deleted — both functions are still exercised directly by existing tests (gh277, gh279, gh280, gh299, gh304, and others). Full suite: 1232/1232 Jest tests pass.

**GH-324** Added an "i" info icon to the Soil page's "Correction Program" panel (`soil-nutrition-analysis.js`), matching the existing `db-info-icon`/`GAIP_GLOSSARY` pattern used elsewhere on this page (GH-279, GH-283). The new `sn-correction-program` glossary entry states the calculation (`deficit ppm = MLSN threshold − current level`, converted to kg/ha, divided by the correction product's nutrient % to get an application rate) and, more importantly, distinguishes this one-time "close the gap today" dose from the Nutrition Program page's Lift, which spreads the same deficit gradually across 2–3 years (`yearsToCorrect`) alongside the normal fertiliser schedule — the two panels can show different-looking numbers for the same deficit by design, not by error, and Correction Program doesn't account for Lift already scheduled, turf removal, or the product actually selected in the real programme. Added `tests/gh324-correction-program-info-icon.test.js` (2 tests). Full suite: 1234/1234 Jest tests pass.

**GH-325** Hid the "Lift (kg/ha)" column from the Nutrient Delivery Summary table (`nutrition-au-fertiliser-integration.js` and `nutrition-prebble-integration.js`) — removed the column header, its `<td>` cell, and the now-unused `liftMap`/`lift`/`liftDisplay` display-only variables. The underlying calculation is untouched: `Required` still includes Lift internally (`program.annual_lift` is still computed and carried through unchanged), only the standalone display column is gone. Updated `tests/gh312-unified-nutrient-balance-status.test.js`'s two column-presence assertions to expect Lift's absence instead of presence. Full suite: 1234/1234 Jest tests pass.

**GH-326** Also hid the "Removal (kg/ha)" column from the same Nutrient Delivery Summary table (same two files) — same reasoning as GH-325: Removal is already folded into the displayed `Required` figure, so a standalone column was redundant. Calculation untouched, only the `<th>`/`<td>` and the now-unused per-row `removal`/`removalDisplay` variables removed (`removalMap` itself stays — `classifyBalance()` still reads it for the Balance formula). Separately, fixed two real product-selection bugs surfaced by a live client report on an MLSN/AU site where N came out close to exact but P and K looked "way out" once fertiliser products were selected (P: Required=14, Delivered=0; K: Required=0, Delivered=70, 356% over ceiling) — both in `au-fertiliser-products.js`'s `generateAnnualProgram()`: (1) `soilPSufficient = annualTargets.P < 15` was an arbitrary, uncited cutoff on the annual Required figure — a genuine 45% P deficit whose Required (14) landed just under it got misclassified as "sufficient" and P products were penalized instead of recommended; changed to `annualTargets.P <= 0`, reusing nutrition-calendar.js's own already-validated convention (GH-300/305/319) that Required is exactly 0 only when soil is at/above the methodology's ceiling. (2) The granular-product "Autumn K boost for winter hardening" score (Christians et al. 2016) was unconditional on `kRequired` — unlike the sibling liquid-product scoring function, which already gated the same bonus on `kRequired > 0` — so a soil already far above its K ceiling could still receive the full autumn hardening bonus on a high-K granular product; added the same `kRequired > 0` gate the liquid path already had. Added `tests/gh326-p-k-fertiliser-selection-fix.test.js` (5 tests). Full suite: 1239/1239 Jest tests pass.

**GH-327** GH-326's P threshold fix alone wasn't enough — confirmed live that P stayed at Delivered=0 even with `soilPSufficient` corrected. Root cause: `selectNitrogenSource()` (`au-fertiliser-products.js`) never received P as a magnitude at all — only N and K were passed in (`{ N: netN, K: netK }`); P was scored purely as a penalty (`pPenalty`, driven by the boolean `soilPSufficient`) with no reward for matching a real deficit, unlike K's `kScore`, which actively rewards a good delivery-ratio match when `kRequired > 0`. Added `pRequired` (from a new `monthData.P`, passed at the granular call site as `month.P || 0` — P has no slow-release carry-over tracking like `activeN`/`activeK`, so nothing to net it against) and replaced the old penalty-only "P-conscious penalty" block with a `pScore` that mirrors `kScore`'s exact delivery-ratio-banded structure (100/70/40/30/10/-20 for the "needed" case, 30/0/-15/-30 for "not needed"), weighted the same as `kScore` (×0.35) in the final `totalScore`. Confirmed with the user beforehand that this scoring engine (all of `kScore`/`nScore`/`pPenalty`/`autumnKBonus`/etc.) is a hand-tuned heuristic accumulated over ~20 versions, not a peer-reviewed formula — only the underlying agronomic principles it encodes (e.g. Christians et al. 2016 for K/winter hardening) have real citations, not the specific point values — so this fix reuses `kScore`'s existing shape for consistency with the rest of the file, not because that shape is independently validated. The sibling liquid-product scoring function's own `pPenalty` is untouched (out of scope — same granular-only pattern as GH-326's K fix). Added `tests/gh327-p-delivery-accuracy-score.test.js` (7 tests). Full suite: 1246/1246 Jest tests pass.

**GH-328** Applied GH-327's fix to the sibling liquid/soluble scoring function `selectFoliarNitrogen()` too, so granular and liquid product selection use the same P-scoring algorithm — a live test on a second site (N Required=200/Delivered=203.7 On Track, but P Required=0/Delivered=66.9 Excess 245%, K Required=0/Delivered=116.9 Excess 104%) showed liquid top-ups were still contributing unpenalized-enough P/K on top of an already-fixed granular selection, since only granular had gotten the reward-based `pScore` in GH-327. `monthData.P` didn't need a call-site change here (unlike granular) — `selectFoliarNitrogen()`'s call site already spreads `{ ...month, N: remainingN, K: netK, gp }`, and `month.P` came through automatically. Replaced the old `pPenalty` block with a `pScore` mirroring this function's own `kScore` bands (its weights differ slightly from the granular function's — kScore/pScore both ×0.25 here vs ×0.35 there — kept consistent with each function's existing internal weighting rather than forcing the two functions to match each other's weights). Added `tests/gh328-liquid-p-delivery-accuracy-score.test.js` (6 tests); updated `tests/gh327-p-delivery-accuracy-score.test.js`'s regression check (previously asserted the liquid `pPenalty` was untouched; now asserts no `pPenalty` remains anywhere in the file). Full suite: 1252/1252 Jest tests pass.

**GH-329** GH-327/328's `pScore`/`kScore` gave P/K a proper reward-or-penalty score, but a live retest on the same site (product breakdown this time) showed the excess persisting: Ezyreno (QR), analysis 6-2.5-3.7 — a dilute 6% N product needing a large total application mass (2675 kg/ha over 9 applications) to hit N targets — kept winning 9 of 12 months over a concentrated, P/K-free alternative (Lo Biuret Urea 46-0-0) despite Required P/K = 0, because its excellent N-delivery-accuracy score reliably outweighed the P/K "not needed" penalty; even modest 2.5%/3.7% P/K content compounds into a large absolute delivery at that mass (P=67 kg/ha against a Required of 0 and a Range of only 28–42; K=99+18=117 kg/ha against a Range of 109.5–273.7). Discussed with the user: "P/K not needed" isn't a soft trade-off like release-type-vs-GP (where score genuinely helps balance competing, non-absolute criteria) — it's a hard constraint, the same class as the existing herbicide/N-content pre-filter, which already excludes entirely via `return` rather than via score. Moved it there: both `selectNitrogenSource()` (granular) and `selectFoliarNitrogen()` (liquid, for the GH-328 parity) now hard-exclude any candidate that would deliver more than 2 kg/ha effective-monthly of an unneeded nutrient (same "minimal" cutoff `pScore`/`kScore`'s own "not needed" bands already used), provided at least one "clean" (≤2 kg/ha) alternative remains viable for N delivery that month — falls back to keeping the P/K-containing candidates when no clean alternative exists, so a genuine N need is never left unmet. The `pScore`/`kScore` soft penalties stay in place as secondary tie-breakers among whatever survives the hard filter. Added `tests/gh329-hard-exclude-unneeded-p-k.test.js` (6 tests). Full suite: 1258/1258 Jest tests pass.

**GH-330** A live retest that should have exercised GH-329's fix showed identical over-delivery (Ezyreno 6-2.5-3.7 still winning most months, Required P/K = 0) — the retest was on an NZ site (`selectedDistributor: 'prebble'`), and `prebbles-products.js` is a completely separate, independently-written recommendation engine from `au-fertiliser-products.js`, with its own `selectNitrogenSource()`/`selectFoliarNitrogen()` — none of GH-326 through GH-329's AU-side fixes were ever active here. Confirmed both NZ integration files (`nutrition-prebble-integration.js` and `nutrition-nz-fertiliser-integration.js`, including its PGG Wrightson/Aitkens distributor-merged product pools) funnel through this same single `window.PrebbleRecommender`, so fixing it here covers every NZ distributor combination in one place — no separate engine exists per NZ distributor. This file's P defense was only the "starter product" filter (`pPct > 8 && pPct > nPct/2`), which excludes high-P starter blends but lets a low-%-P product like Ezyreno (2.5%) straight through; K's only defense was a token `ratioScore = 80 - kPct` penalty inside the N:K ratio score (e.g. -3.7 raw, weighted ×0.35 ≈ -1.3) — far too weak to outweigh a strong N-match/release/tech-efficiency score, and neither had a hard filter. Applied the same GH-329 pattern here: both `selectNitrogenSource()` (granular) and `selectFoliarNitrogen()` (liquid/soluble) now hard-exclude any candidate that would deliver more than 2 kg/ha (at the rate needed for N) of an unneeded nutrient, provided a clean alternative remains viable, falling back to keeping P/K-containing candidates when none exists. The pre-existing starter-product filter and P-supplementation path (`selectPhosphorusSource()`, separately gated on genuine P deficiency/establishment) are untouched. Checked `uk-fertiliser-products.js`/`nutrition-uk-fertiliser-integration.js` and `aitkens-fertiliser-products.js` — UK's `UkFertiliserRecommender` already has proper two-sided `kScore`/`pScore` reward-and-penalty scoring (ahead of where AU/NZ started), and Aitkens is product-catalog data only (no separate scoring engine, feeds into the same PrebbleRecommender) — no further action needed for either right now. Added `tests/gh330-prebble-hard-exclude-unneeded-p-k.test.js` (5 tests). Full suite: 1263/1263 Jest tests pass.

**GH-331** A live retest on the original "Burns" AU/MLSN golf_greens site — the one that originally surfaced the whole GH-326+ P/K investigation — still showed zero P delivered despite a genuine, confirmed deficit (Required P=14 kg/ha every month all year; both products actually selected, Sportsmaster WSF 20-0-0 and Ammonium Sulphate 21-0-0, carry no P at all). Different root cause from every prior GH-32x fix: AU had no mechanism at all to actively add P when needed — `selectNitrogenSource()`/`selectFoliarNitrogen()` only ever deliver P as an accidental byproduct of whichever product wins the N-delivery competition, and a genuine P-correction product (the catalog already has one — `SOL-MAP`, 12-22-0, `suitableFor` includes `golf_greens`) can never win that competition on N-delivery grounds — it's deliberately rate-capped low (`greensMaxRateKgHa: 15`) so it structurally can't supply enough N to compete, which is agronomically correct (MAP is meant for small point-corrections, not as a primary N carrier). `prebbles-products.js` (NZ) already had this solved with a dedicated `selectPhosphorusSource()` plus a "strategic month" annual-planning strategy: compute the full annual P requirement up front, and — if genuinely deficient — designate ONE agronomically sensible month (first spring month, hemisphere-aware, with GP ≥ 0.4) to begin applying P, rather than letting it dribble out (or not at all) as a byproduct of N-driven product choices. Ported that same approach to AU directly: a new `selectPhosphorusSource()` (same tiered P% preference as NZ's — dedicated ≥20% source preferred, then moderate 2–15%, then any with P; MAP preferred by name match — adapted to this file's product shape: `maxRateKgHa`/`greensMaxRateKgHa`/`maxRateLHa` fields directly on the product rather than a `rates` sub-object), plus a `pApplicationMonth` computed once before `generateAnnualProgram()`'s month loop (`annualTargets.P > 5` gate, same threshold NZ uses; `springMonths = hemisphere === 'south' ? [8,9,10] : [2,3,4]`, same season logic NZ uses). AU's gate is actually more robust than NZ's own here — it uses the already-upfront-computed `annualTargets.P` (a magnitude) rather than NZ's separately-computed boolean `context.pDeficient`, which has its own documented history of being fed incorrect soil data (see b35fix427 in `nutrition-prebble-integration.js`). From that designated month onward, every month checks whatever remains of the full annual P target (`annualTargets.P - delivered.P`, i.e. minus anything already landed as an N-product byproduct or a prior supplement) and applies another dose via `selectPhosphorusSource()` if more than 2 kg/ha is still owed — this repeats rather than firing once, because a single application is often rate-capped well short of the full annual amount (confirmed live on golf_greens: `SOL-MAP` capped at its 15 kg/ha greens limit delivers at most ~3.3 kg P per visit, so a 14 kg/ha deficit needs several visits, same spirit as a real spoon-feeding programme — a single "whole year in one visit" application physically cannot work when the safe per-visit rate is this restrictive). Confirmed live: P Delivered reached 14.1 against a Required of 14 across the resulting applications — the remaining "Deficit (93%)" status in that run was separately verified as the correct, expected multi-year Lift-correction behaviour (P `yearsToCorrect` = 2), not a bug. Also noticed and left in place: `selectGreensSpoonfeed()`, an already-written, generic-nutrient soluble-product picker in this same file, existed with zero call sites (dead code, apparently prepared for exactly this kind of use and never wired up) — didn't repurpose it since `selectPhosphorusSource()` already covers both granular and liquid/soluble pools directly, matching NZ's actual scope. Added `tests/gh331-au-p-supplementation.test.js` (8 tests). Full suite: 1271/1271 Jest tests pass.

**GH-333** Feedback on the Nutrient Delivery Summary table, both `nutrition-au-fertiliser-integration.js` and `nutrition-prebble-integration.js`: (1) Excess and Deficit shared one `statusClass` (`'deficit'`), so both painted the same alarming red — but Deficit is an intentionally gradual, multi-year correction (Lift spread over `yearsToCorrect`, GH-308/309), not an emergency, unlike Excess (soil already over the ceiling, nothing actively correcting it). Split Excess into its own `statusClass: 'excess'`; added `statusVisualClass(statusClass)` (sufficient→green, excess→red, everything else incl. deficit/fallback's marginal→amber) and routed every colour decision through it — badge CSS, diff-cell CSS, row backgrounds, and the Annual Product Summary tfoot Balance row/cells (previously all binary `=== 'sufficient'` checks). CSS: `.nutrient-status-deficit` now amber (was red, matching `.nutrient-status-marginal`), new `.nutrient-status-excess` red; added `.warning` variants alongside every existing `.positive`/`.negative` pair in both files (including the older duplicate style block in `nutrition-prebble-integration.js`'s `injectStyles()`). (2) Balance's leading "+" (e.g. "+207.0") read as "this much was added" when Balance = Current + Delivered − Removal is a projected absolute reserve, not a delivery delta — for K with Delivered=0, the entire "+207" came from Current alone. Dropped the artificial `+` prefix on positive values (genuine shortfalls still show their natural `-`). (3) The original `(${pct}%)` suffix on Excess/Deficit was Balance-as-%-of-ceiling/floor (e.g. "266%"), which reads as if the whole reserve is 2.66x normal. After two intermediate iterations (dropping the percentage entirely, then showing the raw kg/ha overage/shortfall), landed on what the user actually wanted: the percentage of *only the overage/shortfall itself* relative to the ceiling/floor — "Excess (+166%)" means the excess amount is 166% of the ceiling, not that the total Balance is; "Deficit (-13%)" means the shortfall is 13% of the floor. (4) Brought the Removal column back (hidden in GH-326 as "redundant with Required") — Required blends Removal + Lift together, so Balance (which subtracts Removal alone) couldn't be verified from the table without it.

**GH-333 follow-up** Extended the same "On Track" question to N: N always falls through `classifyBalance()`'s fallback branch (no sufficiency range exists for N — it's an annual application target, not a soil reserve), which still showed a raw completion ratio, e.g. "On Track (100%)"/"On Track (102%)", inconsistent with the range-based branch's new delta-from-edge framing. Tried two designs in sequence, both confirmed live with the user: first added a delta-from-target number to every tier including On Track (`deltaPct = pct - 100`, e.g. "On Track (+2%)") to match the range-based branch's initial "show distance from nearest edge for On Track too" version — then the user reconsidered both: the percentage only actually matters once something is Deficit/Monitor/Excess, so On Track (both the range-based branch and N's fallback) went back to a plain label with no number, while Monitor/Deficit in the fallback branch keep the delta-from-target percentage (`(pct >= 70 ? 'Monitor' : 'Deficit') + ' (${deltaPct}%)'`). Also moved the Range column to sit immediately before Balance (previously Balance, Range, Status — now Range, Balance, Status), so the target window and the actual result read next to each other, right before the verdict. Confirmed the Excess branch is genuinely computed from the upper bound (`ceilingKgHa = range.max * unit`), not the floor or Current alone. Rewrote `tests/gh333-excess-deficit-color-and-removal.test.js` (20 tests, added column-order and ceiling-basis regression checks) and updated `tests/gh310-nutrient-status-percentage-label.test.js`, `tests/gh306-nutrient-delivery-summary-zero-required.test.js` for the final On-Track-is-plain / Monitor-Deficit-show-delta shape. Full suite: 1291/1291 Jest tests pass.

**GH-334** Asked to highlight the "Delivered" column in the Nutrient Delivery Summary table so it stands out among the surrounding calculated columns — it's the one figure a user needs to act on at a glance. First tried a light background tint on both header and cells; the user clarified they just wanted bold numbers with no colour, matching how the Balance column already reads (bold + status colour via `.nutrient-diff`, no background tint) — dropped the header/background styling and kept only `font-weight: 700` on the Delivered cell, in both `nutrition-au-fertiliser-integration.js` and `nutrition-prebble-integration.js`. Added `tests/gh334-delivered-column-bold.test.js` (6 tests). Full suite: 1297/1297 Jest tests pass.

**GH-335** Data-check request against `files/fixes/26-08-25-distr-check/Prebbles_Turf_World_Fertiliser_Range.xlsx` (a reconciled Prebbles Turf World catalogue, elemental analysis cross-matched to ICL/LebanonTurf/Growth Products manufacturer labels) turned up 44 listed products against `prebbles-products.js`'s existing 42-product catalog; cross-referencing by NPK/name found 30 already covered and 14 genuine gaps. Added 13 of the 14 to `prebbles-products.js`'s `granular`/`liquid` arrays: the entire ICL Sierrablen/Sierraform GT controlled-release line (3 products — absent even though the file's "ICL" brand entries were all Sportsmaster WSF solubles, a different product family), two more Country Club MD Greens grades (12-0-20, 22-0-13), plain D.A.P. 18-20-0, Sports Fert NO3 +TE, and a new soil-amendment/biostimulant group with no published nutrient analysis (EzySpread Lime/Dolomite/Fairway Gypsum/Greens Gypsum, Biopower Seaweed Flakes, Vital Polykelp) — these use `analysis: {N:0,P:0,K:0}` and omit `maxRateKgHa`/`releaseWeeks` rather than inventing figures, letting the existing `|| 350`/`|| 4` fallbacks in the recommendation engine apply (same pattern already used for `KIESERITE`/`BIOSEAW`), with `notes` stating the rate is unpublished and to confirm before quoting. Where a rate/SGN wasn't published but a same-brand sibling grade already in the catalog gave a defensible basis (e.g. CC MD Greens 12-0-20/22-0-13 borrowing `CCMDG18`'s 100–200 kg/ha; D.A.P. and Sports Fert NO3 borrowing `MAPGRAN`/`CCLEB16`'s treatment), used that sibling's figure with a comment flagging it as inferred, not published for that exact SKU. Left out: Sierrablen Turf Starter 16-11-10 (ICL-02 in the source) — its analysis is elementally identical to the already-cataloged `STARPR` "Proscape Starter" 16-11-10 and the source workbook itself flags the ICL/LebanonTurf attribution as unconfirmed, so adding it as a second entry would risk a silent duplicate rather than a real gap; and Reno Boost (Prebbles house brand) — zero published data of any kind (no NPK even in the product name), nothing to add without inventing a composition. Both need the NZ price schedule from Prebbles (0800 28 00 28) to resolve, per the source workbook's own "Gaps & flags" sheet. Full suite: 1297/1297 Jest tests pass.

**GH-336** Live report on a Canberra golf_greens/MLSN site: N Required=200, Delivered=159.3 (Balance -40.7, "Monitor (-20%)") — Required/Delivered were both individually plausible from GH-326-331's earlier fixes, so this needed fresh investigation. Added 5 `[GH336-DEBUG]` console logs to `au-fertiliser-products.js`'s `generateAnnualProgram()` month loop (month start: netN/activeN/gp; granular decision: picked product, would-deliver N/K, accepted y/n; liquid gate: remainingN, will-attempt y/n; liquid decision: picked product, asked-to-cover, would-deliver; month end: running delivered.N/K totals) — kept in place pending confirmation, same as prior `GH3xx-DEBUG` tags. Full suite: 1297/1297 Jest tests pass (logging only, no behaviour change).

**GH-337** The GH-336-DEBUG logs, reconciled month-by-month, showed each month's own requirement nominally "met" (net surplus +10.1 across the year by that accounting) — yet annual Delivered still landed at 159.3, short of 200. The user asked the question that found the real bug: "we added 35, covered 35, then carryover 17 — how is that 52 from a 35kg dose?" `selectNitrogenSource()` sized slow/controlled-release granular batches with `rateKgHa = Math.round(nRequired / nPct)` — nRequired (netN) is only ONE month's share of what a multi-month-release batch needs to deliver in total, but the code both credited the WHOLE batch as satisfying that month's full requirement (`delivered.N += nDelivered`) AND separately carried `nDelivered / monthsCovered` into the following month via `activeNutrients` — double-crediting the same finite N content toward two months' requirement (January's Country Club IV, 18% N, 2-month release, sized for January's 35.3kg alone: delivered 35.3kg credited to January in full, then ALSO carried 17.65kg into February from that same batch — 52.95kg of credited requirement from a batch containing only 35.3kg of actual N). Fixed by scaling the sizing target by `monthsCovered` (`nTarget = nRequired * monthsCovered`, computed before the rate calculation instead of after) so a batch's per-month release (`nDelivered / monthsCovered`) matches `nRequired`, not the whole batch — `monthsCovered` is always a whole number (`Math.max(1, Math.ceil(releaseWeeks / 4))`), so quick/standard-release products (`monthsCovered === 1`) are unaffected, same formula as before. Label min/max capping and the shortfall note now reference the scaled `nTarget`; when a doubled (or more) target exceeds a product's label max — common on greens with tight rate limits — it now honestly caps and reports a shortfall instead of silently under-dosing via the old formula's phantom double-credit. Added `tests/gh337-slow-release-rate-scaling.test.js` (7 tests: structural pins + a standalone reimplementation of the scaled formula against the real Canberra January numbers). Full suite: 1304/1304 Jest tests pass.

**GH-338** User asked how nutrient requirements get calculated when there's no soil sample, and how it worked before. Investigation found: `extractPpm()` (`nutrition-calendar.js`) defaulted every branch to `|| 0`, including "the field doesn't exist in the data at all" — collapsing "no soil sample" into "measured 0 ppm". Since 0 ppm reads as maximally below every floor, this silently triggered the LARGEST possible Lift correction for every P/K/Ca/Mg/S on a site that was never actually tested — the opposite of "no correction", and the exact "silently substitute maximal correction" behaviour flagged as unacceptable. The legacy sibling engine, `nutrition-requirement-engine.js`'s `calculateAllRequirements()`, already does the right thing: `if (currentLevel !== undefined && currentLevel !== null) { ... }` — a missing nutrient is simply omitted from results, never defaulted or fabricated. `calculateDeficit()` (the legacy per-methodology fallback, used when a resolved AA/SLAN/MLSN range isn't available) had the identical bug one level deeper: `threshold - currentPpm` with `currentPpm` undefined/null coerces to `threshold - 0`, returning the FULL threshold as a fabricated deficit. Fixed both: `extractPpm()` now returns `null` (not `0`) when a field is genuinely absent, distinguishable from a real (if unlikely) `0` reading; `calculateDeficit()` gained an explicit `typeof currentPpm !== 'number'` guard; `computeProgram()`'s STEP 4 loop checks the same condition before touching either the aaRanges branch or `calculateDeficit()`, skips deficit/lift entirely for that nutrient (excluding N — N was never soil-ppm-based, it's a direct annual override, confirmed with the user this fix doesn't apply to it), and flags it in a new `missing_soil_data` map returned from `computeProgram()`. Required for a flagged nutrient is removal-only (Removal depends on Annual N, not soil ppm, so it's still a defensible number), not a fabricated correction. Propagated `missing_soil_data` through `nutrition-au-fertiliser-integration.js`, `nutrition-prebble-integration.js`, and `nutrition-nz-fertiliser-integration.js` (same carry-through pattern as `annual_removal`/`annual_lift`, GH-312) so `classifyBalance()` returns an explicit `'no-data'` status ("No Soil Data") *before* the existing `canCompute` check, rather than letting a removal-only Required silently fall into the pct-based fallback and read as a confirmed Deficit/On Track verdict. Added a `'no-data'` → neutral-grey mapping to `statusVisualClass()` and matching CSS (`.nutrient-status-no-data`, `.nutrient-diff.neutral`, `.au-fert-neutral`/`.prebble-neutral`, `.au-fert-balance-row--neutral`/`.prebble-balance-row--neutral`, including the older duplicate style block in `nutrition-prebble-integration.js`'s `injectStyles()`) in both files. Added `tests/gh338-missing-soil-data.test.js` (12 tests); updated `tests/gh313-nutrient-range-column.test.js` and `tests/gh333-excess-deficit-color-and-removal.test.js` for the new no-data branch. Full suite: 1316/1316 Jest tests pass.

**GH-339** Live logs from a Westview site (N under-delivered: 174.8 of 200 kg required) traced to `selectFoliarNitrogen()`'s liquid-product scoring — `[GH336-DEBUG]` score-breakdown logging (added to the candidate-scoring loop to investigate) showed Hi Start Turf (N=10%, P=13%) out-scoring Greenmaster Liquid High N (N=25%) in February purely on `pScore` (P-delivery-accuracy, weight ×0.25): Hi Start Turf's P content happened to match February's small P requirement almost perfectly (`pScore: 100`), while Greenmaster (a pure-N liquid) scored `pScore: 25` for having none. Nothing in `totalScore` scored N-delivery capacity itself — `pureNScore` only applies as a bonus at `gp < 0.3`, and February's `gp` was 0.42. Confirmed via `au-fertiliser-products.js`: the sibling granular selector, `selectNitrogenSource()`, already has this exact check (`nScore`, commented "THE MOST IMPORTANT SCORE") comparing delivered N against required N — `selectFoliarNitrogen()` never had an equivalent. Net effect: Hi Start Turf won February (and Long Paddock Rapid Uptake won April/October the same way) despite capping out at 8.0/6.6/6.6 kg N after 4 applications against 16.7/11/11.8 kg needed — a low-N%/off-purpose product structurally incapable of filling the month's gap kept beating a purpose-built high-N liquid that could. Fixed by adding an `nScore` to `selectFoliarNitrogen()` (SCORE 1.5), mirroring the granular selector's delivery-ratio bands (0.85–1.15 ratio → 50, down to a new `< 0.3` "structurally can't fill this month's N gap" band → -40, not present in the granular version since liquids have a hard label-rate/4-application ceiling that granular batches don't), weighted the same as `kScore`/`pScore` (×0.25). Verified against the pasted Westview log by hand: recomputing February/April/October with `nScore` added flips the winner to Greenmaster Liquid Spring & Summer/High N in all three months (e.g. Feb: Hi Start Turf 40.6 → 30.6, Greenmaster Liquid High N 36.6 → 49.1), while March — already correctly won by Greenmaster — is unaffected. `nDeliveryRatio`/`nScore` added to the existing `[GH336-DEBUG]` score-breakdown log for live verification. Added `tests/gh339-foliar-n-delivery-score.test.js` (structural pins + a standalone reimplementation of the banding against the real Westview numbers). Full suite: 1316/1316 Jest tests pass (before adding the new GH-339 test file).

**GH-340** User flagged K over-delivery on an NZ site (Required=110.1kg, Delivered=144.7kg, +31%) — same symptom family as GH-339 but the NZ engine (`prebbles-products.js`, `PrebbleRecommender`) has no `pScore` equivalent, so the AU root cause didn't apply. Traced instead to `selectNitrogenSource()`'s K-catch-up bonus (`kDeficitBonus`, up to 50 unweighted points for K≥15% products) at [prebbles-products.js:2416-2419](prebbles-products.js#L2416-L2419): `kRunningBehind` compared K delivered-so-far against the FULL annual K requirement (`annualKRequired`), which is trivially true in January (`0 / 110.1 < 0.7`) before any month has had a chance to deliver anything — "behind" by definition, not by an actual missed schedule. Confirmed live: this fired the bonus in both January and February on a Browntop Bent greens site, both months picking Sierraform GT Anti-Stress (K=21.6%) at 200kg/ha (43.2kg K each) — 86.4kg (78% of the annual 110.1kg target) delivered in the first 2 of 12 months, before later months (CC MD Greens STD 16-0-6.7 in Mar/Dec, CC MD IV Greens in Apr/Nov) added more on top, landing at 144.7kg. Fixed by computing `annualKRequiredToDate` (pro-rata sum of `monthData.K` for the months processed so far, inclusive) in `generateProgram()`'s month loop and comparing `annualKDelivered` against that instead of the full-year total — "behind" now means behind the actual elapsed-year schedule. January still triggers the bonus on its own (nothing has been delivered before the year's first month, which is expected), but the bonus no longer perpetuates itself into February once January's application already covers Feb's pro-rata share. Added `tests/gh340-nz-k-catchup-pacing.test.js`. Full suite: 1322/1322 Jest tests pass (before adding the new GH-340 test file).

**GH-341** `kDeficitBonus` (NZ K catch-up bonus) rewarded raw K% with no regard for how well-suited the candidate otherwise was, letting a badly N:K-matched, poorly-suited-to-soil-temp product win purely on the bonus in January (still-unavoidable `kRunningBehind: true`). Fixed by scaling `kDeficitBonus` by `ratioScore/100`, so a poor match gets proportionally discounted instead of overriding a better fit. Added `tests/gh341-nz-k-deficit-bonus-ratio-scaling.test.js`.

**GH-342** P had no annual tracking in `au-fertiliser-products.js`, so each month's `pScore` compared against the raw monthly P slice with no memory of P already delivered — non-overlapping granular picks across the year could each independently deliver P, compounding well past the annual target. Added `activeP`/`netP` (release-window carry-over, mirroring N/K), then capped `netP` further against the remaining annual P budget (`annualTargets.P - delivered.P`) so later months see the true remaining need. Also fixed the P "not needed" scoring branch to gate on `pRequired <= 0` instead of the annual `soilPSufficient` flag. Added `tests/gh342-au-p-carryover-tracking.test.js`.

**GH-343** Same fix as GH-342, applied to K: `netK` only capped against release-window carry-over (`activeK`), not the annual K budget, so non-overlapping granular picks (Canberra: Country Club IV 18-9-18 twice, Sierraform GT All Seasons once) each delivered K independently, landing at 131.2kg against a 110kg annual target. `netK` now also caps against `annualTargets.K - delivered.K`. Added `tests/gh343-au-k-annual-budget-cap.test.js`.

**GH-344** (NZ) The low-GP (<0.3) winter branch in `getMonthlyRecommendation()` bypasses `selectNitrogenSource()` entirely and picked the highest-K% candidate unconditionally, regardless of whether K was needed that month — confirmed live on a soccer site with K already excess: July picked a K=16% product over a K=4.2% one despite `monthData.K=0`. Scoring is now two-sided: prefer high K only when `monthData.K > 0` (winter hardening intent preserved), prefer low/no K otherwise. Added `tests/gh344-nz-winter-branch-k-preference.test.js`.

**GH-345** (NZ) The `isCovered` branch in `generateProgram()` double-counted a slow-release batch's N/K: the full batch was credited to `delivered.N/K` at its application month, then a decayed share was added again at each subsequent covered month. Inflated the running pacing tally (`kRunningBehind`, strategic P's `annualPRemaining`) without affecting the final Delivered column. Removed the re-credit; covered months now only add genuinely new liquid top-ups. Added `tests/gh345-nz-covered-month-no-double-count.test.js`.

**GH-346** Monthly Nutrient Program table: GP=1% rendered green ("high") instead of red ("low"). `gp-status.js`'s `toPct()` treats any value ≤1 as a 0-1 fraction and multiplies by 100; `nutrition-calendar.js` had already converted to a percentage before calling `getLevel()`, so `gpPct=1` collided with that heuristic and got re-multiplied to 100. Fixed by passing the raw fraction (`m.gp`) instead, matching the convention already used by the AU/NZ integration files. Added `tests/gh346-nutrition-calendar-gp-color-collision.test.js`.

**GH-347** (NZ) In `prebbles-products.js`'s rate-capping branches (granular `selectNitrogenSource()` and liquid `selectFoliarNitrogen()`), `splitCount` was computed to decide if splitting was reasonable, then never reset to 1 when it wasn't (capped to a single application instead). `kDelivered` (and, in the liquid path, a post-rounding N recalculation) multiplied by that stale `splitCount`, overstating delivery as if several full-rate applications had happened instead of one — confirmed live: a single capped 200kg/ha application (32kg K) was counted as 96kg K (stale `splitCount=3`). Fixed by resetting `splitCount = 1` in both branches. Added `tests/gh347-nz-stale-splitcount-overstate.test.js`.

**GH-349** (NZ) Andersons Pendi Pro 22-0-5 (PGG Wrightson) contains a pre-emergent herbicide but had no seeding/winter safety rules. Added a `preEmergentHerbicide` flag on the product, excluded it from the low-GP winter branch on sports fields once GP drops below 20%, and added a static warning note ("verify no seeding/overseeding... within the next 12 weeks") whenever it's recommended on sports fields. Added `tests/gh349-nz-preemergent-herbicide-rules.test.js`.

**GH-350** Word export audit found two report-only bugs. (1) Monthly Schedule GP colour passed an already-converted percentage into `GAIP_GPStatus.getColorDocx()`, hitting the same `toPct()` double-conversion ambiguity GH-346 fixed elsewhere — a month at exactly 0%/1% GP would render green instead of red; fixed by passing the raw fraction. (2) The GH-349 herbicide warning rendered mixed into the regular notes with no visual distinction; now split into its own bold paragraph, matching the live UI. Added `tests/gh350-word-export-gp-color-and-herbicide-note.test.js`.

**GH-351** `nutrition-requirement-engine.js`'s AMMONIUM_ACETATE branch never set an `intent` field (unlike the SLAN branch), so the Word report's K Reconciliation table couldn't tell "soil already sufficient, programme mining reserves" apart from "genuinely deficient" — every AA sample with a negative K balance rendered as a red "Advisory, review N programme" instead of the correct amber "Trend". Confirmed live: K=276ppm (sufficient) showed K req=100.0 correctly but was flagged Advisory anyway. Fixed by setting `intent` on all three AA branches (`suppress-above-ceiling` / `lift-to-floor` / `removal-only`), matching SLAN's pattern. Added `tests/gh351-aa-intent-field-missing.test.js`.

**GH-352** Word report's Annual Nutrient Requirements showed non-zero P/K/S for AA sites even when the live calendar correctly zeroed them (e.g. K=276ppm showing K req=100 instead of 0). Root cause: `word-export.js`'s `_aaRanges` resolution read `window.GAIP_STATE.soil` for methodology/texture/CEC, which doesn't exist yet at that point in `collectData()` — confirmed live via debug log (`_state.soil exists: false`) — so the certificate range lookup silently never ran and every AA nutrient fell to the "no range available" graceful-degradation path (pure removal, no ceiling ever applied), regardless of actual soil level. Fixed by reading `data.soil` instead (the same per-sample object the engine call itself already correctly uses).

**GH-353** Follow-up to GH-352: `data.soil` never carries a `soilTexture`/`texture` field, so `HillLabsSampleTypes.deriveCode()` couldn't match the site's actual certificate code and silently fell back to the generic sands/others range instead of the real S277 band — confirmed live (`derived code: null`, `soilTexture: null` on a real sand-profile S277 site). Fixed by falling back to `window.GAIP_HUB_CONFIG.soilTexture`, the same page-level PHP-injected global `nutrition-calendar.js` already uses for this exact gap (stable per page load, unlike GAIP_STATE.soil). Added `tests/gh353-word-export-soiltexture-hub-config-fallback.test.js`.

**GH-354** Combined-export per-sample programme recompute silently failed for every AA site tested (`Cannot read properties of null (reading '0')`, caught and swallowed by a try/catch). Root cause, found via full stack trace: `nutrition-calendar.js`'s `computeProgram()` only checked that `inputs.monthlyTemps` was truthy, not that all 12 months held a real number — `calculateMonthlyGP()` does that per-month check and returns `null` on the first gap, and that `null` then reached `distributeByGP()` unchecked (`monthlyGP[m]` on `null`), crashing deep inside the per-sample loop instead of surfacing as the intended `climateDataUnavailable` result. Fixed by validating completeness in `computeProgram()`'s existing GH-245 guard, matching `calculateMonthlyGP()`'s own check. Added `tests/gh354-incomplete-monthlytemps-crash.test.js`.

**GH-355** Follow-up to GH-353: `window.GAIP_HUB_CONFIG.soilTexture` also came back empty on a real live site (confirmed via direct DB read of `samples.soil_texture_snapshot = 'sand'` plus a full automated export re-run) — the P/K/S req numbers still matched the live calendar only because the generic sands/others band happened to agree with the real S277 range at those ppm levels; the derived certificate code stayed `null`. Added a third, tried-first texture source: `window.GAIP_STATE.turf.construction` (`'sand_profile'` → `'sand'`), confirmed reliably populated this early (species resolution already depends on the same object) and the same site-config field `hub-tissue-v3.js`'s own AA texture bucketing already keys off. Added coverage to `tests/gh353-word-export-soiltexture-hub-config-fallback.test.js`.

**GH-356** Added a real-data regression test convention (`tests/fixtures/`) — snapshots of an actual verified site/sample (pulled via direct DB read, cross-checked against the live UI and a real Combined Word export) with the exact confirmed-correct outputs, so future changes to AA/SLAN/MLSN logic get checked against real recorded numbers, not just hand-picked ones. First example: `tests/fixtures/test5-soccer-sample141.json` + `tests/real-data-test5-soccer.test.js` (Test5 - NZ, sample 141, real P/K/Ca/Mg/S values against the real Hill Labs S277 ranges).

**GH-357** Root cause of GH-353/355's texture gap: `/reports/export` never had the Plan page's GH-294 soil-texture pass-through at all — `ReportsController::pageData()` never computed it and `export.blade.php` never threaded it into `GAIP_HUB_CONFIG`, so `window.GAIP_HUB_CONFIG.soilTexture` (the fallback GH-353 added) was always empty on this specific page even though the same fallback already worked correctly on Plan. Fixed by mirroring GH-294's exact pattern (site override → account fallback) in `ReportsController` and `export.blade.php`. Confirmed live end-to-end (DB + UI + a real Combined Word export): `derived code` now correctly resolves to `S277` with the real certificate ranges (P 20–30, K 78.2–195.5), not the generic fallback. Added `tests/Feature/ReportsExportSoilTextureTest.php` (mirrors `PlanPageSoilTextureTest.php`).

**GH-358** The entire PHPUnit suite (`docker exec gilba_app php artisan test`) was silently unrunnable — every Feature test using `RefreshDatabase` aborted on migration, so GH-357's new test (and the pre-existing `PlanPageSoilTextureTest`) couldn't actually execute. Two causes: (1) `add_unique_index_to_predictions` migration used MySQL-only `DELETE p1 FROM ... INNER JOIN` syntax, invalid on the SQLite `:memory:` DB `phpunit.xml` forces for tests — added an SQLite-only portable equivalent alongside the untouched MySQL statement; (2) `UserFactory` still wrote a `password` column that a later migration had renamed to `password_hash` (Magic Link auth) — updated the factory. Also fixed `PlanPageSoilTextureTest`/`ReportsExportSoilTextureTest`'s own test helpers, which never set the required `accounts.owner_user_id` and explicitly inserted `null` into the NOT-NULL `accounts.soil_texture` (schema default only applies when the column is omitted, not when null is passed explicitly). Suite now actually runs: 85 passed, 10 failed (the 10 failures are pre-existing, unrelated `SiteApiTest` permission/API issues never previously visible because the suite never got that far — flagged separately, not investigated here).

**GH-359** Investigated and fixed the 10 `SiteApiTest` failures GH-358 revealed — all were stale tests written before deliberate later changes, not production bugs. (1) The RBAC migration (`2026_06_04_000000_add_rbac_and_auth_tables.php`) retired `'owner'` as a `site_user.role` value and bulk-converted existing rows to `'manager'`; `SiteController` correctly assigns `'manager'` to a site's creator, but `SiteApiTest` still asserted/attached the retired `'owner'` value, and `User::canEditSite()/canManageSite()` correctly don't recognize it — causing both the direct role-assertion failures and every cascading 403 on site update/config update/sample store/spray-log create/delete for sites built via the test's own helper. Updated the test's assertions and its `createSiteForUser()` helper to use `'manager'`. (2) `SiteController::index()` also had a genuine live bug from the same-era GH-66 refactor: `$user->sites()->wherePivot('status', 'active')` references a `site_user.status` column that was never added by any migration (confirmed against both the real MySQL schema and the SQLite test DB) — this 500'd `GET /api/sites` for every non-admin user, only ever masked because manual testing used an admin account (which takes the other branch). Removed the erroneous clause. (3) `test_authenticated_user_sync_can_restore_soft_deleted_sample` predated GH-194's safety guard against wiping all samples of a type when a client sends an empty array — rewrote it to exercise real per-sample reconciliation (delete one sample while another of the same type stays present) instead of relying on the exact behavior GH-194 deliberately prevents. (4) `ExampleTest`'s root-redirect assertion predated `/` being repointed at `/dashboard` (the new hub's home) instead of the legacy `/hub`. Full suite: 95 passed, 0 failed.

**GH-360** Hoxton audit v6, D30 (distributor filter not reaching Word export product selection). Verification step: confirmed live (Test5-NZ, Prebble's distributor selected on Plan > Nutrition) that the on-screen and Combined Word export product sets match exactly (`MESA Proscape 51%`, `Ammos 22 (Nitro 22)`) — the distributor propagation GH-230 added does work once the region resolves, so D30's remaining failure had to be in region resolution itself. An interim guard added here (skip a sample whose site config has an `nzDistributor` but whose region resolved to AU) was **superseded and removed by GH-362**, which fixes the resolution rather than guarding its output; nothing from this entry remains in the code.

**GH-361** Hoxton audit v6, D07a (no tissue gate on K/P requirement). `nutrition-calendar.js`'s `computeProgram()` derived P and K removal from N by a fixed generic ratio (`CONFIG.nutrientRatiosToN`: P 0.10, K 0.55 — a textbook tissue composition) regardless of whether a real tissue sample existed for that site. Confirmed on this repo's own Test5-NZ / Soccer tissue sample (id 144: N 4.57%, P 0.62%, K 1.05% — N and P match the audit's Hoxton figures exactly) that the generic ratio under-reads P removal by roughly a third against the real P/N of 0.136, the effect the audit described. Note the direction is per-nutrient and depends on the sample: on this same fixture real tissue K is 1.05% (K/N 0.23, below the generic 0.55), so the gate *lowers* K removal here rather than raising it — the audit's Hoxton capture reads tissue K 3.05% (K/N 0.667) and would raise it. Fix, agreed with the client: when a real tissue sample is available (N, P, K all present and N > 0), its measured P/N and K/N ratios now govern the removal baseline instead of the generic constant; Ca/Mg/S deliberately stay on the generic ratio (D07a scopes the tissue gate to P/K only — Ca/Mg reach the client through the amendment path, not this one). Threaded `inputs.tissuePercent` through both the live Plan-page path (`collectFromState()`, reading `GAIP_STATE.tissue`) and the Combined Word export's per-sample overlay (`word-export-combined.js`, reading `r.data.tissue`, the same per-sample pipeline soil already uses). No tissue sample (the common case) is unaffected — this is additive. New `tests/gh361-tissue-gate-k-p-ratio.test.js` exercises the ratio switch, the Ca/Mg/S scope boundary, the partial/zero-tissue guard against a divide-by-zero, and (GH-362) the plausibility clamp.

**GH-362** Hoxton audit v6, D30 root cause — replaces GH-360's guard. The Combined export resolved its product-catalogue branch (`_isNZ`/`_usePrebble`/`_useAU`) **once for the whole document**, from `NutritionPrebbleIntegration.isNewZealand()` → `regional-profiles.js` `detectRegionFromHub()`, which reads the single global `.gaip-lat`/`.gaip-lon` DOM inputs. Two failures follow, both producing the audit's exact symptom (an NZ/Prebbles site's export carrying AU-catalogue products): the collection loop switches the active site per entry, so those inputs held whichever site was collected *last* and every sample in a multi-site export got that one site's catalogue; and when the inputs are empty or non-numeric `detectRegionFromHub()` returns its `'uk_ireland'` **default**, which `isNewZealand()` accepted as a real answer and returned false from — never reaching its own Methods 2 and 3, which read `GAIP_STATE`/`GAIP_HUB_CONFIG` and would have answered correctly. Same class as the GH-245 follow-up that fixed the *climate* binding per-sample and left this one global. Fixed at both levels: the branch is now resolved per sample from that sample's own coordinates (`r.data.engineInputs.climate`, already per-sample), and `isNewZealand()` only trusts `detectRegionFromHub()` when the inputs it reads hold usable coordinates. An NZ sample with no Prebble catalogue loaded now omits its programme and says so instead of silently substituting the AU catalogue — which also required clearing `r.data.nutritionProgram` (`collectData()` may have already filled it from the on-screen `GAIP_NUTRITION_PROGRAM` cache) and opening the report section on the reason flag: the `hasData`-only gate had made `renderNutritionProgramSection()`'s own "no programme, and here is why" branch unreachable, so **GH-245's climate-unavailable message had never actually printed**. Also clamps GH-361's derived tissue ratio to a plausibility band — tissue macros can be entered in mg/kg as well as %, and a mixed-unit sample yields a ratio ~1300x too large (a 200 kg N/ha programme would have become 271,000 kg P/ha). Confirmed live: branch resolves `via sample-coordinates`, product set unchanged on the working case. New `tests/gh362-nz-region-detection-fallthrough.test.js` (6 tests) pins the fall-through in both directions.

**GH-363** The Combined export's per-sample climate overlay handed `computeProgram()` a `monthlyTemps` object keyed **1-12** (`climate-normals-service.js`'s convention, which `nutrition-requirement-engine.js` shares) while `GilbaNutritionCalendar` works in **0-11** and has `extractMonthlyTemps()` to reindex exactly once on the way in — the overlay bypassed it. GH-354's completeness check (`[0..11].every(...)`) is therefore always false against that object, so **every** sample returned `climateDataUnavailable: 'fetch-failed'` even where NASA POWER had resolved cleanly: confirmed live on a fully-resolved site, per-sample programmes came out `ok=0 failed=1`, and with `r.data.nutritionProgram` left untouched on that branch the report silently printed the cached site-level programme instead of the per-sample recompute `b35fix307`/GH-245 introduced. GH-354 guarded the crash this caused rather than the mismatch causing it. Reindexed through the calendar's own helper (one implementation of the convention, not two) and cleared the stale programme on the climate-unavailable branch, so an uncomputable sample now says so rather than showing another site's numbers. Confirmed live: same site now returns `ok=1 failed=0`, no climate-unavailable warnings, and GH-361's tissue gate — previously unreachable behind this failure — fires with the real `P/N=0.136 K/N=0.230`. Exported document byte-identical apart from timestamps on the single-site case, where the cache happened to match.

**GH-364** Soil-texture resolution order in the Word export, correcting two things the GH-352..357 chain left behind. (1) GH-355 ranked a `turf.construction` guess (`sand_profile` -> `sand`, a two-way bucket) **above** the site's real `sites.soil_texture_override`/`accounts.soil_texture` -- it did that because at the time the other sources were empty on that page, which GH-357 then root-caused and fixed, leaving the ordering behind it. A hybrid site (construction `sand_profile`, texture `clay_loam`) therefore resolved S277 in the export while the Plan page -- which reads only `GAIP_HUB_CONFIG.soilTexture`, `nutrition-calendar.js` having no concept of construction -- resolved the generic band, so UI and export disagreed on P/K/Ca/Mg. construction is now the last resort. (2) `GAIP_HUB_CONFIG.soilTexture` is the ACTIVE site's value, resolved once per page load, but a combined export deliberately spans several sites (climate is resolved per-coordinate for that exact reason) -- so every sample of every site was ranged against whichever site happened to be active at page load. `samples.soil_texture_snapshot` (GH-263/264, surfaced as `soilTextureSnapshot`) is the per-sample value and `_buildEngineInputs()` runs once per sample with that sample loaded, so it now leads the chain. Also added the GH-357 pass-through to `reports/scenarios` and `reports/forensic`, which load the same export modules and which `ReportsController` was already computing `$soilTexture` for.

**GH-365** A batch of related defects found by an independent review of the same NZ/AA work. (1) `nutrition-requirement-engine.js`'s AA fall-through returned `intent: 'removal-only'` both when soil sat inside a resolved range and when no range resolved at all, so `_classifyKReconState()` printed "soil sufficient, programme replenishment recommended" to the client for a soil level never compared to anything -- the unresolved case is now `removal-only-unverified` with its own honest wording, same arithmetic. (2) The MLSN branch carried no `intent` at all, so the DEFAULT methodology still had the exact K-reconciliation bug GH-351 fixed for AA: a sufficient soil with a negative programme balance rendered red "Advisory". (3) `GAIP_GPStatus.toPct()` guesses the caller's unit and is unavoidably wrong for exactly one input -- GP `1` is 100% to a fraction-caller and 1% to a percentage-caller, so a near-dormant surface at 1% GP rendered green "High"; GH-346 and GH-350 each patched one call site, the ambiguity stayed. Added unit-explicit `…Pct`/`…Frac` entry points and moved all thirteen call sites onto the one matching their own unit. (Also corrects GH-350's changelog claim: 0% always rendered correctly; only exactly-1 was wrong.) (4) `prebbles-products.js`'s GH-349 herbicide exclusion ran *after* the MESA-vs-slow-release pool choice, so a pool whose only MESA product carried the flag would be chosen and then emptied, leaving the month with no granular N even though a clean slow-release fallback was available; the filter now runs before the choice. (5) `_collectAATexture()` returned the literal `'sands'` for every site on `/plan` and `/reports/export`, where its `.gaip-aa-soil-texture` element does not exist -- sand thresholds were being applied to clay sites silently. It now resolves the site's real texture and warns when nothing resolves (the downstream sands fallback is unchanged, so no existing site's numbers move -- it is just no longer invisible). (6) `'owner'`, retired as a `site_user.role` value by the RBAC migration, remained in `DatabaseSeeder` and three Feature tests. (7) Test quality: the `real-data-test5-soccer` K-reconciliation block asserted a fixture field against a string literal without calling the classifier it was named after -- it now loads `word-export.js` and calls the real `_classifyKReconState()`, and its engine block resolves ranges through the production `deriveCode()`/`getRangesPpm()` chain instead of reading them from `expected`; the fixture gained the sample's own `soil_texture_snapshot`. Three `src.slice(idx, idx + N)` windows that three prior commits had each had to widen were re-anchored to a following statement.

**GH-366** GH-361's tissue gate never fired on the Plan page, only in the Word export. It reads the tissue sample from `GAIP_STATE`, which the export builds per sample, but the Plan bridge only ever populated turf/climate/location/`inputs.soil` and `GAIP_SampleManager` isn't loaded on that page — so `GAIP_STATE` had no tissue at all and the calendar silently fell back to the generic textbook ratio. Confirmed live on Test5-NZ: the export applied P/N 0.136 while the on-screen programme applied 0.10 for the same site and the same sample, i.e. GH-361 had introduced exactly the UI-vs-export divergence the audit raises at D31. Fixed with the same server-side pass-through shape GH-294/GH-357 used for soil texture: `PageController::topbarData()` resolves the site's most recent tissue sample (N/P/K as floats, non-numeric or absent readings stay null so the gate can tell "not measured" from a real zero) and `plan.blade.php` threads it into `GAIP_HUB_CONFIG.tissuePercent` and `state.inputs.tissue`, only when a sample actually exists. Confirmed live after the fix: the Plan page now logs the same `P/N=0.136 K/N=0.230` the export does. New `tests/Feature/PlanPageTissuePercentTest.php` (7 tests) covers the resolution, the latest-sample-wins ordering, soil-samples-don't-count, the null-not-zero rule, and both blade branches.

**GH-367** Hoxton audit D30, the two halves GH-362 left open, both found by an independent review. (1) The distributor binding itself was never checked: `(_siteCfg && _siteCfg.nzDistributor) || 'all'` collapsed "the operator never picked one" (legitimate — `'all'` is the dropdown's own default), "the site config could not be read" and "the filter machinery is missing" into one silent widening of the catalogue, so a PGG Wrightson customer could receive a purchasing table built from the raw Prebble-only pool. That is literally the question the audit's fix shape asks ("does the export path receive the distributor at all, or receive it and ignore it?") and its assertion 16 requires an unresolved binding to be a blocking failure. The site-config lookup now records whether it could run at all, separately from what it returned, and the two unresolved cases skip the sample with a named reason instead of defaulting. (2) GH-362's own "coordinates unknown" branch was unreachable: `_buildEngineInputs()` substitutes a Sydney-ish `-33/151` placeholder when a site has no saved coordinates, and those numbers read downstream as a valid Australian location — so an NZ site without coordinates still resolved to the AU catalogue silently. The substitution is now flagged (`climate.coordinatesDefaulted`) and the per-sample branch treats a placeholder as unknown, which is what makes the fallbacks reachable. The report also names which binding failed rather than printing one generic sentence. New `tests/gh367-d30-distributor-binding-fail-loud.test.js` (13 tests) pins both, including the resolution rules as an executable decision table.

**GH-368** Hoxton audit D07a in the second engine. `nutrition-requirement-engine.js` carries its own removal table (`perennialRyegrass: { N: 180, P: 18, K: 100 }` — P/N 0.10 and K/N 0.556, the same generic composition, and literally the figures the audit quotes from E3), and it is that engine which feeds the Annual Nutrient Requirements table the client complained about. GH-361 had added the tissue gate only to `nutrition-calendar.js`, so after it the two engines disagreed *more* than before — on this repo's own Test5-NZ tissue the calendar answered 46 kg K/ha while this engine still answered 100, against the audit's assertion 20. The same rule, scope (P and K only) and plausibility band now apply here, scaled against this table's own N basis, with the sample's tissue threaded in by the caller so the engine stays pure. New `tests/gh368-two-engines-tissue-parity.test.js` (10 tests) checks both engines resolve the same ratio from the same measurement.

**E2E parity harness** `tests/e2e/ui-vs-export-parity.test.js` — an opt-in harness that logs into the running stack, generates the programme on the Plan page, reads the numbers as rendered, produces a real Combined Word export, parses the .docx, and asserts the two agree (the audit's assertion 20). It also asserts every product in the export exists in that region's own catalogue by loading the catalogue modules rather than matching hand-written brand names (assertion 14), and fails the run if any fail-loud console signal fired. Added because the GH-352..368 work was repeatedly verified with green unit tests and console logs while the rendered surfaces disagreed. Skipped by default so the normal suite stays offline; run with `GILBA_E2E=1` plus credentials. First run immediately corrected a standing assumption: FoliMAX, which the audit lists as evidence of an AU catalogue leaking into an NZ export, is a PGG Wrightson **NZ** product — the audit itself flags that product-origin attribution as unverified recall.

**GH-369** The GH-361/368 tissue-ratio gate can lower a K/P removal figure while a separate tissue-sufficiency check independently flags the same reading as critically low — both correct, but juxtaposed with nothing explaining they answer different questions (replacement dose vs. plant status). Rather than reconciling the two numbers (which would risk over-applying soil-broadcast K into what may be an uptake restriction, not undersupply), threaded a `tissueInformed` flag through `nutrition-requirement-engine.js` and surfaced the tissue-status check on the same K Reconciliation row/ANR line as the figure it concerns, with an inline note that the two are independent. Also fixed a gap this surfaced: the Combined export's own K Reconciliation compute() call never received tissue data at all, so its K req could never actually be tissue-informed. The tissue-critical advisory now states that no generic foliar-K rate is verified in this codebase, instead of a number. **Follow-up pass (independent review, same day) found and fixed five further bugs in this first cut, none of which were cosmetic:** (1) `tissueInformed` described the removal component, not the printed figure — the AA suppress-above-ceiling branch forces the printed requirement to 0 while still reporting `tissueInformed=true`, so the sentence could falsely claim a ceiling-suppressed 0.0 was tissue-derived; now gated on `intent !== 'suppress-above-ceiling'` via a shared `_isTissueContradictionRow()` SSOT. (2) `nutrition-requirement-engine.js` decided P/K tissue eligibility independently per-nutrient, while `nutrition-calendar.js` requires all of N/P/K present and both ratios in-band or the whole gate is off — a P reading in mg/kg could make the calendar reject tissue governance for both nutrients while this engine still accepted K alone; fixed with `resolveTissueGate()`, applying the identical all-or-nothing rule (confirmed by cross-loading both engines against the same mixed-unit sample in a new test). The prior changelog's claim that the flag was threaded "through both requirement engines" was also false — corrected here. (3) The explanation only reached the K Reconciliation table, gated behind `_facilityKDelivered != null` — absent on exactly the branches (GH-362/363/367's climate/catalogue/distributor-unavailable cases) most likely to leave a tissue-lowered figure unexplained; now also marked (‡) on the always-rendered Annual Nutrient Requirements table. (4) P had the flag computed but nothing read it; P now gets the identical row-level treatment as K. (5) `if (data.nutritionSummary.annualK)` skipped the whole K row — including the audit's own headline "K req 0.0" case — since 0 is falsy; changed to `!= null`. Also fixed: the third tissue call site (word-export-combined.js's calendar-programme overlay) had its own inline parse that unconditionally overwrote a possibly-better upstream resolution with nulls — now only overlays when it actually resolves something; the two shared tissue helpers' input coercion was inconsistent (`typeof x === 'number'` vs `parseFloat`); the K-Reconciliation caption could point at a tissue-analysis section that doesn't exist on a tissue-free export — now gated on at least one row having tissue data; and the previously-uncited "20-40 kg K/ha" soil-side K-deficiency dose was brought in line with the new honest tissue-side wording (no invented rate) rather than being left as an unflagged exception beside it. Rewrote `tests/gh369-tissue-status-independence-note.test.js` (43 tests, including a real `buildSections()` rendering-level check of the corrected suppress-above-ceiling and tissue-free cases, not just source-regex pins) and updated `tests/gh309-aa-lift-nutrition-requirement-engine.test.js` for GH-370's unit-conversion fix landing in the same file.

**GH-370** `nutrition-requirement-engine.js`'s below-floor/below-threshold correction term (all three methodology branches — AA lift-to-floor, SLAN below-floor, MLSN below-threshold) applied a raw ppm deficit directly as a kg/ha quantity, with zero occurrences of `bulkDensity`/`soilDepth`/`* 0.1` anywhere in the file (confirmed by grep). The sibling engine, `nutrition-calendar.js`, has always done this correctly: `kgHa = (floor_ppm - current_ppm) * bulkDensity(g/cm3) * soilDepth(cm) * 0.1`, a real unit conversion (soil mass to depth under one hectare), not an empirical multiplier. Never bit on this repo's own Hoxton/Test5-NZ fixtures because every nutrient there sits above ceiling (correction = 0 either way) — live-wrong by roughly the missing factor (order of magnitude 2-3x for a typical sand profile at 15cm) the first time a real site sits genuinely below floor on this path, found while verifying D31 for Decision 11. Fixed by computing `_ppmToKgHaFactor` once per nutrient call and applying it before dividing by years-to-correct in all three branches, defaulting to `nutrition-calendar.js`'s own `CONFIG.defaultBulkDensity`/`defaultSoilDepth` (1.4 g/cm³, 10cm) when the caller doesn't supply a real per-sample reading — not an independently-invented default. Also threaded real bulk density/depth through: `data.soil.bulkDensity`/`.depth` are now populated in `collectData()` from the active sample's `rawData` (canonical `bulkDensity`/`depthCm` fields, matching `hub-persistence.js`'s own reads of the same shape) with a `GAIP_STATE.inputs.soil` fallback — both engine call sites (`word-export.js`'s single-export ANR block, `word-export-combined.js`'s ANR pass) already pass `soil: data.soil`/`soil: r.data.soil` directly, so no separate wiring was needed beyond populating the two fields once. Updated `tests/gh309-aa-lift-nutrition-requirement-engine.test.js`'s two below-floor assertions (encoded the pre-fix, dimensionally-wrong formula) and added `tests/gh370-ppm-to-kgha-unit-conversion.test.js` (7 tests) with a constructed below-floor fixture on real Hill Labs S277/Carrow 2004 ranges, since Hoxton's own fixture never exercises this branch.

**GH-371** Hoxton audit D01: a site-record coordinate write invalidated nothing. `nutrition-calendar.js`'s `computeProgram()` now stamps `meta.lat`/`meta.lon` from `collectFromState()`'s own resolved coordinates (which gained a `longitude` field and a `state.location` fallback tier alongside the existing DOM-first `latitude` — needed on `/plan`, which has no `.gaip-lat`/`.gaip-lon` DOM inputs at all and relies on `state.location` from `$activeSite->latitude/longitude`). The stamp is computed once in `persistSiteConfigPatch()` — **not** in `site-config-persistence.js`'s `mergeConfig()` as first implemented: a live Playwright check against the real dev stack found that plan.blade.php never loads `site-config-persistence.js` at all (documented in this file's own comment) and falls through to `persistSiteConfigPatch()`'s direct-PUT fallback, which stamping only inside `mergeConfig()` never reached — confirmed live via a direct DB read showing `nutritionProgramCoords` NULL after generating from Plan. Moved the computation to `persistSiteConfigPatch()`, the one point both paths actually share, and re-verified live: the stamp now lands correctly from Plan too. `mergeConfig()` merges the pre-stamped patch generically, no special-casing. One write covers `nutritionProgram` too (the four regional integrations' product-recommendation object), since it's always generated from the same calendar programme moments later in the same event chain, not four separate stamps. `snapshotConfig()`'s carry-forward step, `restoreConfig()`, and `nutrition-calendar.js`'s `restoreFromPersisted()` each compare the stamp against the site's current coordinates (0.01° tolerance) before carrying forward / restoring / rendering, and drop the cached programme or show a new "site coordinates changed, please regenerate" banner on a real mismatch instead of a live recompute (not practical at `restoreFromPersisted()`'s point in the page-load sequence — same reasoning basis as GH-245's climate-unavailable fallback). `word-export.js`'s Monthly Schedule/Nutrition Program read (previously cross-site-only) gets the identical check via `GAIP_SiteConfig`. `SiteController::update()` additionally clears `nutritionProgram`/`nutritionCalendarProgram`/`nutritionProgramCoords` from the site's `gaip` `SiteConfig` row server-side on a real coordinate change (confirmed via `$request->has()`, not `$data` key-presence, since `latitude`/`longitude` validate to `null` even when absent from the request) — belt-and-braces alongside the client-side fix, not a replacement; confirmed live (direct DB read before/after a real Settings coordinate change on Test5-NZ) that this fires and clears the cache immediately, independent of the client-side path. No stamp on already-cached data (saved before this fix) is trusted as-is, a forward-looking guard, not a retroactive one. **Consequence of that choice, on the client read path, worth stating explicitly since the paragraph above can read as though the server-side clear is authoritative — it is not, for a client holding local state:** `site-config-persistence.js`'s `pullConfigsFromServer()` only adds or overwrites non-null keys the server sends and never deletes local ones, so a historical *unstamped* programme sitting in a browser's localStorage survives `SiteController::update()`'s server-side clear and is restored as trusted on `/reports/export` and the legacy hub pages. That is the intended forward-looking behaviour rather than a defect — an unstamped programme is trusted by design — but the localStorage half of the mechanism is easy to miss when reading only the server-side description. The Plan page is unaffected (it reads the config from the DB, not from local state). Not verified live; established by code trace during the open-question-10 investigation.
