Note that GH-377's species/methodology check later added a second, independent net over the same restore paths. **Live-verified end-to-end**: changed Test5-NZ's real coordinates to London via Settings, generated a fresh Plan programme (Northern-hemisphere GP curve confirmed: Jan/Feb 2%, Jul 91%), generated a Combined Word export immediately after with no further action (Monthly N Distribution table showed the identical Northern-hemisphere curve, not stale Auckland data), reloaded Plan fresh in a new page (identical numbers restored, not a stale blob), then restored the original Auckland coordinates. New `tests/gh371-d01-coordinate-invalidation.test.js` (27 tests) and `app/tests/Feature/GH371CoordinateInvalidationTest.php` (5 tests); fixed `tests/gh322b-restore-dispatches-generated-event.test.js`'s two fixed-character-count source-slice windows (broken by this fix's own code landing earlier in `restoreFromPersisted()`) by re-anchoring to a following statement instead of a fourth arbitrary widening, per the lesson recorded in GH-365's own changelog entry. D01a (temperature-semantic labelling) and D02a (coordinate/temperature-vector provenance printing) are natural next steps building on this but are separate, smaller pieces, not attempted here. **Follow-up (independent review) found one more genuine gap, now closed:** the paragraph above describes `SiteController::update()`'s server-side clear as though it were the whole server-side story — it wasn't. `SiteController::updateConfig()` (`PUT /sites/{id}/config/gaip`) is a SECOND, independent way `Site.latitude/longitude` — and this same cached programme — can change (it syncs `config.location.lat/lon` back onto the site row on every call) and was completely untouched by the fix above, which only instrumented `update()`. Concretely dangerous because Settings' Save button fires `Promise.all([PATCH /sites/{id}, PUT .../config/gaip])` concurrently on every save (`settings-init.js`): the PUT body is a clone of the page's pre-save config, so on a coordinate-changing save it still carried the OLD `nutritionProgram`/`nutritionCalendarProgram`/`nutritionProgramCoords`, and `updateConfig()`'s `SiteConfig::updateOrCreate()` does a wholesale replace, not a merge — so if that stale PUT landed after `update()`'s own clear, the just-cleared fields got written straight back in, on the exact save action (edit coordinates, click Save) a real user actually performs. The client-side stamp/check (`nutrition-calendar.js`/`site-config-persistence.js`) still caught a resurrected row on the next restore, so a normal user saw a "please regenerate" banner rather than silently wrong numbers — but anything reading the persisted `SiteConfig` row directly (the exact case the server-side clear exists for) was exposed. Fixed on both sides. Server: `SiteController::updateConfig()` gained a new private `resolveGaipConfigWrite()`, reusing `coordinateChanged()` rather than reimplementing it, for two purposes — (1) `updateConfig()`'s own `config.location.lat/lon` sync onto the site row now runs the identical real-change-vs-no-op test `update()` uses, clearing the three cache keys when IT detects a real change; (2) since a same-request before/after diff can't see a change the SIBLING request already made moments earlier (by the time the stale PUT runs, the site row may already show the NEW coordinates, matching the PUT's own `location.lat/lon`, so a plain diff sees nothing there), the three keys are now only ever taken from an incoming payload when it carries its OWN `nutritionProgramCoords` stamp AND that stamp matches the coordinates this write is establishing — i.e. genuinely fresh, just-generated data, the same trust test the client-side restore path already applies to reads. Otherwise the incoming values are discarded and whatever's already in the DB is carried forward instead (so a config PUT that never legitimately touches nutrition data — e.g. Settings, after the client-side fix below — can't silently blow away a still-valid cache just because its own payload doesn't mention it) — unless coordinates are genuinely changing this same request, in which case even the existing DB value is now stale too and stays cleared rather than being resurrected. Client: `settings-init.js`'s two config-cloning save handlers (site form, turf-profile form — both build their PUT body from a raw `D.gaipConfig` clone) now `delete` all three cache keys from that clone before sending, unconditionally, since neither form has any legitimate reason to round-trip them; the third `config/gaip` caller in this file (the import-bundle flow) already hand-builds its PUT body as `{turf, location, pgr}` only and never carried these keys in the first place, so it needed no change. Sequencing the two requests instead of stripping was considered and rejected as the sole fix: the server-side fix alone already closes the race regardless of landing order, and stripping has no latency cost, unlike awaiting a second round trip. New tests in both existing files, amended rather than reticketed (this project's own convention, as GH-369 was amended earlier this session): `app/tests/Feature/GH371CoordinateInvalidationTest.php` gained 5 more tests (10 total) — the actual PATCH-then-PUT race reproduced end-to-end in both landing orders, `updateConfig()` detecting a real coordinate change and clearing on its own, a no-op resubmit correctly not clearing, and a freshly-stamped legitimate write still persisting (guards against the fix over-triggering and silently breaking Plan's own programme-generation save path). `tests/gh371-d01-coordinate-invalidation.test.js` gained a structural-pin block (3 tests, 30 total) for the `settings-init.js` strip, matching this file's own established convention for this DOM-heavy, hard-to-sandbox module (the same convention already used in it for `word-export.js`). Full suite: 112 PHPUnit tests / 1557 Jest tests pass, no regressions. **Live-verified against the running dev stack** (a scratch fixture site, "New test - location", not client data — its config and coordinates fully restored afterward from a byte-exact backup taken before any mutation): seeded a known `gaip` config (a `location` plus a `nutritionCalendarProgram`/`nutritionProgramCoords` stamped, all at Wollongong's coordinates), fired a real `PATCH /api/sites/{id}` moving the site to Auckland — a direct DB read confirmed `update()`'s existing clear fired (all three keys gone) — then fired the actual dangerous payload live: `PUT /api/sites/{id}/config/gaip` with `location` already at the NEW Auckland coordinates but the three cache keys still at the OLD Wollongong-stamped values (the exact pre-client-fix shape `settings-init.js` would have sent). Direct DB read afterward: still no `nutritionProgram`/`nutritionCalendarProgram`/`nutritionProgramCoords` on the row — not resurrected. Two contrast checks followed on the same live site: a `PUT` carrying a correctly-stamped programme for the NOW-current Auckland coordinates persisted normally (`annual_totals.N: 210`, `monthly[0].N: 17.5` both landed in the DB, confirmed by direct read — the fix doesn't block legitimate writes), and a completely unrelated `PUT` (irrigation method only, coordinates unchanged, payload omitting the cache keys entirely — the post-fix Settings shape) left that same programme untouched in the DB afterward (preserved, not silently wiped). **A second independent review pass (this project's standing rule: review every GH-NNN fix on a stronger model before moving on) of the follow-up above found two further real bugs in `resolveGaipConfigWrite()`'s first cut, both now fixed in the same method:** (1) the TRUST branch additionally required `!$coordinatesChanging`, which silently dropped a site's very first-ever programme write whenever its coordinates were being set for the first time — `coordinateChanged(null, $newLat, true)` always reports "changed" once `$previousLatitude` is `null`, and a brand-new site's row genuinely starts that way (`SiteController::syncRegistry()`'s `forceFill()` never sets `latitude`/`longitude`), so the exact `syncRegistry()` → `site-config-persistence.js`'s `pushConfigsToServer()` sequence a real new site goes through lost its first programme. Fixed by dropping that extra condition: `$stampFresh` alone (the incoming stamp matching `$targetLat`/`$targetLon`, the coordinates this write is establishing) is already the correct trust test regardless of whether those coordinates are new or unchanged — live-verified via the actual `POST /api/sites/sync` → `PUT .../config/gaip` sequence against a brand-new site (DB read before: `latitude`/`longitude` both `NULL`; DB read after the config PUT: the freshly-stamped programme persisted correctly, all three keys present). (2) TRUST's "has cache fields" check counted a bare `nutritionProgramCoords` alone (no actual programme) as sufficient to trust — since TRUST returns the payload verbatim into a wholesale replace, a stamp-only payload would have silently wiped a real, still-valid programme sitting under the other two keys it never mentioned; no current caller sends this shape, but it was a latent sharp edge. Fixed by requiring `nutritionProgram` or `nutritionCalendarProgram` to actually be present (`$hasProgramData`), not just the stamp. Both regressions were confirmed genuine by temporarily reintroducing each condition and watching the corresponding new test fail, then restoring the fix and watching it pass again. Three more tests added to `GH371CoordinateInvalidationTest.php` (13 total): the null-latitude first-write case, the bare-stamp-doesn't-wipe case, and a malformed (non-array) `nutritionProgramCoords` degrading safely instead of erroring. Also fixed a real gap in the JS regression suite this same review found: `tests/gh371-d01-coordinate-invalidation.test.js`'s "strip runs unconditionally" test used `indexOf()`, which only ever finds the FIRST occurrence, so it silently never actually checked the turf-profile form's own strip despite its name claiming to cover "either form" — rewritten to walk both occurrences explicitly. Full suite after this pass: 115 PHPUnit / 1557 Jest, no regressions. **Two further findings from this same review pass were deliberately NOT fixed here, left as known, accepted, out-of-scope items** (per this ticket's specific focus on the coordinate race — fixing these would mean building a general "which non-coordinate inputs also invalidate a cached programme" mechanism, a materially different and larger feature): the strip-then-carry-forward mechanism this fix relies on can now resurrect a programme that a plain wholesale-replace would previously have accidentally cleared as a side effect, when a config write changes `turf` (species/methodology/etc., a real programme input) without changing coordinates — concretely, `settings-init.js`'s import-bundle flow (`applySiteConfig`) PUTs `{turf, location, pgr}` and, post-fix, now carries the pre-import programme forward across a turf change it should probably have invalidated too; `site-setup-wizard.js`'s onboarding flow does the same but is low-risk in practice (usually a brand-new site with nothing cached yet to resurrect). Separately, a legitimately fresh but *unstamped* programme (`computeProgram()` couldn't resolve numeric coordinates, so `persistSiteConfigPatch()` never added a stamp) is silently replaced by whatever older, stamped programme the DB already has rather than being accepted or flagged — checked against the real dev DB: 9 of the 10 real sites currently have no stamp at all (all generated before stamping shipped), so the practical exposure is narrow (any generation that happens to resolve no real coordinates) but the failure mode is silent, which cuts against this project's own "show what's missing, don't quietly fall back" rule. Also newly confirmed while auditing every `config/gaip` PUT call site for this second pass: `site-setup-wizard.js`'s `persistWizardState()` (~line 1243-1298) genuinely fires the identical concurrent coordinate-PATCH-plus-config-PUT `Promise.all` pattern `settings-init.js`'s site form does. `site-config-persistence.js`'s `pushConfigsToServer()` (~line 268-307) does not — it fires `sites/sync` (label-only, no coordinates) followed by the config PUT, not a coordinate PATCH; the actual coordinate PATCH in that file is a separate function, `saveLocationToServer()` (~line 160-181), invoked from a different handler (`gaip:site-save-requested`, ~line 1504-1515) — so it is not the *same* concurrent-pair race `persistWizardState()`/`settings-init.js` are, though a `gaip:site-save-requested` PATCH landing around a debounced `pushConfigsToServer()` PUT is still a two-request sequence the server-side fix has to cover, and does (**GH-375 correction**: an earlier version of this entry described `pushConfigsToServer()` itself as firing the `Promise.all` pattern, which was inaccurate as a mechanism — the conclusion that neither strips the three cache keys client-side, and that this is not a live bug because the server-side fix covers it regardless, was and remains correct).

**GH-372** Follow-up to GH-366's own open question: does the Plan page and the Combined export pick the SAME tissue sample on a site with more than one on file? Reproduced live on a real site with two dated tissue samples: they agree — both resolve to the chronologically-latest one (Plan's `PageController::topbarData()` via an explicit `COALESCE(lab_date,sample_date) DESC, id DESC` query; the export's `word-export-combined.js` `enumerateSamples()`/`buildZoneMap()` via its own explicit per-zone `date > current.date` comparison, then an explicit `loadSample('tissue', …)` immediately before the read that overwrites whatever `GAIP_SampleManager`'s generic active-sample pointer held). The prior review's specific claim — that the export effectively depends on a position-based "first imported" default — describes a REAL fallback (`site-selector-ui.js`'s `reloadActiveSample()`, `getSamples(dt)[0]`, no date comparison at all) but not the one that actually governs the export's printed figures, which always explicitly re-resolves per zone first. That fallback was still a live, if currently-invisible, landmine — hardened it to pick by date explicitly, the same `date > current.date` technique `buildZoneMap()` already uses, rather than trusting incoming array order (correct until now only because `SampleController::index()` happens to pre-sort `lab_date DESC, id DESC` and the sync pipeline preserves that order). Two further live scenarios, both real findings: (1) an exact-date tie — Burns' own real tissue samples 120/121 turned out to sit in two different zones (no real contention), so a same-zone tie was constructed instead (two samples, one date, one zone); Plan and the export agreed (both picked the higher-id sample) — but only because `buildZoneMap()`'s tie-break is ALSO array-order-dependent, not an explicit id rule, the identical fragility class just fixed in `site-selector-ui.js`, left as a documented-and-pinned risk here rather than fixed, since it wasn't the target of this ticket's fix and isn't reachable via the export's real call path today. (2) a genuine multi-zone site with different zones carrying different tissue dates (Burns, three zones, live-verified real rendered numbers) — confirmed the export correctly gives each zone its own tissue sample while Plan applies one site-wide reading to its one site-wide programme regardless of zone; a real, pre-existing, deliberate structural scope difference (Plan has no per-zone concept at all), not a bug, and not touched here. New `tests/gh372-tissue-sample-selection-consistency.test.js` (9 tests) exercises the real `enumerateSamples()`/`buildZoneMap()` and the real, now-fixed `reloadActiveSample()` — not re-implementations of either — including the non-date-sorted-array case that would have failed pre-fix, the same-zone-tie fragility pin, and the multi-zone independence pin. Full suite: 115 PHPUnit / 1566 Jest, no regressions.

**GH-373** Reverted GH-369's tissue-critical advisory wording to the plain pre-GH-369 instruction ("Tissue K critically low (X%) for SPECIES - apply foliar potassium immediately.", and the P equivalent). GH-369 had tried to give the tissue-side advisory a cited dose to match the soil-side line, found none exists to cite anywhere in this codebase, and settled for saying so in the report text ("no generic foliar-K rate is verified in this system — apply the selected product at its own label rate"). That surfaced the hub's own data gap to a paying client inside an agronomy report, which is the wrong place for it, and the fallback half stated the obvious. Removed the `TISSUE_K_CRITICAL_ADVISORY_NO_DOSE` constant and its three render sites' references (Priority Actions, the single-export ANR row, the combined export's K Reconciliation column); the reasoning for why no dose figure is printed now lives in a code comment where the constant used to sit. Tests updated to pin the restored wording plus two negative assertions (no kg/ha figure, no "verified in this system"/"label rate" commentary) at every render site. Full suite: 1565 Jest, no regressions.

**GH-374** Hoxton audit D06 (P recommended into soil/tissue both already above optimal) verified closed, not re-implemented — the GH-299/300/305 sufficiency-ceiling work already covers P/S generically. Live-checked against Test5-NZ's real S277/AA fixture (Olsen P 40 ppm, ceiling 20–30): Plan page prints Annual P Requirement 0 kg/ha/yr, no P-carrying product (MAP Tech or otherwise) appears in the Monthly Schedule or Annual Product Summary, and the Combined Word export's Annual Nutrient Requirements table shows P req 0.0 alongside K req 0.0 and S req 0.0. Added the missing regression coverage (assertion 19, "same assertion for P and S, symmetrically" — S and K were already pinned per-engine, P had zero coverage in `nutrition-calendar.js`'s `computeProgram()` specifically): extended `tests/real-data-test5-soccer.test.js` with real `computeProgram()` calls against the same confirmed-live fixture, including a below-ceiling contrast case proving the assertions are load-bearing. Full suite: 1574 Jest, no regressions.

**GH-375** Four items from an independent review pass over GH-371's `updateConfig()` amendment, GH-372, and GH-373, batched in one entry since all four trace to that single pass. (1) **Root-cause fix, not a client-side patch**: `SampleController::index()` (`app/Http/Controllers/SampleController.php`) sorted its `/api/samples` response by the raw `orderByDesc('lab_date')->orderByDesc('id')` — MySQL sorts NULLs last on DESC, so a sample with only a `sample_date` (no `lab_date`) sorted behind every sample carrying any `lab_date`, regardless of true chronological order, diverging from `PageController::topbarData()`'s own `orderByRaw('COALESCE(lab_date, sample_date) DESC')->orderByDesc('id')` tissue-sample choice. On a genuine tie (same effective date, one sample via `lab_date`, the other via `sample_date` only) that flipped this endpoint's array order relative to `topbarData()`'s `id DESC` tiebreak, which `site-selector-ui.js`'s GH-372 `reloadActiveSample()` fallback trusts on a tie — exactly the class of UI-vs-export divergence GH-372 was closing. Fixed by sorting `index()` on the identical `COALESCE(lab_date, sample_date) DESC, id DESC` expression `topbarData()` uses. Consumer audit before changing it (per this ticket's own instruction to verify blast radius, not assume): every GET consumer of this endpoint (`soil-nutrition-analysis.js`, `water-balance-analysis.js`, `nutrition-calendar.js`'s Plan sample picker, which reuses the former) re-sorts the array client-side by the same coalesced `lab_date || sample_date` key before using it, so none depend on the server's raw order; no PHPUnit test asserted the old ordering. (Review addendum: that audit missed the fourth and largest consumer, `sample-persistence.js`'s `fetchSamplesFromServer()` — `GET samples?limit=200`, every hub page's sample store — whose response order is preserved into the client store and from there into the export picker and sample-switcher order and the `buildZoneMap()`/`reloadActiveSample()` exact-date tie-breaks. None of those re-sort, but the only samples this change moves are ones with no `lab_date` at all (from "after every dated sample" to their real chronological slot — none exist in the dev DB), ties still resolve `id DESC` as before, and with more than 200 samples the sort now selects the 200 newest by effective date instead of by raw `lab_date`. Red-checked: 2 of the 3 new PHPUnit tests fail against the pre-fix controller.) New `app/tests/Feature/GH375SampleOrderingTest.php` (3 tests) pins the exact null-`lab_date` tie from the review (`index()` and `topbarData()` agreeing on `id DESC`) plus the general coalesced-sort case, server-side. New `tests/gh375-null-lab-date-tie-real-split.test.js` (3 tests) exercises the same tie through the REAL client-side coalescing code (`sample-persistence.js`'s sync loop, extracted via the `vm` technique `tests/gh264-sample-snapshot-backfill.test.js` established, not a re-implementation) feeding the real `site-selector-ui.js` fallback — deliberately not using the pre-coalesced `.date` shortcut every existing GH-372 test fixture uses, since that shortcut bypasses the exact `lab_date`/`sample_date` split this bug lived in. **Live-verified** against the real dev stack and real MySQL (not SQLite, where the NULL-sort behaviour this bug depended on doesn't apply the same way): seeded the exact tie on the "New test - location" scratch site (sample 150: `lab_date`/`sample_date` both 2026-01-15; sample 151: `lab_date` NULL, `sample_date` 2026-01-15) and confirmed three independent surfaces now agree — the raw `GET /api/samples?...` response returns sample 151 first (`id DESC` on the tie, confirmed via an authenticated fetch against the live running app), Plan's `tissuePercent` (server-side `topbarData()`) resolves to sample 151 (N 4.57/P 0.62/K 1.05), and `/reports/export`'s client-side `reloadActiveSample()` fallback auto-loads the same sample 151 (`gh375_tie_y`) — console-confirmed (`Auto-loaded most recent tissue sample: gh375_tie_y`) and via direct `GAIP_SampleManager` state read. Fixture samples deleted and the login user's `last_active_site_id` restored afterward, confirmed via direct DB read (18 samples on the scratch site, matching the pre-test count; no `gh375_tie_x`/`gh375_tie_y` rows remain). (2) `SiteController::resolveGaipConfigWrite()`'s `$hasProgramData` (~line 483) tested `array_key_exists('nutritionProgram', ...) || array_key_exists('nutritionCalendarProgram', ...)` — key presence, not a real value, so a payload carrying an explicit `"nutritionProgram": null` alongside a matching stamp would have satisfied the TRUST branch and been written verbatim, nulling real data under the other key; no current caller emits an explicit null (confirmed in review), so this was latent, not live, but the same class as the already-fixed bare-stamp bug, and the method's own docblock already claimed "requires an actual programme" — which key-presence alone didn't test. Now requires the value to be non-null too; docblock adjusted to say so explicitly. New test in `app/tests/Feature/GH371CoordinateInvalidationTest.php` (now 14 tests) pins the explicit-null case alongside the existing bare-stamp pin. (3) Corrected GH-371's own changelog entry above in place: it described `site-config-persistence.js`'s `pushConfigsToServer()` as firing "the identical concurrent coordinate-PATCH-plus-config-PUT `Promise.all` pattern" — wrong as a mechanism. `pushConfigsToServer()` (~line 268-307) calls `sites/sync` (label-only, no coordinates) then the config PUT; the actual coordinate PATCH in that file is a separate function, `saveLocationToServer()` (~line 160-181), invoked from a different handler (`gaip:site-save-requested`, ~line 1504-1515). `site-setup-wizard.js`'s `persistWizardState()` (~line 1243-1298) genuinely does match the described `Promise.all` pattern. The entry's conclusion (not a live bug, the server-side guard in `resolveGaipConfigWrite()` covers it regardless) was correct and stands; only the mechanism description was wrong — corrected in place with a marked note rather than silently rewritten. (4) `assets/word-export.js`'s soil-side K-deficiency Priority Actions advisory (~line 4713, `generatePriorityActions()`) still carried "no generic soil-applied K correction rate is verified in this system — size the application from the soil K deficit against this site's own bulk density and sample depth, or per the selected product's label rate", the same class of hub-data-gap meta-commentary GH-373 removed from the tissue-side advisory (a paying client's agronomy report is not the place to surface the hub's own data gaps) — GH-373 simply didn't touch this line, a different advisory. Reverted to the plain instruction with no dose figure and no commentary: "Severe K deficiency - apply potassium sulphate promptly." (no invented number — the reasoning for why none is printed now lives only in the surrounding code comment, matching GH-373's own pattern). `tests/gh369-tissue-status-independence-note.test.js`'s soil-side assertion (~line 331) updated to match; the neighbouring tissue-side negative assertion (~line 322, "no verified rate" wording absent) was left untouched. **Live-verified**: generated a real Combined Word export (via `/reports/export`, the only Word-export surface the new hub exposes) against a fixture soil sample seeded with K=2ppm (well below any real threshold's 50% floor) on the "New test - location" scratch site; the actual rendered `.docx` text reads exactly `"Severe K deficiency - apply potassium sulphate promptly."` with no `kg/ha` figure and no "verified in this system"/"label rate" text anywhere in the document — confirmed the Combined export pipeline (`word-export-combined.js` calling `we.buildSections()` per sample, which is the same `generatePriorityActions()` code path this file's own `REVIEW-GH349-onward.md` open question 7 had flagged as "not exercised live via the new hub's UI this session") is in fact reachable and live through the new hub today. Fixture sample deleted afterward, confirmed via direct DB read (site back to 18 samples). Full suite: 119 PHPUnit / 1577 Jest, no regressions (baseline 115/1574 plus this ticket's 4 new PHPUnit + 3 new Jest tests).

**GH-376** D31 stage 1 — extract a shared pure computation core for the removal/correction/ceiling half of the nutrient requirement, **alongside** the two existing engines rather than replacing either. New `assets/nutrition-requirement-core.js` covers AA/SLAN/MLSN branching, the pH-corrected P ladder, the tissue-ratio gate, the ppm→kg/ha conversion and the correction terms, shaped like `nutrition-requirement-engine.js`'s already-pure `compute(inputs)` contract (no `window`/DOM reads — `nutrition-calendar.js`'s equivalent is not pure and its global lookups belong in a caller-side adapter a later stage will build). Nothing in the app routes through it yet; the cutover is a separate stage gated on `tests/e2e/ui-vs-export-parity.test.js`. **Three-way parity, the point of this stage:** on the real Hoxton-equivalent fixture (`tests/fixtures/test5-soccer-sample141.json`, above ceiling) all three agree at 0 — the ceiling/floor logic, unit conversion and tissue gate are genuinely unified already. The divergence the client reported reproduces exactly: at Hoxton's real annualN=200 against perennial ryegrass's table constant of 180, the core and `nutrition-calendar.js` both give P=20.0 (the client's quoted UI figure) while `nutrition-requirement-engine.js` gives 18.0 (his quoted export figure), ~10% on K likewise — the N basis, not the tissue ratio, is what GH-368/369 left unclosed (they unified *which* ratio, never *what N it scales against*). Two further divergence sources found that appear in neither the audit nor the plan: `nutrition-requirement-engine.js` applies a pH-corrected floor ladder for P that `nutrition-calendar.js` lacks entirely (flat floor at any pH — ~13 kg/ha P apart at pH 5.0, a *larger* effect than the N basis), and the generic P/K ratio is flat across all species in one engine and a 12-species table in the other (~1%). Disputed constants resolved where defensible and flagged where not: N basis taken as the caller's real target (an engineering call — the engine already prioritises it for `facility.annualN`, using the static table only for the tissue ratio was its own internal inconsistency); species ratio table taken over the flat constant (fuller, same source family); clipping-collection factors taken from `nutrition-calendar.js` (it carries real citations — Kopp & Guillard 2002, Qian et al. 2003 — where the other cites only its own predecessor file) **but flagged for confirmation**, since that file's own comment claims those factors are "not currently applied" while the code applies them; **traffic modifiers deliberately left neutral at 1.0 and NOT resolved** — neither file cites any source for its numbers, so both candidate tables are exported for comparison rather than one being silently inherited, per this project's rule against unverified agronomic numbers. Also confirmed, not merged: the GP-weighted monthly-N-distribution logic is genuinely duplicated across both engines for the core formula, but they are not interchangeable (`nutrition-calendar.js` has `even`/`front_loaded` modes the other lacks, with `front_loaded` hardcoded southern-hemisphere; `nutrition-requirement-engine.js` has per-month C3/C4 blending for oversown surfaces the other lacks; and they index months 0-11 vs 1-12, the seam that already broke once in GH-363) — a candidate for the same treatment in a later stage, out of scope here. New `tests/gh376-nutrition-requirement-core.test.js` (22) and `tests/gh376-three-way-nutrition-parity.test.js` (12, running all three implementations over shared inputs with the divergence table documented in its header). No existing engine touched, no behaviour changed.

**GH-377** Follow-up to GH-371 (D01): coordinates were not the only input the cached nutrition programme depends on — a grass-species or methodology change left a programme computed under the old values rendering as valid (species drives the C3/C4 growth-potential curve, the removal table and `deriveCode()`'s certificate ranges; methodology selects the MLSN/SLAN/AA threshold family). The concrete resurrecting path is GH-371's own strip-then-carry-forward: Settings' turf form and the import-bundle flow (`settings-init.js` `applySiteConfig()`) PUT a new `turf` with no stamp, and `resolveGaipConfigWrite()` carries the DB programme forward next to it. `computeProgram()` already stamps `meta.species` (nutrient-engine key) and `meta.methodology` on every compute — all 10 real cached programmes in the dev DB carry them (only 3 carry the GH-371 lat/lon stamp) — so this adds a second comparison next to the coordinate one at the same points: `restoreFromPersisted()` (new "Site configuration changed" banner naming the field and the from/to values, same "please regenerate" shape as GH-371's), `restoreConfig()` (same-blob, `config.turf` vs `meta`), `snapshotConfig()`'s carry-forward, and `word-export.js`'s Monthly Schedule read — via two shared helpers in `nutrition-calendar.js` (`collectProgramInputCandidates()`, `programInputsDrift()`), not four copies. Compared: species and methodology only. Deliberately not compared, with the reasons in the code: `surfaceType` (not consumed by `computeProgram()`, and resolved differently per page), `distribution`/`clippingManagement` (Plan-form inputs that `restoreFromPersisted()` itself restores FROM meta after the check), `hemisphere` (covered by the coordinate check), `speciesDisplay` (the key is what computes). Forward-looking like GH-371: an absent stamp field or an unresolvable current value (new `_methodologyDefaulted` flag on `collectFromState()`, alongside the existing `_speciesDefaulted`; the hub store's placeholder `'mlsn'` counts as unresolved) means trust as-is. Over-invalidation guards: the stamped species key is compared raw against a normalised SET of current keys (effective + base + overseed, because hub pages can legitimately feed the overseed species while Plan feeds the base) — never re-normalised, since `toNutrientKey()` is not idempotent (browntop → `creepingBentgrass` → `bentgrass`, which would have flagged Russley on every load); methodology aliases are folded and empty/`mlsn` on NZ coordinates resolves to AA exactly as the Plan bridge does; `snapshotConfig()` skips the check while a restore cascade is in flight and only drops when the saved identity AND the DOM both disagree with the stamp (the DOM species is transiently another site's during the b35fix504 cascade race). Server side untouched (`SiteController.php` is being edited concurrently; the unstamped-fresh-programme item from GH-371's review stays deferred). **Live-verified** on the "New test - location" scratch site: the pre-existing 2026-08-26 cached programme (no coordinate stamp) still restored without a nag; a fresh Plan generation stamped `bentgrass`/`SLAN`; changing the species to Couch through the real Settings turf form left `turf.species = "Couch"` next to `meta.species = "bentgrass"` in the DB (server carry-forward, confirmed by direct read), after which Plan showed the banner instead of the stale numbers (`GAIP_NUTRITION_PROGRAM_UNAVAILABLE = true`), `/reports/export` refused to restore either cached global (`[SiteConfig] GH-377` warning) and a real Combined Word export printed "no fertiliser programme found for this site" with the per-sample recompute reporting `skipped=2` (no annual-N target without the cache) rather than the bentgrass programme — the control export with nothing changed restored both globals, recomputed `ok=2` and printed Nutrition Program / Annual Product Summary / Monthly Schedule; reverting the species restored the SAME cached blob (identical `meta.generated` and monthly N series) with no banner; the methodology cycle (SLAN → MLSN → SLAN) behaved identically. Config row restored byte-identical from the pre-run backup (md5 match), the login user's `last_active_site_id` restored, both confirmed by direct read. New `tests/gh377-program-input-invalidation.test.js` (52 tests: the six real DB shapes as no-change fixtures, species/methodology/both changes, absent and partial meta, unresolved current state, overseed and NZ tolerances, the real `plan.blade.php` bridge run via `vm` for the Plan path, the exact post-import blob through the real `restoreConfig()`, `snapshotConfig()`'s transient-DOM and in-cascade cases, structural pins for `word-export.js` and `applySiteConfig()`); `tests/gh371-d01-coordinate-invalidation.test.js`'s accept-condition pin amended for the second flag. Full suite at this ticket's final run: 1668 Jest / 119 PHPUnit, no regressions (baseline 1611 / 119; +52 from this ticket, +5 from `tests/gh370-real-data-belowfloor-test5-verified.test.js`, which landed concurrently from a different ticket; no PHP file edited here). **Review fix (same ticket):** on hub pages the check compares the programme against the hub's *local* copy of the turf, but `site-config-persistence.js`'s `pullConfigsFromServer()` refreshed only `species`/`turfType`/`variety`/… from a newer server config — never `turf.methodology` or the overseed/base-species fields — while always taking the programme keys. On a device whose cached config predated a Settings methodology change, a programme freshly regenerated on Plan therefore arrived next to the old methodology and every hub page (`/reports/export`, scenarios, forensic, hub) refused it as stale. Reproduced live on `/reports/export` by putting the exact DB state Settings + Plan leave behind on the scratch site (`[SiteConfig] GH-377: … computed for methodology=mlsn but this site's saved config now says methodology=slan`, docx recomputed under the stale SLAN; row restored byte-identical afterwards, md5-confirmed); fixed by adding `methodology`, `overseedSpecies`, `coolOverseed`, `warmBase` to the pull's newer-server identity list — the live re-run synced `mlsn`, logged nothing and printed the MLSN programme. Three tests added to `tests/gh377-program-input-invalidation.test.js` (55 total), two of which fail against the pre-fix list. Known limit, unchanged: the pull's "server is newer" rule keys on `savedAt`, which Settings alone does not bump (Plan's and the hub's own programme writes do), so a Settings-only change with no regenerate still reaches other devices' caches only when something later bumps it — in that case the cached programme and turf are stale together and nothing is refused.

**GH-378** A cross-site Combined export blanked the soil section of the first sample after every site switch — "No soil test data - MLSN recommendations estimated" and no Annual Nutrient Requirements row, while the data sat in the DB. This is what the Hoxton review's open question 8 (the Burns "Rye Nursery" K=7.6 and "12th Fairway" K=195 samples) turned out to be; it is not a Burns data-shape problem and not specific to that site. Mechanism, traced live with a value-setter hook on the `[data-mlsn="K"]` input and a stack hook on `document.dispatchEvent`: the export loop calls `sm.setActiveSite(B)`, `sample-manager.js` announces `gaip:site-changed`, `gaip-clear-data.js` clears the soil/water inputs (harmless — the sample is not loaded yet), and `site-selector-ui.js`'s listener returns early because `GAIP_COMBINED_EXPORT_ACTIVE` is set — **without recording the announced site in its own `_lastDispatchedSiteId` shadow**. `loadSample('soil', …)` then populates the inputs. ~450ms later `gaip:site-config-applied` fires, `updateUI()` compares the now-active site B against its stale shadow (still A), concludes the site changed, and dispatches a **duplicate** `gaip:site-changed` — so `gaip-clear-data.js` wipes the freshly-populated inputs a second time, and `collectData()`'s DOM fallback finds nothing, leaving `soil.hasData` unset. Fix: the listener records the announced site into `_lastDispatchedSiteId` before its export early-return, so `updateUI()` never re-announces a switch `SampleManager` already announced. `updateUI()`'s own dispatch is deliberately kept — it remains the only announcement on the page-load restore path, where `restoreFromPersistence()` switches silently. New `tests/gh378-no-duplicate-site-changed-during-export.test.js` (5 tests) loads the real `sample-manager.js`, `site-selector-ui.js` and `gaip-clear-data.js` on a small fake DOM with a working event bus and replays the export loop's exact ordering; red-checked against the pre-fix module via `GH378_SITE_SELECTOR_PATH` (3 of the 5 fail there, the page-load one correctly still passes). **Live-verified end-to-end (review pass)** with real Combined exports from `/reports/export` on the dev stack (active site Test5-NZ, no `last_active_site_id` change needed or made): a cross-site export of Burns "Rye Nursery" + "12th Fairway" + Test5 "Soccer" announced each site switch exactly once (`gaip:site-changed` counted from the page) and rendered every sample's real soil values — Rye Nursery K 7.6 / Ca 51.9 / Mg 18.3 ppm with its Annual Nutrient Requirements row (K 8 ppm, K req 113.5 kg/ha), 12th Fairway K 195 / P 18 ppm (P req 24.5, K req 0.0), Soccer as before; the identical export against the pre-fix module (`a1ec2d6` `site-selector-ui.js` swapped in, restored afterwards, md5-confirmed) announced every switch twice and blanked the first sample after each switch (Rye Nursery: no soil values and no ANR row, its "Sampled" line showing the water sample's 2018 date; Soccer: "No soil test data", no ANR row, no Monthly Schedule) — open question 8 reproduced exactly. A single-site export of the active site (no switch, zero announcements) is unchanged: soil values, ANR row and Monthly Schedule all present. The page-load restore path is unchanged too — both modules show the same two announcements (SampleManager's server-sync switch at ~180 ms, which lands before this listener is even registered, and `updateUI()`'s `gaip:site-config-applied` one at ~3 s). Two corrections to this entry's original mechanism note: the duplicate did NOT re-run `site-switch-cleanup.js`'s `clearTurfIdentity()` (that listener ignores a repeat announcement of the same site), and the second sample on a switched-to site (12th Fairway) rendered identically before and after — including N 250, which comes from the export page's nutrition-panel N input (the active site's value) and is unrelated to this fix (recorded as a separate observation in `REVIEW-GH349-onward.md`, open question 12).

**GH-379** The Combined Word export's per-sample Monthly Schedule silently computed under MLSN on an AA / Hill Labs S277 site: `word-export.js` stamps `data.soil.methodology` upper-cased (`'AMMONIUM_ACETATE'`), `word-export-combined.js` copied that into the calendar inputs, and `nutrition-calendar.js`'s `computeProgram()` compared it case-sensitively against `'ammonium_acetate'` (the SLAN test a few lines below, `getThresholds()` and `normalizeMethodology()` all fold case), so the AA branch matched nothing and the programme fell through to the MLSN band — K floor 37 instead of the certificate's 78.2, no lift, a different monthly K series into the product recommender, while the same document's Annual Nutrient Requirements, K Reconciliation and Soil Amendment sections all applied the S277 basis. `computeProgram()` now resolves `inputs.methodology` once through `NutritionCalendar.normalizeMethodology()` and branches only on the folded key; `meta.methodology` / `soil.methodology` record that key, and the Generic-badge gate in `renderSummary()` uses the same normaliser. `word-export-combined.js` also folds the value at the per-sample hand-off (defence in depth; the Prebble P-deficiency threshold two hundred lines down keys on the same field), and its cotula S78 placeholder test — `=== 'cotula_s78'` against the always-upper-cased stamp, i.e. dead — now compares case-insensitively (no cotula site in the dev DB, so that half is code-verified only). The live re-check then exposed a second gap the MLSN fall-through had been masking: the export's calendar inputs came from `collectFromState()` on `/reports/export`, where `soilTexture` and `CEC` are both null, so `deriveCode()` could not resolve S277 and the now-reached AA branch ranged against the generic sands/others band (K 100-235, K 151) while the same report's ANR had already resolved S277 per sample; `_buildEngineInputs()` now surfaces the per-sample texture it resolved (`engineInputs.soilTexture`) and the combined loop overlays it plus the sample's own `data.soil.CEC` onto the calendar inputs, only when resolved. **Live** (Test5 - NZ / Soccer, sample 141 soil K 40, sample 144 tissue K 2%): before — no `[GH302-DEBUG] AA ceiling check` line, monthly K Σ109, April `Country Club 24-0-10 @ 121 kg/ha ×2`, Annual Product Summary MESA ×4 (716 kg/ha, K 115) + Country Club 24-0-10 ×2 (242, K 24), K delivered 151; after — `AA ceiling check … deriveCode result: S277`, K range 78.2-195.5 certificate, lift 26.74, K 136, monthly K series identical to the Plan page's rendered row (16.5/16.5/16.3/14.2/10.4/6.7/4.9/5.4/7.2/9.5/12.7/15.7), April `MESA Country Club 100% @ 152 kg/ha ×2` (the Plan page's own April pick), Annual Product Summary MESA ×6 (1020 kg/ha, K 163), K delivered 175 (Plan 175.2). ANR K req still prints 105.5 (the D31 / GH-376 N-basis divergence, unchanged, out of scope). Sample rows and `last_active_site_id` untouched (md5-confirmed); the only DB write was the app's own page-load re-stamp of `nutritionProgram.meta.generated`/`savedAt` (content identical). New `tests/gh379-methodology-case-invariance.test.js` (35 tests: nine AA spellings, SLAN/MLSN pairs, stamps, the texture/CEC gap, structural pins for both export files); red-checked via `GH379_*_PATH` against the pre-fix modules — 30 of 35 fail there. `tests/gh304-nutrition-calendar-range-source.test.js`'s badge pin updated to the normaliser form. Also looked at and deliberately left: `mulders-interaction-checker.js`'s `normaliseToBasis()` (`=== "ammonium_acetate"`, fed the upper-cased stamp by both export files, so its cmol/kg→mg/kg AA conversion never runs in the export — which is the correct outcome for this hub's mg/kg-stored AA values; recorded as an open question in `REVIEW-GH349-onward.md`). Suite: 1711 Jest (5 skipped) / 120 PHPUnit, baseline 1676 / 120, no regressions.

**GH-380** The E2E UI-vs-export parity harness (`tests/e2e/ui-vs-export-parity.test.js`, Hoxton assertion 20) had been reporting nothing while the Plan page and the Combined Word export disagreed by 30 kg/ha of K on Test5 - NZ / Soccer, and each reason was verified rather than assumed: `require('playwright')` never resolved from the repo (the only install lived in a scratch directory), so with `GILBA_E2E=1` it printed "playwright is not installed" and skipped — every full-suite "5 skipped" was this file; `.github/workflows/deploy-test-ec2.yml` never runs Jest at all; its one recorded green run (2026-09-07) was made at soil K 276 ppm, above the ceiling, where every engine returns 0 and a divergence cannot show; it exported whichever sample matched a search term on whichever site happened to be active; and it compared only the Annual Requirements cards against the ANR table (that check alone would have caught today's 30.5 kg/ha K gap, but not the Delivered, K Reconciliation, product-selection or threshold divergences GH-379 fixed). Rebuilt. **Runnable**: `playwright` is a pinned devDependency (`1.63.0` exact — the Chromium build already cached on the dev machine, so `npm install` alone makes it resolve; `npx playwright install chromium` once per machine) and `npm run test:e2e` is the one command (`GILBA_E2E=1 jest tests/e2e --runInBand --testTimeout=240000`, with `GILBA_E2E_EMAIL`/`GILBA_E2E_PASSWORD` in the environment). The root `package.json` is test-only tooling (the deploy workflow runs `npm ci` inside `app/`, never at the root), so nothing ships. Plain `npx jest` still skips it, now with one stdout line saying so, and with `GILBA_E2E=1` set any blocker — playwright or its browser missing, credentials missing, login refused, fixture absent from the stack — is a failure, never a skip. **Deterministic**: `tests/fixtures/e2e-parity-test5-soccer.json` pins the site (Test5 - NZ) and sample (soil 141 "Soccer", client id `sample_141`, tissue 144) with their current inputs (soil K 40 / P 40, tissue K 2 %, as edited through the UI on 2026-09-09); the harness switches the login's `last_active_site_id` to the fixture site through `PATCH /api/active-site` and restores it afterwards, ticks exactly that sample's checkbox in the export picker by `data-sample-uid`, checks the ppm values it sees on both surfaces against the fixture (drift is reported as drift, not as a parity failure), and refuses its precondition unless the sample is below the floor the Plan page itself resolved. **Wider**: it reads the Plan's structured programme (`GilbaNutritionCalendar.program` and `GAIP_NUTRITION_PROGRAM`, the distributor-aware panel the user sees) plus its rendered text, hooks `computeProgram()`, `NutritionRequirementEngine_Pure.compute()` and `PrebbleRecommender.generateProgram()` on `/reports/export` (observation only, every wrapper calls through) to capture the inputs and outputs the .docx never prints, parses the .docx tables structurally instead of positionally, and asserts, each as its own test: the methodology branch, floors/ceilings and range sources both surfaces resolved (the check that catches the GH-379 class directly); the inputs that reached each engine (species key, annual N, soil ppm, tissue, bulk density/depth); the export's calendar branch against the Plan programme (removal, lift, totals — same function, must be equal); the GH-370 correction term; Annual Requirements cards vs the ANR table (assertion 20); Removal/Required vs the engine and the K Reconciliation row; Delivered vs "K delivered" and the Annual Product Summary; product ids, application counts, totals and the month-by-month schedule on both surfaces; that each surface's "Balance" is the arithmetic of its own definition (Plan: projected soil pool = Current + Delivered − Removal; export: programme − requirement — legitimately different quantities, documented in the test, not forced equal); and catalogue membership (assertion 14). **Real run** (33 s against the dev stack): 12 pass, 2 fail — Plan K card 136 vs export ANR K req 105.5; P removal 34 vs 24.4, K removal 109 vs 78.8, K req 136 vs 105.5 on the K Reconciliation row as well — the D31 N-basis divergence (the engine scales the shared tissue ratio against the species table's N 180, the calendar against the site's real 250), left failing on purpose with the tolerance untouched; thresholds (S277 certificate 78.2–195.5 on both surfaces), the calendar branch, the 26.74 lift, delivered K 175, the products (MESA Country Club 100% ×6 / 1020 kg/ha, Pro Balance ×2, Ammos 22 ×1 + ×2 balance) and the monthly schedule all agree. `tests/gh370-real-data-belowfloor-test5-verified.test.js` no longer pins that gap as expected behaviour (its `removal ratio = 250/180` assertion is gone); the honest engine-vs-calendar parity assertion is there as a `test.failing`, which passes only while the body throws and fails the moment the engines agree, so the default suite stays green and the flip forces a promotion to a plain `test` when D31 stage 2 lands. **CI**: not changed. Running the harness in the existing workflow would need a compose stack on the runner, a seeded fixture user/site/sample (no seeder for Test5 exists; `DatabaseSeeder` creates one precinct site), outbound access to NASA POWER for the climate normals the Plan generation fetches, `npx playwright install --with-deps chromium`, and a separate non-gating job (it is red by design until D31 stage 2) — sensible as a manual/nightly job gating the GH-376 cutover PR, not as part of the deploy job. Suite: 1712 Jest passed / 1 skipped (the harness's own placeholder) / 120 PHPUnit.

**GH-381** Closed D31's N-basis divergence — the specific gap GH-380's live harness run caught (Plan K card 136 vs export ANR K req 105.5, P removal 34 vs 24.4, K removal 109 vs 78.8). Root cause: `nutrition-requirement-engine.js`'s `getRemovalRate()` scaled removal — both the tissue-informed branch (GH-368) and the generic-ratio branch — against `REMOVAL_RATES[species].N` (the species table's own N, e.g. 180 for perennial ryegrass) instead of the site's real annual N target, while `nutrition-calendar.js`'s `computeProgram()` has always scaled `baseRemoval[nutrient] = annualN * ratio` unconditionally against the real target. This was the same file's own internal inconsistency, not a different engineering choice: `compute()`'s facility-level calculation already resolves the real `annualN` (three-tier: explicit `turf.nProgramKgHaYr` override, then the species table default, then `mixedCool.N` as a last resort) — the per-sample `nutrientConfig` simply never received it. The table's generic entries were never a flat absolute number either; every species keeps the same implied ratio the calendar's `CONFIG.nutrientRatiosToN` carries as an explicit constant (perennialRyegrass P/N 18/180=0.10, K/N 100/180=0.5556, matching the calendar's 0.10/0.55 almost exactly) — so scaling the generic branch by the real annualN the same way as the tissue branch is a correction of the existing table's own intent, not a new one. Fixed by resolving `annualN` once, before the per-sample config is built, threading it into `nutrientConfig.annualN` and then into `getRemovalRate(species, nutrient, tissueGate, annualN)`, which now derives a ratio for both branches (tissue-measured when eligible, else the table's own implied `nutrient/N` ratio) and scales it against the real annualN — falling back to the table's own N only if a caller omits annualN entirely, so nothing crashes on a stale call site. On the live below-floor fixture (Test5 - NZ, soil K 40 ppm, tissue-gated) the engine now returns K 84.1 / P 40.9, matching the GH-376 shared core exactly (which already resolved the real annualN correctly from the start — confirming this was specifically the standalone engine's own gap) and closely tracking the calendar's rounded 84/41 (the small residual is generic-ratio precision, 0.5556 vs 0.55, not a basis mismatch). `tests/gh370-real-data-belowfloor-test5-verified.test.js` updated: its hardcoded pre-fix expectations (removal 41.4/24.4, K req 68.1, P req 31.4) replaced with the recomputed post-fix figures (verified via a direct `Engine.compute()` call against the same fixture inputs, not re-captured live in this pass — the original pre-fix live numbers are kept in the fixture JSON's `_preGh381LiveRender` for history); its `test.failing` D31-parity assertion — written to fail until this exact gap closed — now passes and is promoted to a plain `test`, per the comment that put it there. **Live-verified end to end**: `npm run test:e2e` (GH-380's harness) against the real running stack — previously 12 passed / 2 failed on this exact defect — now reports **14 passed / 14 total**, no tolerance widened, no assertion removed. Full local suite: 1712 Jest passed / 1 skipped, 120 PHPUnit, no regressions. **What remains open, deliberately untouched here**: two further GH-376 divergence items neither this fix nor this fixture's AA methodology exercise — the pH-corrected P floor ladder that exists only in `nutrition-requirement-engine.js`/the shared core (`nutrition-calendar.js` has none at all), and the two engines' own differing, uncited traffic modifiers. Both need the user's agronomic sign-off, not an engineering call, before either is touched.

**GH-382** Closed one of the two GH-381 leftovers: `nutrition-calendar.js`'s SLAN P floor was flat (27 ppm, the pH-independent Carrow 2004 baseline) at every soil pH, while `nutrition-requirement-engine.js` (and the GH-376 shared core) have long applied a real, cited pH adjustment — the "Spencer scaled-ladder" method (Carrow et al. 2004 GCM 72(1):194-198 for the 27ppm floor, Carrow/Waddington/Rieke 2001 for the pH x P-availability ratios) — so a Plan-page SLAN site away from neutral pH used a different P floor than the export for the identical sample. First attempt touched `NutritionCalendar.getThresholds()`'s SLAN branch and was live-traced to be dead code for this specific bug: SLAN's P floor for the actual correction-term calculation comes from a separate block in `computeProgram()` that populates `aaRanges.P` unconditionally, so `getThresholds()` is never reached for SLAN's P at all. Reverted that dead branch; the real fix sits in the `aaRanges` population block itself, calling the engine's own exported, already-cited `NutritionRequirementEngine_Pure._getSlanTargetP(ph)` — the same pattern `word-export.js` already uses at its own `_pHForSlanP` call site — rather than copying the ladder a third time, so there remains exactly one real implementation of the ladder. Degrades to the pre-fix flat floor when the engine script isn't loaded or pH isn't a usable number, never guesses a pH. `collectFromState()` now resolves `inputs.pH` (`soil.pH_water` preferred, matching `word-export.js`'s own established resolution order, falling back to a generic `soil.pH`, else `null`) — pH was not read into the calendar's inputs anywhere before this. Scope is P only for SLAN; K/Ca/Mg/S and the AA/MLSN branches are untouched. New `tests/gh382-slan-p-floor-ph-ladder.test.js` (10 tests) loads the real engine so `_getSlanTargetP` is the genuine cited function, not a re-implementation of its ratio table; cross-checks the calendar's resolved floor against the engine's own output at five pH bands; red-checked (3 of 10 fail against the pre-fix code). **Live-verified**: `npm run test:e2e` — 14 passed / 14 total, matching GH-381's result, confirming this change didn't regress anything the harness already covered. Full suite: 1722 Jest passed / 1 skipped, 120 PHPUnit, no regressions. Traffic modifiers and the clipping-management factor (the other GH-381 leftover, plus the newly-found scope divergence — the engine applies neither to its AA/SLAN branches at all, only MLSN, while the calendar applies both everywhere) are still open, in progress as GH-383/384.

**Corrections to this entry, found later during D31 stage-2 planning — recorded here rather than silently amended above, because both were stated as fact and acted on.** (1) This entry originally claimed the P scope was SLAN-only "since neither engine applies a pH adjustment to them either". That is wrong: `nutrition-requirement-engine.js:43-64` (and the GH-376 core) carry a *second*, separate pH ladder for MLSN's P threshold — 35/28/21/32/40 ppm at pH ≤5.5 / ≤6.0 / 6.0-7.5 / ≤8.0 / above, labelled a "Gilba augmentation" with no external citation, inherited from the old Nutrition Summary panel — while `nutrition-calendar.js` holds a flat MLSN P threshold of 21 at any pH. So the same UI-vs-export divergence this entry closed for SLAN remains open for MLSN, in the same direction, and was never in this fix's scope. (2) The "live-verified, 14/14" claim is true of the harness but overstates what shipped: `plan.blade.php` does not load `nutrition-requirement-engine.js` or `gaip-classification-constants.js`, so on the live Plan page the new call falls into this fix's own graceful-degradation path and resolves the flat 27 floor. The fix is real and correct in the calendar module; it is inert on the page it was written for until those scripts are loaded there. The harness passed because Test5 - NZ is an AA site, where neither the SLAN ladder nor its absence is reachable. Both are resolved by the D31 unification (one engine, loaded on both surfaces); the MLSN ladder's fate is a user decision recorded in `files/fixes/26-08-17-hoxton-v6/PLAN-D31-unify-engines.md` as D-7.

**GH-383** D31 stage 0 + 1 of the "one requirement engine" unification (`files/fixes/26-08-17-hoxton-v6/PLAN-D31-unify-engines.md`): the Word export now computes its per-nutrient requirements through the shared core `assets/nutrition-requirement-core.js`, and both surfaces resolve every programme-level input through one new module, `assets/nutrition-program-inputs.js`. The core, landed unused at GH-376, was finished first: clipping management became the calendar's string vocabulary with `collected` as the default (GH-376 shipped `!!clippingsCollected`, so an absent value meant `returned` and silently halved P/K removal); traffic was removed from the per-nutrient path entirely, because the modifier scales the annual N upstream and removal already scales with N, so a second application would double-count it; sufficiency ranges became a required, caller-resolved input for all three methodologies, which removed the last `window.GilbaClassificationConstants` read and made the module fully pure; a nutrient with no soil reading is now returned as removal-only with `missingSoilData` set instead of being omitted (D-9); the ceiling comparison is `>=` on every methodology (D-8); and the below-floor lift target is the floor itself rather than 1.5x the MLSN minimum (D-6) — the one deliberate numeric move, aligning the export to the Plan page, which takes MLSN calcium on the dev sites from roughly 125 to 48 kg/ha and Burns 12th Fairway's P requirement from 21.5 to 14.1. `nutrition-requirement-engine.js` became a facade: its public `compute()` and the facility GP distribution are unchanged, and `_calculateNutrientRequirement`, `REMOVAL_RATES`, `MLSN_THRESHOLDS`, the pH ladders, the tissue gate and the clipping table are now lazy re-exports of the core's, so there is one copy of each in the product. The old `CLIPPING_COLLECTION_FACTOR = 2.5` is gone — uncited, MLSN-only, and the opposite polarity (it amplified on collected instead of reducing on returned); the legacy boolean `turf.clippingsCollected` is ignored rather than aliased, because it has no writer anywhere in `assets/` or `app/` and has therefore always arrived as false, so aliasing false to `returned` would have halved K removal for the old hub's panel and for every existing caller — ignoring it keeps the factor at 1.0, the number those callers already get. The new adapter is the only module allowed to read site config, sample state or the DOM for annual N, clipping, traffic, turf type, species, methodology, texture, CEC, pH and the sufficiency ranges, and it stamps a provenance source per field; `word-export.js`'s `_buildEngineInputs()` lost its 145-line AA-range IIFE (the GH-352/353/355/357/364/379 seam), its `GAIP_STATE.turf.clippingsCollected` and `GAIP_STATE.traffic.intensity` reads (neither field has ever had a writer) and its `.gaip-nutrition-annual-n` / `.gaip-n-program` DOM reads. That last pair was the cross-site annual-N leak: the hidden legacy input is filled by `restoreFromPersisted()` with the first restored site's `target_n` and then not refilled, so every later site in a multi-site Combined export inherited it — Burns samples printed 250 kg N/ha in a Test5-active export instead of their own 120. `word-export-combined.js`'s per-sample calendar branch now builds its inputs from the same adapter keyed by `r.siteId`, which also retires the `parseFloat(...) || 0` soil-ppm block that turned a missing reading into 0 ppm, i.e. maximally deficient against every floor — the failure GH-338 fixed on the Plan page and left standing in the export. A site whose inputs cannot be resolved is now skipped with a warning rather than computed against another site's configuration. The calendar persists `meta.annualNBase` (the pre-traffic figure) and `meta.inputSources`, and restores the Annual N input from the base rather than from the traffic-adjusted `adjustments.target_n`, which would otherwise compound the modifier on every regenerate; its two false comments — the clipping factors described as "not currently applied" when STEP 3 has always applied them — are corrected. `nutrition-requirement-core.js`, `nutrition-program-inputs.js` and (on Plan) `gaip-classification-constants.js` are enqueued on plan, reports/export, scenarios, forensic and hub, head-like on the four bundle pages so they execute before the deferred scripts. The Plan page's own arithmetic is unchanged in this ticket — `computeProgram()` still computes its own removal and correction; that is GH-384. Deliberately not done: the annual N shown on the Plan form is still only what the user typed, never seeded from Settings > Turf (D-4b's second half, declined); traffic derivation and persistence remain unwired (`deriveTrafficIntensity()` returns moderate/1.0 with `source: 'not-wired'`). New tests: `gh383-nutrition-program-inputs.test.js` (34), `gh383-clipping-uniform-all-methodologies.test.js` (17, reusing the stopped work's cases against the real modules), `gh383-input-contract-guard.test.js` (14, source-text guard on the retired reads); `gh376-nutrition-requirement-core.test.js` rewritten to the new contract (30) and `gh376-three-way-nutrition-parity.test.js` rewritten as a two-way surface comparison, with five assertions marked `test.failing` because they are the GH-384 gate. Ten red-checks recorded in the ticket's report, each breaking one decision and naming the test that caught it. **Live-verified**: `npm run test:e2e` 14/14 with the Test5 - NZ fixture at `clippingManagement: 'returned'` AND at `'collected'` — the `returned` state reported 12/14 before this change (K required 82 on the Plan vs 136.1 in the export, P removal 14 vs 33.9), which is the pure input divergence this stage closes. Also implemented from decision D-4b: when a site's annual N could not come from a generated programme, the Combined export prints a note above the Annual Nutrient Requirements table naming the site and the source it fell back to (Site Settings → Turf, or the species default) and saying that no programme has been generated — gated on the adapter's own `sources.annualN` stamp, so it can never claim a fallback that did not happen. The other half of D-4b was declined by the user and is not implemented: the Plan page's own Annual N field is still never seeded from Settings.
Full suite 1802 Jest passed / 1 skipped / 158 suites (baseline 1722 / 155), 120 PHPUnit unchanged.

**GH-384** D31 stage 2 — the Plan page's `computeProgram()` now delegates STEPS 2-5 (base removal, the clipping factor, the deficit/lift correction and the ceiling) to `assets/nutrition-requirement-core.js`, the same module GH-383 put the Word export on, so the audit's D31 finding — "two requirement engines, one product" — is closed: there is one implementation of the per-nutrient requirement, one range resolver and one input adapter behind both surfaces. What the calendar keeps is everything the core deliberately excludes: the traffic-adjusted annual N, the GP-weighted monthly distribution, the monthly N cap, the monthly programme build and the whole persisted output shape (`annual_totals`, `annual_removal`, `annual_lift`, `annual_totals_range`, `annual_totals_range_source`, `missing_soil_data`, `tissue_gate_applied`, `adjustments`, `meta`, `soil`, `program`), which the regional integrations, `plan-ui.js`, `word-export*.js` and `k-reconciliation-decision.js` read unchanged. `annual_totals_range` is built explicitly as `{min,max}` rather than handed the resolver's own object, which also carries a methodology label and a citation that are not part of that contract. Four settled decisions move Plan-page numbers, each re-derived by hand in the tests rather than read back off the new code. **D-5** — the generic (non-tissue) removal ratio is now the per-species `REMOVAL_RATES` table (Carrow/Waddington/Rieke 2001; Christians/Patton/Law 2017) instead of one flat set applied to every species (P 0.10 / K 0.55 / Ca 0.17 / Mg 0.08 / S 0.05, Turner & Hummel 1992, a single cool-season composition). P/N is 0.10 in every table row so P does not move; at N 200 perennial ryegrass K goes 110 → 111 (100/180 = 0.5556), fine fescue 110 → 120 (60/100 = 0.60), buffalo +14%, bentgrass −3%; Ca/Mg/S move on every site because tissue never governs them (at N 200: Ca 34 → 33, Mg 16 → 17, S 10 → 11). The retired numbers are kept in `CONFIG` under `_retiredNutrientRatiosToN_gh384`, unreferenced, as the record of what this page printed before. **D-6** — below the floor the lift target is the floor itself, which is what this page always did; asserted explicitly so the cutover could not silently adopt the engine's old 1.5× target. **D-7** — the MLSN P threshold gains the pH ladder (35/28/21/32/40 at pH ≤5.5 / ≤6.0 / 6.0-7.5 / ≤8.0 / above) that the engine has carried for years and this page never had; no stored dev sample sits outside the 6.0-7.5 band, so nothing moves today, and GH-382's correction note about this is now closed. **D-8** — a reading exactly at the ceiling applies zero, on all three methodologies, which is also what this page already did. Rounding: the core is canonical at 0.1 kg/ha and this page displays whole kg, at the same two points it always rounded, so a Plan total can differ from the export's one-decimal figure by 1 kg/ha — the residual is asserted at ±1 in the parity test, never absorbed by widening a tolerance. `calculateDeficit()` and `getThresholds()` remain exported for their direct callers but are no longer reached by `computeProgram()`. Also fixed: the Combined export's K Reconciliation caption still read "lift toward target × 1.5 over 2 years" after the arithmetic changed, which would have described the printed MLSN figure incorrectly to the client. Tests: new `gh384-plan-page-through-core.test.js` (16); `gh376-three-way-nutrition-parity.test.js`'s five stage-2 gates promoted from `test.failing` to plain assertions; `gh361`, `gh338`, `gh311`, `gh319`, `gh379`, `gh382` re-derived or re-pointed, with the arithmetic written into each. Four further red-checks recorded in the ticket's report. **Live-verified on three sites with three different methodologies, Plan page against the rendered .docx, not only the harness fixture**: Test5 - NZ (AA) 14/14 at `clippingManagement` `collected` AND `returned` — at `returned` the Plan's K required is 81 and the export's ANR "K req" 81.4, where before GH-383 they were 82 and 136.1; Burns 12th Fairway (MLSN, P 18 ppm below the 21 ppm minimum) 14/14, Plan P required 14 and export ANR "P req" 14.1, where the export printed 21.5 before decision D-6; New test - location "Putter Green" (SLAN, P 23.81 and K 19.31 both below floor) 14/14, Plan K 103 / P 14 / S 8 against export ANR K req 103.0 / P req 14.2 / S req 7.9. Two new fixtures (`e2e-parity-burns-12th-fairway-mlsn.json`, `e2e-parity-new-test-location-slan.json`) and four harness corrections the new fixtures exposed: the AU/UK product recommenders are hooked as well as the NZ one; the engine's resolved ranges are read from `ranges` (which covers all three methodologies) rather than the AA-only `aaRanges`; the ANR table's printed soil ppm is compared at whole-number precision because the renderer rounds it; and the Plan page's soil sample is now selected through its own picker, because on a multi-sample site the two surfaces were otherwise compared on different samples. **Two findings recorded, neither fixed here**: the Plan page's sample picker never restores a remembered selection — `soil-nutrition-analysis.js`'s `mountSampleDropdown` compares `samples[j].id === persistedId` with `===`, and the id from `/api/samples` is a number while anything read back from `localStorage` is a string, so the comparison is always false; and on Burns the Plan page and the Combined export select different product catalogues (Plan: ICL-SPORTSMASTER3 / SOL-AS / VAR-MAPTECH; document: MESA 30, Greenmaster Liquid, Wilbur Ellis GroMaxx 46, FoliMAX N-Hancer-N), a pre-existing regional-catalogue question in the GH-362/D30 family that the engine unification does not touch — the two new fixtures declare `productParity.asserted: false` with that reason in the fixture itself rather than the harness quietly skipping the rows. Also verified live on `/hub`: the old hub's Nutrition Summary panel renders through the engine facade with no page errors and the same figures. Full suite 1818 Jest passed / 1 skipped / 159 suites (baseline 1722 / 155), 120 PHPUnit unchanged. Still open by design: traffic derivation and persistence (stage 3) and the GP-weighted monthly distribution, which remains duplicated between the calendar and the engine (stage 4).

**GH-385** `snapshotConfig()` compared two different sites and threw both their nutrition programmes away on every site switch. `saveCurrentSiteConfig()` snapshots the site being switched AWAY from, but by the time it runs `GAIP_SampleManager` and the DOM already describe the site being switched TO — so the carry-forward block at the bottom of `snapshotConfig()` read `_configs[getActiveSiteId()]`, i.e. the ARRIVING site's stored config and its nutrition-programme coordinate stamp, and compared that stamp against `.gaip-lat`/`.gaip-lon`, which still held the DEPARTING site's coordinates. Two different sites on the two sides of one comparison: GH-371's "the coordinates moved, drop the cached programme" rule fired every single time, and the emptied result was then written into the departing site's slot. This is `REVIEW-GH349-onward.md`'s open question 11, latent since GH-371 and made load-bearing by GH-383 — the Word export now takes each site's annual N from that field, so a transient drop produced both a wrong annual N (Settings → Turf instead of the client's own target) and a client-facing note stating "no nutrition programme has been generated" printed on a site that has one. Reproduced live before fixing, on a two-site Combined export of Test5 - NZ + Burns with the browser instrumented: at t=0 both sites held their programmes (target_n 250 and 120), at t=1382 ms the switch to Burns left Test5 with none, and at t=5042 ms the switch back left Burns with none; the instrumented snapshot logged `coordinates changed for site <Burns> (-35.2285452,149.0022925 -> -36.8508827,174.7644881)` while `previousSiteId` was Test5 — Burns' stamp against Test5's coordinates. Fixed by giving `snapshotConfig()` an optional `forSiteId`: the carry-forward reads the config of the site the snapshot is FOR, and when that is not the active site the location comes from that site's own stored record rather than from a DOM describing somebody else — which is also what `saveCurrentSiteConfig()` substituted immediately afterwards (b35fix169), except that the staleness check runs inside `snapshotConfig()` and so never saw it. A site with no stored location gets an empty one rather than the active site's, because a wrong position drives the climate normals and the regional product catalogue. Every call site now names its site; most were already the active one, but several run inside `setTimeout` callbacks where the active site can have moved on before they fire. GH-371's real invalidation is unchanged and still fires when the ACTIVE site's own coordinates move, and when a named site's own stored coordinates have moved away from its stamp — both asserted. New `tests/gh385-snapshot-config-site-scoped.test.js` (10 tests) encoding the live reproduction verbatim; red-checked three ways (restore the active-site read: 2 fail, `Received: undefined` for the carried programme; restore the DOM location read for a foreign site: 2 fail, `Expected -35.2285452 / Received -36.8508827`; drop the `_previousSiteId` argument: the call-site guard fails). `tests/gh371-*` and `tests/gh377-*` pass unchanged (95 tests across the three files). **Live-verified**: the same instrumented two-site export now shows a single timeline entry — neither programme is ever dropped — and both engine calls resolve `annualNSource: 'plan-persisted'` with each site's own target (Burns 120, Test5 250, previously Test5 resolved `settings-turf`); the D-4b note correctly does not appear. ANR row unchanged at `12th Fairway 120 / P req 14.1` and `Soccer 250 / K req 136.1`. Full suite 1829 Jest passed / 1 skipped / 160 suites, 120 PHPUnit, E2E 14/14 on all three fixtures.

**GH-386** Both sample pickers in `assets/soil-nutrition-analysis.js` — the Analysis page's zone selector and the reusable `mountSampleDropdown()` that Plan → Nutrition uses to choose which soil sample the Nutrition Program is generated for — stored the chosen sample's id in `localStorage` and then compared it back with `samples[j].id === persistedId`. `/api/samples` returns `id` as a JSON number and everything read out of `localStorage` is a string, so `123 === '123'` was false, the match never succeeded, and the picker silently reopened on whatever the list order put first. User-visible consequence: on a multi-sample site the Plan page opens on a sample the user did not choose, and Generate there computes and persists a nutrition programme against it with nothing on screen saying so. Found while adding GH-384's MLSN/SLAN E2E fixtures, where the Plan page sat on Burns' "Green 2" while the export used "12th Fairway". The canonical identifier was chosen deliberately rather than by whichever value was nearest: `samples.id`, the database primary key, compared as a string on both sides — it is what `/api/samples` returns, what `/api/samples/{id}/analyse` is addressed by, and it exists for every sample. `client_uid` was rejected because it is nullable (several tissue samples in the dev data have none), client-supplied rather than allocated, and not unique across sites; the Word export picker's own `data-sample-uid` scheme (`<siteId>::<client_uid | label | sample_<id>>`, built by `sample-persistence.js` for the offline store) answers a different question and is deliberately untouched — conflating the two would be worse than the bug. Both loops now route through one `_snSampleIdMatches()` helper, the write side stringifies explicitly at `_snSaveActiveId()` and at `nutrition-calendar.js`'s `onSelect`, and a missing id on either side is never a match, so "nothing remembered" cannot resolve to sample 0. Existing stored values are already the id stringified, so nothing needs migrating — a remembered selection starts working on the next page load. New `tests/gh386-sample-picker-remembers-selection.test.js` (9 tests) including the numeric-prefix trap (14 and 1411 must not match 141); red-checked twice (restore the strict comparison: `Expected true / Received false` and the list lookup returns −1 instead of 1; let one picker keep its own comparison: the "no raw `=== persistedId` survives" guard fails). The helper is attached after the module's export line rather than inside the object literal because eleven existing test files load this module by string-splicing on that exact line. **Live-verified on Burns, a seven-sample site, the same run before and after**: pick "Rye Nursery" (sample 53, soil K 7.6) → before the fix a reload came back on "12th Fairway" with K 195 and P 18, the list's first entry, while `localStorage` still held "53"; after the fix the reload comes back on "Rye Nursery" with K 7.6. The E2E harness still drives the picker through the UI to pin its fixture sample — that is what a user does, and with nothing remembered the default is list order regardless. Full suite 1838 Jest passed / 1 skipped / 161 suites, 120 PHPUnit, E2E 14/14 on all three fixtures.

**GH-387** The Plan page and the Combined Word export handed their product recommender different SURFACE TYPES, so the same sample got different products out of the same catalogue. Reported after GH-384 as a regional-catalogue divergence in the GH-362/D30 family; establishing the root cause first showed that reading was wrong — every product involved (Sportsmaster WSF, Ammonium Sulphate Tech, MAP Tech on one side; MESA 30, Greenmaster Liquid Spring & Summer, Greenmaster Liquid High N, Wilbur Ellis GroMaxx 46, FoliMAX N-Hancer-N on the other) lives in the SAME Australian catalogue, and both pages resolved `isAustralia() === true`. Hooking the AU recommender on both surfaces for Burns "12th Fairway" gave identical monthly nutrient series (N 120.1, P 14.0, K 0.0 across 12 months), identical methodology and identical distributor filter, with exactly one input differing: `surfaceType` was `golf_greens` on the Plan and `golf` in the export. Root cause is GH-383's own rewiring of the Combined export's per-sample calendar inputs, which assigned `_siteInputs.turfType` into `perSampleInputs.surfaceType`. Those are three different things: `turfType` is golf/sports/lawns, `surfaceType` is the surface the calendar works in (greens, soccer, …), and the recommenders key on a canonical third form (golf_greens, tees, fairways, bowling_greens, cricket_wickets). The same defect was live in a quieter form on Test5 - NZ — export `sports` against the Plan's `soccer` — where it changed no products because the Prebble recommender's selection is insensitive to that value at those figures, which is precisely why it stayed invisible while the harness was green. Fixed in the shared input adapter, which now resolves both values once for both surfaces: `surfaceType` follows nutrition-calendar.js's own long-standing chain (a per-sample soil reading, then the site's sub-category, then its 'sports' default, including the cotula_bowling_green special case) and `recommenderSurfaceType` is the canonical key mapped from turfType + subCategory. `nutrition-au-fertiliser-integration.js`'s own `_mapTurfType` now delegates to that mapping, keeping its literal copy only as the fallback for a page that has not loaded the adapter, so one implementation answers for both pages.
