New `tests/gh387-surface-type-parity.test.js` (10 tests) pinning all three vocabularies, every rung of the mapping and the provenance stamp; red-checked three ways (restore the turfType assignment: both structural rows fail; break the golf-greens rung: `Expected "golf_greens" / Received "greens"`; stop delegating: the one-copy assertion fails). **Live-verified on Burns "12th Fairway", both surfaces, same run**: both now resolve `surfaceType: 'golf_greens'` and choose the identical three products — Sportsmaster WSF 20-0-0 + Seaweed, Ammonium Sulphate Tech (soluble), MAP Tech — and the rendered document's Annual Product Summary lists exactly those three. Test5 - NZ now resolves `soccer` on both surfaces (was `sports` in the export) with its product set unchanged: MESA Country Club 100%, Ammos 22 (Nitro 22), Pro Balance. The E2E harness's product-programme rows are now asserted for every fixture — GH-384's `productParity.asserted: false` opt-out is deleted along with the defect that justified it — which needed five harness corrections the AU fixtures exposed: an AU catalogue for assertion 14 (it previously checked Australian products against the NZ catalogue after matching "Hill Labs" in the text), a product roll-up from the monthly series so the Plan's enriched integration object and the export's raw recommender return are compared like with like, a header-indexed Annual Product Summary footer parse (the AU panel's header is Product | Applications | Total Rate | N | P | K, so the harness had been reading the application count as delivered N — 12 instead of 120), a name guard for AU annual-summary entries that key by product id, and a K Reconciliation section that is legitimately absent when the programme delivers no K at all. **Two findings recorded, neither in scope:** the K Reconciliation section is suppressed on Burns because the correct greens products carry no K and `_perSampleKDelivered()` returns null at zero — correct behaviour, but the client sees no K discussion at all on a site whose soil K is 195 ppm against a 55.5 ppm ceiling; and the AU Annual Product Summary's per-product N figures do not partition its own delivered total (113 + 7 + 5 = 125 against a printed 120, MAP Tech's 5.4 kg N appearing as a row but not in the total), identically on BOTH surfaces, so it is an AU-renderer inconsistency and not a parity defect — the harness's row-sum rows are therefore asserted for the NZ renderer only, with the reason written at the assertion. Full suite 1848 Jest passed / 1 skipped / 162 suites, 120 PHPUnit, E2E 14/14 on all three fixtures with product parity now asserted.

**GH-388** Three leftovers from the D31 cutover, all the same shape — a second copy, or a rule delivered to one surface only. **(1) Dead duplicated arithmetic.** GH-384 routed `computeProgram()` through the shared core and the shared range resolver, which left `NutritionCalendar.calculateDeficit()`, `.getThresholds()`, `CONFIG.mlsnThresholds` / `.slanThresholds` / `.aaThresholds` and the whole `_collectAATexture()` / `inputs.aaTextureKey` plumbing unreachable: a second implementation of exactly the arithmetic and exactly the threshold tables D31 exists to de-duplicate, sitting inside the file whose own changelog claims the duplication is gone. Verified before removing — no caller anywhere in `assets/`, `app/` or `tests/`; the only references were prose in three test headers and one source-text assertion, now re-pointed at the core's equivalent. The behaviour they carried is unchanged one level down: the null-not-zero guard is the core's `typeof currentLevel !== 'number'` branch, the ppm→kg/ha conversion is its `ppmToKgHaFactor`, and the AA sands/others bucketing is the shared resolver's `aaTextureKey()` off the one texture chain. `CONFIG.clippingManagement`, `CONFIG.trafficModifiers` and `CONFIG.yearsToCorrect` are genuinely still used and stay. **(2) Decision D-4b, half delivered.** GH-383 printed the "annual N target came from Site Settings" note in the Combined export only; the single-sample export renders its own Annual Nutrient Requirements section and printed nothing, so a site with no generated programme received an unannotated document even though `annualNSource` was already on `engineInputs.turf`. Same wording on both paths now. **(3) The single export swallowed the adapter's fail-loud.** `resolveSiteProgramInputs()` throws when a site config cannot be resolved, deliberately, so that no surface computes against another site's configuration; `word-export.js` caught it, warned to the console and carried on with `_userN = null` — the engine then substituted `REMOVAL_RATES`' per-species N, `annualNSource` was null so not even the note could fire, and the client got a document full of confident figures scaled against a number nobody chose. It now nulls `engineInputs` and returns, which routes into b35fix329's established "no silent fallback" drop rather than inventing a second suppression path, and warns naming the site — the same shape `word-export-combined.js` already used. New `tests/gh388-calendar-dead-arithmetic-and-single-export.test.js` (12 tests), including a runtime check that the removed members are no longer exposed on the module and that the constants they held still exist once, in the core. Red-checked three ways: reintroduce a private threshold table and `getThresholds()` (3 fail, including `Received: [Function anonymous]` from the runtime check); suppress the single export's note with `if (false && …)` (the gate assertion fails — it matches the whole condition precisely so that leaving the note's text in place cannot satisfy it); restore the swallowed catch (2 fail). **Live-verified**: with a site's persisted programme temporarily removed, the export prints "Note — annual nitrogen target source: Burns (Site Settings → Turf). No nutrition programme has been generated on the Plan page for this site, so the annual N target every requirement below is scaled against was taken from the site configuration rather than from a programme. Generate the programme on the Plan page to base these figures on your own target." above the Annual Nutrient Requirements table, with the adapter resolving `annualN: 150` from `turf.nProgram`; the site's config was restored afterwards and verified byte-identical by MD5. The single-export note itself could not be exercised through the UI: every export from the new hub routes through the Combined pipeline regardless of how many samples are ticked (REVIEW-GH349-onward.md open question 7), so that branch is reachable only from the old hub — it is covered by test and by inspection, and that limit is stated rather than glossed. Full suite 1860 Jest passed / 1 skipped / 163 suites, 120 PHPUnit, E2E 14/14 on all three fixtures.

**GH-389** The E2E parity harness gained the multi-site row the D31 plan's section 10.3 called for. Per-site annual N in a multi-site Combined export was GH-383's headline fix — before it, `word-export.js` read the target from `.gaip-nutrition-annual-n`, a hidden legacy input that `nutrition-calendar.js`'s `restoreFromPersisted()` fills with the first restored site's `target_n` and then refuses to refill, so every later site in the document inherited it — and it was protected only by one manual probe. The harness now ticks a second sample from a different site and asserts that each sample's ANR "N kg/ha" equals THAT site's own persisted `meta.annualNBase`, read from the page's per-site config store, plus that the two figures differ (equal targets would make a leak indistinguishable from correct behaviour) and that every engine call resolved its N from a generated programme rather than the Settings fallback. The fixture names the second sample, so the pairing is data rather than a hard-coded site: Test5 - NZ (target 250) with Burns "12th Fairway" (target 120). Done as a SEPARATE second export rather than a second tick in the existing one, deliberately — the Combined document's Annual Product Summary aggregates every ticked sample, so a two-sample run would silently change what every other comparison in the file means; only the ANR table and the engine calls are read from it. Red-checked by reintroducing the original defect (the pre-GH-383 hidden-input read) in `_buildEngineInputs`: the new row fails by name with Burns' 12th Fairway printing **250** — Test5's target — against its own 120, `"diff": -130`. Fixtures without a second site assert that they have none rather than passing silently. Harness now 15 tests; 15/15 on all three fixtures. Full suite 1860 Jest passed / 1 skipped / 163 suites, 120 PHPUnit unchanged.

**Engineering backlog (internal, not a client comment)**

- **Check whether SLAN's `targetPpm` gets misread as a ceiling in `renderAnnualRequirements()`.** Found 2026-08-21 while explaining the "Annual Nutrient Requirements" panel's methodology differences to the user, unrelated to the AA fixes in this session. `mlsnEngine()`'s SLAN branch (`hub-tissue-v3.js:2765-2770`, explicitly "unchanged" and untouched by all of GH-258-277) sets `targetPpm: range.lo` — the **floor** of the SLAN range, by design (comment: "For SLAN, target is the low end of range"). But `renderAnnualRequirements()` (`soil-nutrition-analysis.js:891-896`) reads `nObj.targetPpm` for every non-AA methodology and uses it as if it were a **ceiling**: `isHigh = actual >= targetV`, zeroing the displayed kg/ha requirement whenever `actual >= targetV`. For SLAN this means `isHigh` can trigger the moment a nutrient merely reaches the *floor* of its sufficiency range (i.e. as soon as it's SUFFICIENT, not just when it's genuinely HIGH) — the Annual Requirement would show 0 far too early for SLAN sites, unlike MLSN (whose generic PACE-style `targetPpm = mlsnThreshold + uptakePpm` or `×1.5`, `hub-tissue-v3.js:2836-2839`, is a genuine ceiling-ish value, not a floor) and unlike AA (which correctly uses `rangeMax`, GH-260). Not yet confirmed live on a real SLAN site — found by reading the three branches side by side while answering an unrelated question, not from a bug report. Before fixing: reproduce on a real SLAN site/sample (SUFFICIENT-but-not-HIGH nutrient) to confirm the panel actually shows 0 kg/ha incorrectly; if confirmed, the fix is presumably to give `renderAnnualRequirements()` a SLAN-specific ceiling source (SLAN's own `range.hi`, analogous to AA's `rangeMax`) instead of reusing the floor-shaped `targetPpm` field, mirroring the `isAA` branch's shape.

- **Remove `methodology_snapshot` (`samples`/`site_summaries` columns) entirely.** Investigated during D07/GH-265: this field is computed at sample-creation time (`site.methodology_override ?: account.methodology`, `SampleController.php:373`) and returned in several API payloads, but as of GH-265, nothing in the live codebase actually reads it to make a classification decision — both the JS side (`ammonium-acetate-methodology.js`, region-based auto-select) and the PHP side (`Controller::effectiveMethodology()`, same NZ-bounds check) independently recompute the effective methodology live from site coordinates every time, ignoring this stored value. GH-263 was the only place that ever tried to use it for a live decision, and that turned out to be wrong (GH-265): the snapshot goes stale the instant a site's methodology changes after a sample is saved, while the live geography-based computation doesn't. Contrast with `soil_texture_snapshot`, which stays — `SampleAnalysisController.php:52` genuinely depends on it (no live geography-based fallback exists for rootzone texture). Before removing: grep for `methodology_snapshot`/`methodologySnapshot` across `app/` and `assets/` to confirm no other consumer appeared since this note was written, check `Sample`/`SiteSummary` model fillable lists and the initial-schema migration, and decide whether a DB migration to drop the columns is worth it or whether leaving the (now definitely write-only, never-read) columns in place is lower-risk — this is cleanup, not a bug fix, so no rush.

Phytotoxicity добавляет новый расчёт в оркестратор, но пока им никто кроме задач не пользуется. 

Heat/Frost/Drought — единственное, что трогает уже используемый другими частями объект, но только путём добавления новых полей, не изменения существующих.

оба только в tasks block. Но с нюансом:

Phytotoxicity — чисто изолированно. Новое поле computed.phytotoxicity, которое сегодня никто не читает и после подключения будет читать только очередь задач. Ничего общего с другими карточками/расчётами.

Heat/Frost/Drought — эффект тоже только в tasks block, но реализация технически трогает общий объект computed.stress — тот же самый, который читает disease-stress-climate-coupling.js для корректировки риска болезней, и откуда берутся combinedGrowthModifier/environmentalStressIndex (влияют на Growth Potential и другие карточки). Я проверяла: coupling-движок реагирует только на конкретные типы (drought/shade/waterlogging/temperature), не на heat/frost — значит, если только добавлять новые поля и не трогать существующие расчёты, побочных эффектов не будет.

То есть по замыслу — да, оба только tasks block.
